# Campaign verification checklist — living data file

**Fixes shipped from this scoring, same day (2026-08-15), all NOT YET TESTED:** **Fix 83** front-local posture hysteresis (`LOCAL_RATIO_HOLD = 1.1`) → **R51**; **Fix 84** the C.10 pre-build escape moved above the idle-civ throttle + `r48_c10_*` dispatch counters → R48 recut; **Fix 85** theatre air bases prefer ground-committed theatres (cap 8, band 350 "air-front") → **R52**; **Fix 86** convoy-danger decay 5→4, threat per sunk convoy 50→100 and `CONVOY_DANGER_FOR_MAX_IMPORTANCE` 400→50 → R36 Fix-86 leg (the 2026-08-16 local probe ran at decay 2 / threat 50). `wa_tlm_version` → **16**. **All four owe an F9 boot test before the next cloud campaign** (three scripted-effect files, one on_action, one event, one defines file touched).

**As of:** 2026-08-15 · last campaign scored: cloud `af003548` (BHU observer, **124** monthly saves 1936.2–1946.5, unbranched — sessions 21→4384 monotone, HOI4 1.19.2, difficulty normal, `dlcs=257535`). Build = **`1ff21fb22`** (repo HEAD): `wa_tlm_version = 15` in the **first** save, and the first save was written **2m42s after the HEAD commit** (HEAD 12:12:47, first save 12:15:29 — the same pattern as every prior cloud run). **Fixes 76–82 (proactive synth rubber 76, PC fairness 77/78, La-5FN gate 79, USA synth reach 80, refinery caps 81, Narvik 82) and WA_TLM v15 are therefore all IN.** GER is annihilated between 1946.3 and 1946.4 — the last live GER save is `1946.3_Mar.hoi4`; anything read off GER after that is a dead-tag fossil (see the R19 dead-tag caveat).

**Headline: 22 PASSED / 18 FAILED / 2 NOT CHECKED across F1–F9 and R6–R50.** The war arc holds for the second campaign running — F1–F5 and F7 all pass, F5 ~10 months late (`fall_of_berlin_sov` 1946.3.13 → `ww2_has_ended` 1946.3.31, `hitler_has_died` never set — the inverse dropout of `bec4d829`) — **R34 reaches 5/5 and is due for retirement**, four fresh fixes verify on their first test (R19's Fix 77, R31's Fix 79, R49's Fix 81, R50's Fix 82), R7b/R31/R19 flip from FAILED to PASSED, and the actionable output is (a) a western front that lands on time and then freezes for 18 months with the binding term now named, and (b) a proactive rubber lane (Fixes 76/80) that never armed on a build that carries it.

**1. Priority construction is HEALTHY, and the `be18f9c7` rail collapse is gone.** 1 660 buildings delivered by six majors (GER 491, SOV 401, ENG 278, JAP 229, USA 186, ITA 75; 1 199 of them railways); across GER/ENG/USA/SOV **1 087 completions against 20 sweeps + 9 stale + 12 peace + 0 orphan + 0 lost = 96.4% completions**; type-13 depth capped at 12 everywhere (the 80→24→2 sawtooth does not recur). Contention: ENG runs PC at **134% of its hard cap** at 1943.6 (137 vs 102), ENG/GER `pc_civs_avail = 0` from 1945.6 (PC eats the whole pool). ITA queue empty 1936–1940.6, ITA completion 58%, `stale_n` 31.

**2. Railway latency:** type-13 median age 1–2 months, never >2; naval bases 10–18 months (cost 10 000 vs 800 — arithmetic, not a defect); ENG cancelled+restarted the Hong Kong (87%→1%) and Alexandria (52%→3%) naval bases. USA built **12 railways in ten years** and held zero rail/naval projects at every Med/Normandy date.

**3. D-Day lands on time and then rolls back — and the mechanism is now named, and it is not the Fix 55 cliff.** D-Day 1944.6.7, Paris 1944.6.22, peak 1944.8 = 6/24 French states with 121 Allied vs 95 GER divisions; 1944.9 = **2 states** (15+1016, the beaches), 82 vs 142 GER; pinned there 20 of the next 22 months; France 24/24 only at 1946.4 by GER collapse. Mechanism of the rollback: ENG+USA `posture_vs_ger` 1 (1944.7) → **0 (1944.8, 1944.9)** → 1 (1944.10/11) — orders retracted with `fielded_eq_ratio` ≥ 0.930 (above both equipment gates) and the global air veto not firing (GER 14 109 × 0.4 = 5 644 < ENG ~8 900); the binding term is the level-1 **front-local** branch `_post_local_friendly > 1.5 × _post_local_enemy` (`WA_AI_MILITARY_posture_effects.txt:456`, `@WA_AI_POSTURE_LOCAL_RATIO = 1.5`, **no hysteresis**) — 121/95 = 1.27 fails it in the month GER reinforces 26→95→142 divisions (its Eastern front already purged: GER-held SOV states 5→0 by 1944.12, GER army flat 285–291). Air over region 239: GER 5 174 vs Allied 4 250 (1.22:1) at 1944.8, 1.25:1 at 1944.11, 1.53:1 at 1945.3 — ENG flew 3 924 of the 4 250, USA **326**; USA 92–94% no-mission, **zero** USAAF planes based in France at any sampled date; UK capacity 10 700 vs 14 184 faction planes. Then an 18-month freeze (1944.9→1946.3) at level-1 posture, 71–115 Allied vs 57–97 Axis divisions, two state flips.

**4. Battleplans:** SOV level-1 vs GER every month 1942.6→1946.5, never grind; SOV front flat at 142–144 states 1942.6→1944.6 (24 months at 1.38–1.49×) and +6 states 1945.1→1945.9 at 1.53–1.66×; GER `posture_vs_*` all 0 from 1945.5 to death with eq 0.995 and 286 divisions (unexplained; GER `post_last_t` = 1945.12 while `comp_last_t` = 1946.3); xr<25 share 79–92% on all majors, grind 0/0/9/34/35/39 observations — dead code for the 6th campaign; `war_relation` casualties fold in air/naval losses (USA 414k vs GER at 1943.6 with 0 divisions in Europe) — do not read them as land exchange.

**5. Atlantic: lost 1942–43, contained by shipbuilding 1944–46, never won.** GER lifetime convoy kills **11 413** (worse than `f9321934`'s 10 802, ~3× `be18f9c7`'s 3 960); ENG `nav_conv_threat` pinned at 100 for all 72 war months; ENG free-convoy pool 0 at 1943.4/1943.9/1943.10 and 40 at 1942.9, recovering to 3 186; Allied U-boat kills ≈2 801 (ENG 1 997, USA 804, **CAN 0**) ≈ 4.1 convoys per boat; GER subs 102→148→110, never suppressed. See R36.

**6. USA rubber:** deficit permanent from 1942.6 but a 4.3× better end-state (EFFECTIVE −142 vs −614; park 74 levels, produced 1 543); **the Fix 76/80 proactive lane never armed** — `wa_tlm_r48_prebuild_synth_n = 0` with `_first_t = _last_t = 0` on USA/ENG/GER/JAP/SOV/ITA on a v15 build, while USA's `WA_AI_construction_timer` flag was ABSENT at 1941.9/1942.1 with rubber +26.7/+10.0 and imports 106.7/92.0, i.e. the C.10 gate was open on the trigger's own terms. Suspects, in order, are in R48.

**7. New save-reading gotchas** (recorded in the anomalies list below): a decoy `countries={` at depth 3 precedes the real one; naval-invasion orders live in the top-level `theatres` block, not under `countries/TAG`; a province omits `controller=` when it equals the state controller; a production line omits `active_factories=` when it is 0; `savegame.py tlm` mis-renders `wa_tlm_pc_built_by_type^N` as a ring series — read it with `var`.

**8. Other:** the Italy arc is entirely unflagged (ITA 27→7 states 1943.9→1943.12, turns democratic ~1943.10–11, never capitulates, Sicily recaptured and held to 1946.5); ENG `persistent_strategy` type 2/3/6 id=8 value=-200 triple at 1945.1 and 1945.6; ENG import blackout at 1946.5 (all 0.0), alu park 0 active / 14 inactive; `USA_provide_synthetic_refineries_SOV` fired 19× (SOV synth 25→60 is lend-lease, not construction); CAN 0 U-boat kills; JAP `naval_submarine` role at exactly 1 factory for 100 months.

**LIVE DEFECT, opened 2026-08-14 while correcting this file against campaign `be18f9c7` (build `d683fb022`) — the PC allocation override's two writers have DIVERGED, which is the exact hazard the code comments against.** `WA_AI_PC_override_max_factories_factor` (flag + country variable) is written by two independent systems: the UK-air branch at `common/scripted_effects/WA_AI_CONSTRUCTION_PRIORITY_strategies.txt:1293` and the railway queue at `common/scripted_effects/WA_AI_CONSTRUCTION_PRIORITY_railway_core.txt:206`; the single reader is `WA_AI_CONSTRUCTION_PRIORITY_core.txt:335-337`, which multiplies ENG's available factories by whatever the last writer left. The comment block at `strategies.txt:1280-1285` states the contract explicitly — *"The value here is deliberately identical to railway's 0.5: with matching values the two writers are idempotent … Do not diverge the numbers without giving each system its own flag, or they will alternate on a last-writer-wins timer."* Commit **`2937070f7`** raised the air writer 0.5 → **0.6** (alongside `@AI_PC_ALLOC_FRACTION` 0.35 → 0.40) **without touching the railway writer and without updating that comment**; the commit message does not mention the change at all, so it reads as a drive-by. The invariant is broken today: ENG's monthly PC ceiling is 0.6 or 0.5 depending on which system pulsed last, and every other AI running a railway queue is unaffected only because it never reaches the air writer. **`be18f9c7` reads 0.6 on ENG at every sample, so the air writer is currently winning and the practical impact is small — the contract, not the outcome, is what is broken.** Recommended resolution, in order: (1) **give each system its own flag and variable**, which is what the comment itself prescribes and the only fix that removes the race rather than hiding it; (2) failing that, keep **0.6** as the value that wins — it is what the current campaign actually ran and it is the number tuned against `@AI_PC_ALLOC_FRACTION = 0.40` — and raise `railway_core.txt:206` to match, **but only after an impact pass on non-ENG railway builders**, for whom that is a real allocation change; (3) do **not** silently drop the air writer back to 0.5 without establishing what the 0.6 bump bought, since that number was never justified in writing. Whoever fixes this must also correct the now-false "deliberately identical to railway's 0.5" sentence in the comment. Scored by **R8 probe 4**, whose expected value and interpretation were corrected in the same session.

**Front/posture stack — standing design decision (2026-08-13):** **Stage 2 is deliberately NOT done.** Collapsing the three faction pairs into one Default-layer pair was considered and rejected for now: `front_control` is "Exclusive per area, last-set wins per mode, Country > Faction > Default", so moving the exec blocks to Default would put them in the same layer as the emergency stops (`EXEC_no_stockpiles_stop` priority 10000, `EXEC_low_equipment_hold` priority 500) and make resolution file-order dependent. Resolve that explicitly before attempting it.

**Equipment generalisation — SUSPENDED (2026-08-13, still current).** Settled negatively and not to be revisited: the design layer (`ai_equipment` priority — Fixes 46/48/50), the production-line layer (`production_upgrade_desire_offset` — Fix 51; vanilla's own comment says it offsets the desire to *upgrade an existing line*, so `+100` on an older chassis is a no-op and line *creation* never sees it), engine obsolescence (not an archetype rule, not a production gate), and research weighting (`research_weight_factor` — Fix 57). The only lever that binds is the hard research gate `allow = { NOT = { is_ai = yes } }` (Fix 69, R40). `equipment_variant_production_factor` cannot discriminate two chassis of one archetype. Actionable without another campaign: the `ai_equipment` **coverage gap** (`tools/equipment_evaluator/output/coverage_gaps.md`, ENG's four post-Cromwell mediums the worst case) — close it by *authoring the missing designs*, not by blanket `-100`s. Fix 39 is EXONERATED of the ENG aluminium-latch accusation (see R32; the reading used `resource@X` as the net column). Still open and unowned: **`fall_of_berlin_sov` never fires on the scripted-defeat path**.

Protocol for scoring, retiring, and adding items: see `../SKILL.md`. Streak = consecutive PASSED; FAILED resets it to 0; `N/A (DLC)` / `NOT CHECKED` leave it untouched.

**Tooling note (2026-08-11, no campaign scored):** `savegame.py` gained four commands — `army`, `resources`, `buildings`, `decisions` — that replace the hand-rolled scans F8/R5/R10/R20/R25/R29 used to describe. The probe lines below were rewired to them. Two process changes shipped with it: `../SKILL.md` step 2 now requires `Probe:` lines to be quoted **verbatim** into extraction prompts (the paraphrase that dropped R10's `units` scoping contaminated F8/R5/R10 in one scoring), and `documentation/WA_TLM_TELEMETRY_SYSTEM.md` §2.1 is now the semantics registry for the legacy `*_dbg_*` counters.

**Save-directory hazard (2026-08-11):** the cloud runs reuse the same filename scheme (`YYYY.M_Mon.hoi4`), so `911bed3c` **overwrote 123 of `31eaf7e6`'s files in place**. Only `1946.5_May.hoi4` and `1946.6_Jun.hoi4` still belong to `31eaf7e6`, and their in-game dates are *later* than `911bed3c`'s final save — globbing by filename/date silently splices two campaigns into a fake 125-save run. Always group by `game_unique_id`.

### F2. France falls on time

If the SCW still deadlocks WITH S1 confirmed working, escalate to the shelved options (N1/N2/N4 balance nudges, B1/B3 scripted resolution backstops — see the 2026-08-10 SCW investigation).

Resolve every id through the top-level `equipments={}` registry and select on `archetype = infantry_equipment`; keep `heavy_infantry_equipment` broken out separately (it is a distinct archetype, and merging it is what made the two passes look reconcilable). ## RETIREABLE — fix verifications

Delete an item when its streak reaches its threshold (3 = narrow probe, 5 = behavioural). **Retirements and merges are recorded in the ledger at the end of this file** (`## Retired and merged items — ledger`), together with the instrumentation dispositions that are still owed. Any R-number cited in an item but absent as a `###` heading is there.

**Causal chains (2026-08-11 — each chain's behavioural OUTCOME is scored ONCE by its owner and cited by the others):** **USAAF-Europe** → outcome owned by **R15** (≥25% overseas + UK basing pre-D-Day; the funding/construction/allocation links R23, R16, R26 are retired, R8's capacity legs now sit in R54). **ENG-economy** → outcome owned by **R24 criterion 2** (R23/R25/R26 retired). **Mediterranean** — the "R9 blocks R13/R17" dependency is **retired** (`911bed3c` falsified it: A* still dead, yet the Normandy beachhead worked), so R13 and R17 are diagnosed on their own merits. **Eastern-front** → outcome owned by **F5** (R27 retired at 5/5, its rule in `documentation/WA_AI_MILITARY_SYSTEM.md` §9; R1 retired at 7/5, its age cycle is R39 leg 3).


### R6. Majors mechanize

- **Fix under test:** `5d2663848` + Tier 1 composition work.

Pass: GER **and** SOV ≥ 18 at some sampled quarter of 1942+. Absence contract: no `wa_tlm_*` namespace = pre-TLM build, probe void. **First-campaign sanity cross-check before scoring:** `wa_tlm_comp_armor` = 0 on a country with known fielded tanks means the `has_army_size` type token silently failed — mark NOT CHECKED and fix the ladder, do not score. *(Probe gap history 2026-08-10: no `wa_ai_template*` variable ever reached saves and divisions reference templates by numeric id only — composition was unrecoverable from a save; this instrumentation is the fix.)*
- **Threshold:** 5 (behavioural).
- **Streak:** 0
- **History:**

### R7b. Lend-lease relief — infantry-equipment leg (recalibrated)

- **Fix under test:** `2d48a1a17` (35k/6k surplus, 4k support starving, common-enemy pairs).
- **Pass:** ROM/HUN rifle stockpiles off zero while GER holds surplus.
- **Probe:** recipient `production` → `equipments` infantry-equipment totals + `creator="GER"` share (same registry-resolution and ledger caveat as R7a).
- **Threshold:** 5 (behavioural).
- **Streak:** 1
- **History:**
  - 2026-08-10 · `66d6b53c` · PASSED — the recalibrated gate demonstrably fired: HUN GER-created rifles 10 → 6 975 between 1943.6 and 1943.9 with GER at 41 764 (over the new 34 999 gate, under 49 999 → base 6 000/pull — a delivery the old 60k gate would never have allowed). Caveat: the leg opens only in brief donor-surplus windows (GER above gate at 2/8 samples; in 1944 donor and recipients starve together and the leg is correctly gated off).
  - 2026-08-11 · `31eaf7e6` · NOT CHECKED — the mechanism never ran: GER cleared the >34 999 donor gate at exactly one sample (1943.9, 36 062 — of which only 17.3k GER-created; the gate opened on captured war booty) while neither recipient was starving (<10k) on that date; on the dates ROM/HUN do starve (1944.6, 1945.3) GER sits below the gate. Zero GER-created rifles delivered all campaign. Finding for a follow-up: the donor/recipient gate windows never overlap for GER, and `has_equipment` reads a stockpile inflated by captures.
  - 2026-08-11 · `911bed3c` · **FAILED — the never-overlapping-windows failure is now confirmed structural, not campaign variance.** Donor gate (>34 999) and recipient-starving bar (<10 000) coincide at ZERO of four anchors. Two near-misses bracket the problem precisely: at 1943.6 GER holds **34 616 — 383 rifles short** of the gate while ROM/HUN sit at 13.2k/11.4k; at 1945.6 GER holds **102 499** (3× the gate) while both recipients sit just *over* the starving bar at 12.2k/12.6k. Upgrade from last campaign: not literally zero — HUN gained **2 407 GER-created rifles** between 1942.6 and 1943.6, so one delivery burst did fire. ROM never got more than 38. **Two calibration findings for the fix:** (a) the absolute donor gate and absolute starving bar are tuned so tightly that a 383-unit miss kills a year of relief — make one or both relative to army size / recipient deficit; (b) the same surplus is R5's 116.8k hoard, so this is one defect with two symptoms, not two.
  - 2026-08-12 · `9a4cd657` · **FAILED — and the diagnosis has genuinely moved: the windows DID overlap once, and still nothing shipped.** A full 122-month sweep finds GER clearing the >34 999 donor gate in **14 months** (1940.7–1940.9 and 1945.1–1945.11). The only month where that coincides with a starving, at-war, non-capitulated ally is **1945.1 — GER 39 421 rifles vs HUN 4 137** (ROM/BUL/ITA all 12.3–14.8k, above the 10 000 bar). HUN then rose 4 137→18 282 by 1945.2, but its GER-created rifle stock went **0→4** — the gain was its own production, so no relief transfer fired in the one valid window (minimum tier is 6 000). Campaign-wide maxima of GER-created rifles in recipients: ITA 596, ROM 375, HUN 7, BUL 1 — **zero relief-scale deliveries in 122 months**. The 1940.7–1940.9 overlaps are correctly refused (ROM/BUL at 0 rifles, but neither at war). GER's own peak is **101 033 at 1945.6**, so donor wealth is again not the binding constraint.
  - **Next step is instrumentation, not another threshold re-tune (2026-08-12).** R7a fires on the *same donor/recipient pairs in the same weeks*, so the shared pair test is sound and the infantry branch is failing on something specific to it. Prime suspect inside `WA_AI_LEND_LEASE_request_surplus_relief`: `random_other_country` picks **one** donor per recipient per week with the `limit` evaluated on the candidate, so a recipient can repeatedly draw a non-qualifying country and silently do nothing. Add a counter on *donor selected* and *infantry branch entered* before the next fix attempt — three campaigns have now been spent inferring this from stockpile deltas.
  - 2026-08-12 · `a8b29e09` · **PASSED — reverses three consecutive FAILEDs, with an exact tier signature.** Relief-scale GER-created rifles are demonstrably delivered, and GER's vanilla lend-lease ledger is all zeros, so these are `send_equipment` transfers as designed: **ITA 1943.3 holds a single `ger_inf_4` entry of exactly 6 000** (the base `send_equipment` tier) after sitting at 9 403 the month before with GER crossing the 34 999 gate mid-month; **HUN 1942.12 `ger_inf_3` = 8 723** (HUN 8 370 at 1942.11, GER 33 904 and rising); **BUL 1945.1 `ger_inf_4` = 7 406** (BUL 10 448 at 1944.12, GER 43 948). Campaign-wide maxima of GER-created rifles: HUN **8 723**, BUL **7 406**, ITA **6 000**, ROM 2 491 — against 7 / 1 / 596 / 375 last campaign. GER clears the >34 999 donor gate in **11 of 126 months** (1940.8–1940.9, 1944.5, 1944.12, 1945.1–1945.7; peak 95 805 at 1945.6). **The unreliability is still real and still worth instrumenting:** two snapshot-level overlaps produced nothing (1944.5 GER 38 302 vs HUN 9 263; 1945.6 GER 95 805 vs ROM 9 933), while three deliveries landed in months that did not read as overlaps — GER's own stock swings violently between weekly pulses (support 29 995 → 7 035 → 29 333 across 1943.7–1943.9), so monthly snapshots straddle the pulse. This is consistent with the `random_other_country` one-donor-per-week lottery hypothesis; **the donor-selected / infantry-branch-entered counter is still the right next step even with the item passing.** ROM is the weak recipient on both legs and never received a relief-scale drop before the collapse. **Cross-reference correction: the two legs are NOT disjoint in this campaign** — every infantry delivery lands in a month where the support drip is simultaneously live on the same pair, contradicting the `9a4cd657` finding.
  - 2026-08-13 · `02bd4445` · **PASSED — the best reading of any campaign.** Relief-scale GER-created rifles land in **ROM 16,903 (1945.4)**, **HUN 15,701 (1945.6)**, **BUL 19,971 (1944.9)** and ITA 7,142 (1940.10) — against prior maxima of 8,723 / 7,406 / 2,491. GER clears the >34,999 donor gate in **23 of 92 monthly samples** (1942.8, 1942.11, then continuously 1944.5→1946.1; peak **135,006** at 1944.10), and ROM/HUN stockpiles are non-zero at every one of those months. The window-overlap problem that dominated three FAILEDs is gone at this donor wealth.
  - 2026-08-14 · `be18f9c7` · FAILED — Campaign-wide maxima of **GER-created** rifles in a recipient stockpile over 14 monthly anchors 1942.6->1945.6: **ROM 292, HUN 14, BUL 10, ITA 1 655** - nowhere near the 6 000 base `send_equipment` tier, against ROM 16 903 / HUN 15 701 / BUL 19 971 on `02bd4445`. GER clears the >34 999 donor gate at only **2 of 14 sampled months** (1945.3 = 40 251, 1945.4 = 44 338), and on both ROM (18.5-19.0k) and HUN (12.6-13.5k) sit far above the 10 000 starving bar, so the leg is correctly gated shut; every month a recipient actually starves (ROM 0 at 1943.3 and 1944.9, HUN 0 at 1942.12/1943.6/1944.9/1944.12) has GER at 6.9k-23.5k, below the gate. **The never-overlapping-windows failure of `911bed3c`/`9a4cd657` is back.** Aggravating and worth carrying: GER's donor "surplus" is captured booty, not producible stock - GER-created rifles are **0** at 1943.9 and peak at 12 041 while the archetype total reaches 44 338, the rest being JAP/MAN/USA/SOV/YUG-created.
  - 2026-08-15 · `af003548` · PASSED (weakest pass on record) — ROM/HUN rifle stockpiles are off zero in **11 of 12** donor-surplus months, and discrete GER-created deliveries land: HUN `ger_inf_2` 31 → **5 550** (1943.12), ITA 0 → **5 060** (1942.4), ROM `ger_inf_6` −1 → **2 045** (1943.10), BUL 0 → **1 052** (1943.1). Caveats: no delivery reaches the 6 000 base tier at snapshot (max 5 548), and the two clean donor/starving overlaps (1944.10, 1944.11) shipped nothing — the `random_other_country` one-donor-per-week lottery hypothesis stands and the donor-selected counter is still the right next step.

### R8. UK air hosting works

- **Fix under test:** `24ffda1ac` (level-ladder rewrite) + instrumentation `57d6136dd` + `124b2a3b6` (demand side: `Allies_dday_air` pre-arms 1944.2.1 with `Allies_dont_logi_strike_during_bob` standing down in step — +200k on region 239 live from February, so southern-England capacity finally has a pull to fill). New sub-probe: USAAF wings based in the UK at a **1944.3–1944.5 save** (pre-landing window), not just at 1944.6.
- **Fixes 46–47 (added 2026-08-12) — the throughput levers this item's own 2026-08-11 audit named.** That audit ranked three levers; **(1) and (3) are now shipped, (2) is NOT — and (2) is the likeliest remaining binding constraint.**
  - **Fix 46** — `WA_AI_PC_has_same_type_project_here` was ROOT-keyed, so for `allied_build` types the host and its ally each saw an empty array for the other. On `9a4cd657` at 1944.7, **ENG and USA were each funding an air base in state 126 AND state 860 simultaneously**, and the hosting-capacity gate counted the same state twice as claimed. Now backed by a builder-agnostic per-state/per-type counter `WA_AI_PC_state_type_projects^<type>` (incremented in `WA_AI_PC_start_project`, decremented in `WA_AI_PC_end_project_by_id`, both through the same `var:WA_AI_PC_target_state` accessor). Also added the free-slot test to both allied branches, which previously could fund a build that `WA_AI_PC_complete_project_by_id` then refused to spawn.
  - **Fix 47** — air bases move to a new **priority band 300** (above the 250 strategic refineries, below rail-prewar 500) while `WA_AI_PC_air_force_needs_basing_priority` holds (own air force > 500 planes, or a faction partner > 1000); `@UK_AIR_HOSTED_FACTION_SHARE` 0.5 → **0.65** (target 133 levels vs a structural ceiling of 132); the USA branch's host-condition gate **removed entirely** so the ally co-builds until the target is met; and ENG takes `WA_AI_PC_override_max_factories_factor` while the deficit stands, lifting its PC pool from ~37 to ~53. (Fix 47 shipped that override at **0.5**, deliberately equal to the railway system's value on the same shared flag; `2937070f7` later raised the air branch alone to **0.6** — see probe 4 and the defect note in the As-of header.)
  - **Regression this sequence nearly shipped, and the reason the two USA probes below are mandatory:** the band-300 change alone would have *closed* the USA's old `air_factories_assigned < 1` gate permanently (ENG now always assigns something), locking out the builder that supplied **585 of 874 air civ-months** measured over 1942–44. Score the USA's participation explicitly; do not infer it from total capacity growth.
  - **Lever (2) now shipped too — all three are in.** `queued_amount_` counted **all** type-2 projects country-wide, so the UK strategy shared its 3-slot budget with R16's theatre strategy (`911bed3c`: both ENG slots held Pacific projects at 1943.6, all three USA slots non-UK at 1945.6), and band 300 could not arbitrate because UK and theatre air bases sit in the *same* band. `WA_AI_PC_start_project` now takes an opt-in `_project_queue_max_scoped`; when set, `queued_amount_` counts only projects carrying the caller's `_project_type_id` (UK = 20, theatre = 21). Each strategy gets its own 3 slots. Unscoped callers are bit-identical to before.
  - **Two shared-temp traps handled in that change, both worth knowing before touching this path again.** (i) `_project_queue_max_scoped` is latched and zeroed at entry to `WA_AI_PC_start_project`, because **30 of the 35 call sites never set it** — the same class of bug `queue_functions.txt:681-689` records for `_project_queue_max`. (ii) `_project_type_id` is deliberately **NOT** cleared the same way because `WA_AI_PC_start_project` still consumes it for the scoped budget and persists it on the new project; the two air strategies retire their own private tags caller-side. **Fix 50 closes the adjacent railway leak:** the production wrapper now pins stale-project validation to type 13 even when a frontier-port helper leaves the shared temp at 14. The deterministic `WA_TEST_RW_014` harness test reproduces that precondition and requires the stale rail to be cancelled while the port survives.
- **Fix 51 (added 2026-08-14) — WHERE the levels go, not how many.** Five campaigns have scored the capacity leg and it keeps passing; `f9321934` then showed the levels arriving in the wrong counties. At 1946.8 the 98 built levels sat ~8.9 per state across all 11 hosting states, and ENG's two largest plane concentrations were **Gloucestershire (827)** and **Warwickshire (460)** — the two states *furthest* from France — while Kent held 367. That was not a malfunction: the selector was a **level ladder** (`while_loop_effect` over `_uk_air_try_level_` 0→cap, taking a random hosting state sitting at exactly that level), i.e. literally "expand the least-developed state first". A levelling rule, doing what it said; nobody had asked whether levelling was the right ordering key. Fix 51 replaces the ordering key with a **fixed south→north priority walk** over `global.WA_AI_uk_air_priority_states`, taking the first state still under the cap and buildable.
  - **Order (verified against `map/` + `history/states/`, not inherited):** mean pixel distance from each state's provinces to the Northern-France + Low-Countries target set (Vlaanderen/Holland/Nord-Pas-de-Calais, Picardy/Upper+Lower Normandy/Brittany, Ile de France/Paris). **857 Kent → 127 Sussex → 961 South London → 125 Essex → 126 North London → 860 Norfolk → 859 Dorset → 338 Gloucestershire → 129 Leicestershire → 128 Warwickshire → 123 Cornwall.** Two ranks are judgement: Sussex above South London (5px worse mean, better *nearest*, coastal Channel field); and **Cornwall last despite being the second most southerly state** — its frontage is Biscay/Brittany, its distance to the Calais/Belgium arc is 118.8, second-worst of the eleven and worse than Warwickshire's 100.8. Norfolk moves *up* (east coast, faces the Netherlands) and Dorset *down* (south but western) relative to a naive latitude sort.
  - **What this cannot do, stated up front.** It adds **no capacity** — the ceiling is 11 × `@UK_AIR_TARGET_LEVEL_CAP` = 132 levels either way, and `f9321934`'s 9 800-vs-15 174 deficit is a production race this change does not win. It buys range and mission efficiency per level built. If the next campaign shows concentration achieved *and* the basing outcome still dead, the bottleneck is total capacity or the USAAF's own basing decision (R15), and this item should stop absorbing effort.
  - **The state LIST is unchanged, deliberately.** 122 Wales, 858 Berkshire and 130 Yorkshire hold ENG air bases in campaigns but are not hosting states; adding any of them raises the structural ceiling, which this item's own 2026-08-11 audit says not to do until R15 justifies more capacity. Berkshire measures better than Gloucestershire (122.4 vs 133.4) and is the first candidate if that changes.
  - **Structure:** the ordered list is duplicated on purpose — `WA_AI_uk_air_hosting_state` (`WA_AI_CONSTRUCTION_triggers.txt`) stays authoritative for **membership** and now carries the ranking method in its header; `WA_AI_uk_air_hosting_priority_init` (`WA_AI_CONSTRUCTION_PRIORITY_strategies.txt`) mirrors it into a global array. Neither can be derived from the other (`@` constants are file-scoped; a plain-id array cannot be membership-tested from state scope — the `.id` scope-encoding lesson). Drift is made survivable by an **unordered fallback tail** over the same trigger: a state missing from the array loses its rank, never its eligibility. `WA_TLM_r8_uk_air_fallback_n` counts that path.
- **Fix 72 (added 2026-08-14) — PC's building-cost table was under-charging air bases; `project_cost` figures are NOT comparable across this commit.** PC is a *shadow* construction system: `WA_AI_PC_get_building_cost` seeds both `wa_ai_pc_project_cost^<id>` and `wa_ai_pc_progress^<id>`, progress is counted down by assigned factory output, and completion calls `add_building_construction … instant_build = yes`. The AI never pays the engine's price — it pays PC's — so an under-estimate is literally free industry. Three entries had drifted from `common/buildings/00_buildings.txt`: **air base per-level 250 → 300**, **radar per-level 500 → 2500**, **naval base flat 3000 → 10000**. Only the air-base one touches this item.
  - **The discount this item was receiving, measured on `f9321934` at 1944.6:** the 11 UK hosting states climbed **30 → 70** air-base levels (153 level-units of `per_level_extra_cost`, plus 40 × 500 base). Real price **65 900 IC**; PC charged **58 250** — an **11.6% discount**, i.e. roughly 4–5 of the 40 levels were free. Expect the same civ-month budget to buy ~10% fewer levels from here on. **A post-fix campaign showing slightly slower level growth at unchanged `pc_air_factories_assigned` is the fix working, not a regression** — score the *funding* probes (Fix 46/47 probes 2–4), not raw level count, when judging whether the builder is healthy.
  - **Stale figures elsewhere in this item.** The 2026-08-11 quantitative audit (history entry below) derives its throughput arithmetic from `cost = 500 + 250 × level` and "≈35–45 days at a full 20-factory slot". The formula is now `500 + 300 × level`; at the 1944-era level 8–11 range that is ~2 900–3 800 IC per level instead of ~2 500–3 250, so the per-slot rate falls from ~8–10 to **~7–9 levels/yr**. The audit's *conclusion* (throughput-bound, not ceiling-bound) is unaffected and if anything strengthened. Historical entries are left as recorded — do not retrofit them.
  - **Railways followed as Fix 73 (see R19)**, once the diagnosis came in. The type-13 branch's `170 + 130 × level` was not an invention — those are **vanilla's** `rail_way` numbers, faithfully copied; this mod re-priced `rail_way` to a flat `800 / 0` (1000 → 800 in `42b53eccb`, Oct 2023) and the AI's shadow table was never updated. Same drift class as the three above, opposite story. Scored under R19 because the risk is queue fairness, not cost.
- **Fix 72 probe (added 2026-08-14) — cheap, deterministic, no telemetry needed; run it once on the next campaign to confirm the build is IN, then it is done.** Read `var ENG "^wa_ai_pc_project_cost"` alongside `var ENG "^wa_ai_pc_building_type"` and `savegame.py buildings ENG <save> --match air_base` at any save holding a live type-2 project. For a project targeting a hosting state whose `air_base` currently reads level *L*, `wa_ai_pc_project_cost^<id>` must equal **500 + 300 × L** — so the ladder is **500 / 800 / 1 100 / 1 400 …**, never the old **500 / 750 / 1 000 / 1 250**. (Cost is snapshotted from the *live* building level at queue time, so two projects queued for the same state on the same pulse legitimately carry the same cost; only the spawned level advances it.) A single-level campaign-wide check works too: divide any hosting-state project's cost by its state's level — the residual over 500 must be a multiple of 300, not 250. The two ladders first diverge at L = 1, so **do not test on a state at level 0**. A single type-2 cost landing on the old ladder means the build predates the fix and every cost-sensitive reading in this item is on the wrong side of the boundary. Same check applies to type 4 (radar: `2000 + 2500 × L`, was `2000 + 500 × L`) and type 14 (naval base: flat `10000`, was `3000`) on any country — those belong to R9/R21 but share the write site, so a mismatch there falsifies the build just as well.
- **Retirement (Fix 72 leg):** RETIREABLE after **1** campaign whose type-2 costs sit on the new ladder — it verifies a constant, not a behaviour. The behavioural consequence (slower level growth per civ-month) is scored under the capacity leg, which does not retire.
- **Fix 75 (added 2026-08-14) — the target formula, not the throughput, was ending the programme. `@UK_AIR_HOSTED_FACTION_SHARE` 0.65 → 1.0.** Fix 47 raised this 0.5 → 0.65 to keep the gate open longer; `f9321934` shows 0.65 still stood the strategy down **14 months before D-Day**. Measured at 1944.6.1: capacity **7 000** (70 levels) against **10 021** faction planes, so the 0.65 target of 6 514 was already met — `dbg_best` read **−2 ("no deficit") continuously from 1942.7**, and the **last UK air project of the entire campaign started 1943.4** (ENG `dbg_started` frozen at 12, USA at 20, for the following 14 months of saves). The build halted at **70 of the 132-level ceiling (53%)** with ~30% of Allied aviation unhoused. Any share < 1.0 declares victory while part of the faction's air force has nowhere in Britain to stand.
  - **Ceiling still binds; this cannot run away.** 11 × `@UK_AIR_TARGET_LEVEL_CAP` = 132 levels = 13 200 capacity. At 1944 plane counts (10 021) the 1.0 target is **reachable** — 0.65 simply never asked for it. Beyond ~13 200 faction planes the deficit becomes permanent by construction.
  - **Accepted cost, and it is now permanent.** Air bases hold priority band **300**, above the 250-band refineries, for as long as a deficit stands — so past that threshold ENG's shortage refineries run on the Fix 41 overtake lane instead of the sorted fill, permanently rather than temporarily. On `f9321934` the threshold falls in 1945. Refineries held **40–69% of both ENG's and USA's queues** from mid-1943 in response to a real resource deficit, so this is a live tradeoff. **If the refineries need their band back, LOWER this share** — do not raise `@UK_AIR_TARGET_LEVEL_CAP` or extend the state list, which this item forbids until R15 justifies more capacity.
  - **What it cannot fix, stated up front — the shortage is POSITIONAL, not aggregate.** At 1944.6.1 the home island already held **10 100 capacity against 8 033 aircraft, ~2 067 free**, while the USAAF kept just **500 of its 5 431 land-based aircraft in Britain (9%)** and only **200 in the 11 hosting states (3.7%)**. Every south-eastern field was RAF-saturated — Kent 600/600, Essex 700/700, **Sussex 700 in a 600 field (over-stacked)** — while **Norfolk, the largest airfield on the island at 7 levels, sat at 112/700**. USAAF top-5 bases were Virginia, New York City, Maryland, Kentucky, Newark, with 26% of its pool on carrier decks. Extra levels only help if Fix 51's south-first walk lands them on the Channel coast; a USAAF that leaves 700-capacity Norfolk empty may not use them either way.
- **Fix 75 probes (added 2026-08-14) — run all four.**
  1. **The deficit reopens.** `var ENG "^wa_ai_uk_air_dbg_best="` must go **non-negative** (a state id) again instead of holding −2, and `dbg_started` must resume climbing past the ENG 12 / USA 20 where `f9321934` froze. A campaign still showing −2 throughout 1943–44 means the change did not apply — check the build carries `@UK_AIR_HOSTED_FACTION_SHARE = 1.0`.
  2. **Capacity climbs past the old plateau.** `dbg_capacity` should exceed **7 000** and head toward ~10 100 (101 levels) at 1944-era plane counts. Baseline to beat: 70 levels / 7 000, flat from 1944.1.
  3. **Refinery collateral — the regression this could cause.** ENG must still show live type-8/11/15 projects holding non-zero factories while the air deficit stands (`var ENG "^wa_ai_pc_building_type"` + `"^wa_ai_pc_assigned_factories"`). **Zero refinery funding across a whole deficit window means the band-300 cost is too high — lower the share, do not touch the cap.**
  4. **Basing outcome (cited from R15, not scored here).** Baseline to beat: **USAAF 9% of land-based aircraft in Britain, 3.7% in the 11 hosting states, at 1944.6.1.** If concentration (Fix 51) lands, capacity climbs, and this number stays near 9%, the constraint is the USAAF's own basing decision and **R8 should stop absorbing effort** per the Fix 51 note above.
- **Retirement (Fix 75 leg):** does NOT retire on its own — it changes the capacity leg's pass threshold and folds into it. The old "planes/capacity ratio improves vs 3.5×" baseline is superseded: at 1.0 the criterion is whether the deficit closes at all, not whether the ratio merely improves.
- **Pass** (ownership recut 2026-08-11 — R8 owns the CAPACITY side of the USAAF-Europe chain; the basing OUTCOME is scored once, under R15, and cited here; **capacity criterion recut 2026-08-14 by Fix 75 — the target is now 1.0 × faction planes, so "no deficit" means genuine full hosting rather than 65% of it**): `wa_ai_uk_air_dbg_started > 0` on ENG; type-2 projects queued and completing (capacity actually grows); the ENG planes/capacity ratio IMPROVES vs the `31eaf7e6` baseline of 3.5× at 1944.6 (capacity tracks fleet growth — R23 funding + R26 lane are the mechanisms under test here); **and (added 2026-08-14, Fix 51) the CONCENTRATION leg below**; USAAF-in-UK cited from R15's scoring.
- **Fix 51 concentration probes (added 2026-08-14) — run all three. The first is the criterion; the other two say whether the mechanism or the geography is responsible.**
  1. **Per-state distribution is south-heavy.** `savegame.py buildings ENG <saves...> --match air_base`, read per state at 1944.6 and at the last save. **PASS = every one of 857/127/961 is at `@UK_AIR_TARGET_LEVEL_CAP` (12) before any of 128/129/338 exceeds level 4.** FAIL = a Midlands state above 4 while a top-3 southern state is still below 12 *and* that southern state has free building slots (check `buildings ENG` for arms factories saturating the state — a genuinely slot-full Kent that the walk correctly fell through is NOT a failure; see probe 3). Baseline to beat: `f9321934` 1946.8, ~8.9 levels uniformly across all 11, Gloucestershire and Warwickshire holding the two largest plane concentrations.
  2. **The ordered walk is the selector (build fingerprint + mechanism).** `tlm ENG` / `tlm USA`, family `wa_tlm_r8_uk_air_*`. `wa_tlm_version >= 8` or the family is **absent → probe void, never FAILED**. Then: **mean rank = `rank_sum / (q_n − fallback_n)`** should sit **below ~4.0** for most of the campaign (1 = Kent, 11 = Cornwall; a random-among-least-developed selector tends to 6.0). `rank_max` tells how far north it ever reached and should stay low until the southern ranks cap out. `q_n` should track `wa_ai_uk_air_dbg_started` exactly — they are incremented on the same verified-queue gate, so a divergence means one of the two write sites is broken.
  3. **No drift, no stall.** `wa_tlm_r8_uk_air_fallback_n` should be **0** in a consistent build. Non-zero means either (a) `WA_AI_uk_air_hosting_state` and the array disagree — compare the two lists in the diff — or (b) every ranked state was capped or out of building slots on that pulse, which is the legitimate case and should coincide with `rank_max = 11`. Also check `wa_tlm_r8_uk_air_last_t` keeps moving while a deficit stands: a frozen stamp with a live deficit is a stalled walk, which is the specific regression this shape could introduce.
- **Retirement (Fix 51 leg):** RETIREABLE on **3** consecutive campaigns meeting probe 1 with `fallback_n = 0`. Retiring it deletes the `WA_TLM_r8_uk_air_*` write sites and init lines per `WA_TLM_TELEMETRY_SYSTEM.md` §3.7. The capacity and basing legs retire on their own terms.
- **Fix 78 (added 2026-08-14, campaign `be18f9c7`) — the host was never FUNDING the levels, and two of this item's own instruments were lying about it.** `be18f9c7` scored the capacity leg as "delivery far too slow, deficit blows out to 106 levels", and the diagnosis found the cause is neither eligibility nor slots nor the WA cap:
  - **What was falsified.** (a) *Slot exhaustion at level 8* — `air_base` has its own `level_cap { state_max = 20 }` in `00_buildings.txt`; it does **not** compete for the shared state slot pool, and the plateau states sat at level 8 of 12/20 with free slots. (b) *A leaked `WA_AI_PC_state_type_projects^2` blocking the top ranks* — checked on 33 state×save cells across 1942.6 / 1943.5 / 1944.6: **zero mismatches**, every non-zero counter backed by a live project, every zero backed by empty builder arrays. Fix 46's counter is correct. (c) *Cross-builder double-booking* — none at any sampled date (probe 5 clean).
  - **The actual mechanism: ENG's band-300 air projects are outranked by its own band-1000 railway queue, and the Fix 41 overtake lane serves only ONE project a week across the whole queue.** ENG's `wa_ai_pc_air_factories_assigned` reads **0 in 20 of 30 months** of 1942.1–1944.6 with all its type-2 projects at 0 factories and stall 3–12 weeks; `wa_ai_uk_air_dbg_started` froze at **17 for nine straight months** (1943.7–1944.3). The one month ENG's rail queue was nearly empty — 1943.5, a single rail queued — its air projects took **53 factories** immediately. The pool was never the constraint; the queue ordering was. Unfunded projects also hold the 3-slot UK budget open, so funding starvation and site-selection throughput are one defect, not two.
  - **The fix: a reservation lane, not a band change.** `WA_AI_PC_assign_factories` gains a second lane (after Fix 41's, before the sorted fill) that grants `min(20, pool)` to the highest-priority live band-300 type-2 project, **only** while the country holds `WA_AI_PC_air_basing_reserve` — a 30-day flag set exclusively by `WA_AI_build_uk_air_hosting_capacity`'s deficit branch, so it self-releases within a month of the target being met. Raising the air band above rail was rejected: the Fix 41 band table exists to stop the rail livelock, and this must not reopen it. At most one 20-civ slot is diverted.
  - **This also explains the secondary "ENG south-first ordering drifts (mean rank 7.26 vs USA 4.43)" finding — it is a CONSEQUENCE, not a separate defect.** The walk stops at the first buildable state, and a state with a live project of the same type is not buildable (Fix 46, builder-agnostic). ENG's top-ranked states each held a starving, never-completing project, so every pulse fell through them into the unranked tail. Fixing the funding fixes the ordering; **do not re-tune the rank list for this.** `fallback_n = 0` on both builders confirms it was never list drift.
- **Fix 78 probes (added 2026-08-14) — run all four. (1) is the criterion; (2) is the one that tells you the lane armed at all.**
  1. **ENG actually funds air bases.** `var ENG "^wa_ai_pc_air_factories_assigned="` across every monthly save of a deficit window. **PASS = non-zero at the majority of samples**, against the `be18f9c7` baseline of **0 in 20 of 30 months**. Read it together with probe 2 — a 0 here with a rising `air_lane_grants` is a sampling artefact, not starvation.
  2. **The lane armed and fired (build fingerprint + mechanism).** `tlm ENG` / `tlm USA`, metrics `wa_tlm_r8_air_lane_grants` / `_last_t`. `wa_tlm_version >= 15` or the pair is **absent → probe void, never FAILED**. `_grants` counts **funded weeks**, cumulative, so it cannot be missed between monthly saves the way the snapshot gauge can. **`_grants = 0` across a whole deficit window = the lane never fired** — check `flags ENG --match WA_AI_PC_air_basing_reserve` is present while `dbg_best` is non-negative; a missing flag means the deficit branch is not being reached at all, which is a different (upstream) defect.
  3. **`dbg_started` unfreezes.** ENG's counter must keep climbing through a deficit window rather than parking for months. Baseline to beat: frozen at 17 for nine months (1943.7–1944.3) while capacity crawled 8 200 → 8 900.
  4. **Rail did not pay for it — the regression this could cause.** The reserve diverts at most one 20-civ slot from the band-1000 fill. On ENG and GER, type-13 projects must still hold factories and still complete (cross-check R19's rail-completion criterion). **A campaign where ENG's rail queue stops completing entirely while air bases boom means the reservation is too greedy — cap it below 20 rather than removing the lane.**
- **Retirement (Fix 78 leg):** RETIREABLE on **2** consecutive campaigns meeting probes 1–2 with probe 4 clean. Retiring it deletes the `WA_TLM_r8_air_lane_*` write site and init lines per `WA_TLM_TELEMETRY_SYSTEM.md` §3.7.
- **Probe:** `airload.py --states 857,127,961,125,126,860,859,338,129,128,123 <saves...>` for the engine-side load (NOMINAL wing slots vs capacity — added 2026-08-16, see the airload gotcha in `wa-savegame-analysis`; the `dbg_planes`/`dbg_capacity` pair below is a plane count and under-reads the load); `var ENG "^wa_ai_uk_air_dbg" <saves...>`; `var ENG "^wa_ai_pc_building_type"`; USA wing basing via the top-level `strategic_air` block (wings → `air_base` id → `state=`; see R12 note). **Counter caveats (registry: `documentation/WA_TLM_TELEMETRY_SYSTEM.md` §2.1):** `_best = -2` means "no deficit / satisfied" — health, not breakage. `_capacity`'s ×100 is correct (`AIRBASE_CAPACITY_MULT = 100` in this mod).
- **Fix 46/47 probes (added 2026-08-12) — run all five; the funding ones are what distinguish "the fix worked" from "the fix locked a builder out".**
  1. **`_started` semantics changed — do not compare the number across the Fix 47 boundary.** It now counts projects that verifiably entered the queue (queue-growth guard, matching the `thair` twin); before, it counted deficit pulses and over-read ~4× (`9a4cd657` 1944.7: ENG 97 + USA 39 = 136 against 34 real levels; `911bed3c`: 176 against ~47). **A post-fix campaign reading much LOWER is the fix working, not a regression.** Still cross-check against `savegame.py buildings ENG <saves...> --match air_base`.
  2. **USA not locked out** — `var USA "^wa_ai_pc_air_factories_assigned"` must be **non-zero at the majority of samples from the first deficit onward**, and `var USA "^wa_ai_uk_air_dbg_started"` must keep climbing. Zero USA air factories across a whole deficit window = the host-gate regression returned. Baseline to beat: USA supplied 585 of 874 air civ-months over 1942–44 on `9a4cd657`.
  3. **ENG funds it too, without losing its refineries** — `var ENG "^wa_ai_pc_air_factories_assigned"` should sit near 33–40 (was **1** at `9a4cd657` 1944.7), while ENG still shows live type-8/11/15 refinery projects. Air *and* refineries is the point of the override; air *instead of* refineries means the override did not apply.
  4. **The override is live and self-releasing** — `flags ENG --match WA_AI_PC_override_max_factories_factor` present while a deficit stands, and `var ENG "^wa_ai_pc_override_max_factories_factor"` = **0.6**. Absent after the target is met = correct (30-day expiry).
     - **Value corrected 2026-08-14 (campaign `be18f9c7`), and the old interpretation was backwards.** This probe used to expect **0.5** and read anything else as railway contention. The two writers of this shared flag/variable pair now hold **different** values: the UK-air branch writes **0.6** (`WA_AI_CONSTRUCTION_PRIORITY_strategies.txt:1293`, raised from 0.5 by `2937070f7` alongside `@AI_PC_ALLOC_FRACTION` 0.35 → 0.40) while the railway branch still writes **0.5** (`WA_AI_CONSTRUCTION_PRIORITY_railway_core.txt:206`). So the variable is now a **last-writer-wins record, and the value names the winner**: 0.6 = the UK-air writer wrote last (the ceiling Fix 47 sized, which is what this probe wants); **0.5 = the railway writer wrote last**, i.e. ENG spent that month on the railway system's ceiling and the air branch's own sizing was overwritten. `be18f9c7` reads 0.6 on ENG at every sample — the air writer won every time, which is a PASS.
     - **Neither value proves the absence of contention any more, so do not read one as "clean".** Report the value observed at each sample and flag any *alternation* between 0.5 and 0.6 across consecutive saves on ENG — that is the timer the code's own HAZARD comment (`strategies.txt:1280-1285`) warns about, and it is now reachable because the values diverged. **This is a live defect, not a probe artefact** — see the defect note in the As-of header. Historical entries recording 0.5 (e.g. `a8b29e09`) were taken on builds predating `2937070f7`, where both writers wrote 0.5; do not retrofit them.
  5. **No cross-builder double-booking (Fix 46)** — dump both PC queues at 2–3 dates and confirm **no state id appears as a type-2 target in ENG's and USA's queue at the same date**. `9a4cd657` 1944.7 had states 126 and 860 in both.
  6. **Slot budgets are un-shared (lever 2)** — for ENG and USA, read `wa_ai_pc_type_id^<id>` alongside `wa_ai_pc_building_type^<id>` for every queued project. A country may now hold **up to 3 type-2 projects tagged 20 (UK) AND up to 3 tagged 21 (theatre) simultaneously** — that combination is the fix working, not over-queueing. Every type-2 project should carry 20, 21, or 0 (island fortification); a type-2 tagged **13 or 14 means a stale railway id leaked** and the caller-side retirement failed. Baseline that must not recur: at `911bed3c` 1943.6 both of ENG's air slots were Pacific on the pulse the UK deficit reopened.
- **Capacity expectation for the next run:** with ENG ~33 and USA ~60 civs on UK air (vs 41 combined measured), hosting-state levels should climb from the 119 recorded at `9a4cd657` toward the 132 ceiling. **Reaching the ceiling is a real stop condition, not a failure** — at that point the state list or `@UK_AIR_TARGET_LEVEL_CAP` is the constraint, and *neither should be touched before the basing outcome (R15) justifies more capacity.*
- **Threshold:** 5 (behavioural — the wing-basing outcome is the point; the dbg variable alone is not a pass).
- **Streak:** 0
- **Metric notes (2026-08-11, both corrections — an earlier version of this file got both wrong):**
  - `wa_ai_uk_air_dbg_capacity` = (hosting-state `air_base` levels + ROOT-queued type-2 projects targeting a hosting state) × 100. **The ×100 is CORRECT for this mod** — `common/defines/05_defines.lua:173` sets `AIRBASE_CAPACITY_MULT = 100` (vanilla is 200). There is no "2× pessimistic" adjustment to make; the dbg figure *is* the engine figure.
  - **`wa_ai_uk_air_dbg_started` is NOT a project count — do not read it as throughput.** `strategies.txt:1120-1128` increments it unconditionally after `WA_AI_PC_start_project`, which silently declines on `queue_max`/dedup; the theatre twin has the queue-growth guard (`:1440-1451`), the UK one does not. Proof: Δ`started` = Δ`called` = 13 exactly in every full deficit quarter — one "start" per weekly pulse, impossible when ≤3 projects exist and each takes 35–45 days. Across the campaign ENG 115 + USA 61 = 176 recorded starts produced **+50 actual levels (≤28%)**. Read it as "pulses spent in deficit with an eligible state".
    - **SUPERSEDED by Fix 46 (2026-08-12) — this note describes the pre-Fix-46 write site and no longer holds.** The UK counter now carries the same queue-growth guard as the theatre twin, so from that commit on it *is* a real count of projects queued. On `be18f9c7` it reads ENG 19 / USA 44 over the whole campaign — the ~4× over-read is gone. Probe 1 of the Fix 46/47 set already says not to compare across the boundary; this note is kept only so old history entries stay readable.
  - `dbg_best` = **−2 when there is no deficit**, otherwise the ladder level a project was started at. A `−2` is the system saying *satisfied*, not *broken*.
    - **RECUT 2026-08-14 (Fix 78) — and the old wording cost `be18f9c7` a wrong headline.** `dbg_best` is **reset at the top of every gated pulse and only overwritten when a site is actually queued**, so before Fix 78 the value −2 was the resting state of *both* "no deficit" and "deficit open, this pulse queued nothing" (budget full, or every eligible site already carrying a project). A monthly save catches one pulse, so five consecutive monthly −2 readings are **five snapshots, not a five-month state** — on `be18f9c7` the USA read −2 at 1943.2–1943.6 while `dbg_started` advanced **+1 in every one of those months**. Fix 78 splits the legend: **−2 = no deficit, −1 = deficit open but nothing queued this pulse**, else the air-base level of the site queued. From `wa_tlm_version >= 15`, treat a run of −1 as "budget full", never as "no eligible state", and **always corroborate with `dbg_started` / `wa_tlm_r8_uk_air_q_n`, which are cumulative and cannot be missed between saves.**
- **History:**
  - 2026-08-10 · `66d6b53c` · PASSED — `started` 22→74→102 from 1942.6 (engages exactly when planes overshoot capacity — the pre-1942 `best=-2` is correct gating); type-2 projects at Cornwall/Warwickshire/Gloucestershire; those states' air-base levels flat for 6 years then step 1–2 → 7–8; hosting capacity 3 400 → 8 500. **Caveat:** the USAAF barely used it — 3 wings / 500 planes on UK soil pre-D-Day vs 8 595 planes parked in CONUS. Construction leg is proven; consider a numeric wing bar for the basing leg (engine wing placement, not the WA project system).
  - 2026-08-11 · `31eaf7e6` · FAILED — the construction leg is alive (`started` 13→52→83, capacity 3.9k→7.1k, ENG home air bases reach level 8–9) but the basing outcome — the point of the item — is absent: ZERO USA planes based in the UK at 1943.6 and a single 98-plane wing at 1944.6.1 (pre-D-Day), worse than `66d6b53c`'s 500. Second defect: ENG planes/capacity worsens to 3.5× at 1944.6 (+1.4k capacity added against +13.2k planes).
  - 2026-08-11 · code audit — the basing gap is R15's root cause (see R15: net-negative Allied air ledger; between the fall of France and 1944.5.15 NO positive-importance region is reachable from an English airfield, so the USAAF has no reason to cross the Atlantic — the single wing appears in exactly `Allies_dday_air`'s 6-week window). The 3.5× ratio is NOT a stale input: the target formula tracks fleet correctly (faction planes × 0.5) but is throttled by `queue_max = 3` × 1 level/project and structurally capped at 11 states × level 12 = 13 200 capacity — already reached vs a ~25k-plane faction. Real accounting bug found: the queue scan (`strategies.txt:970-974`) counts ANY type-2 project as UK hosting, including theatre-air-base projects elsewhere (bounded +300). Do not chase the capacity leg further until the demand ledger (R15 fix A/B) ships.
  - 2026-08-11 · `911bed3c` · **FAILED — capacity leg PASSES, basing outcome fails again (3rd campaign).** Capacity: `started` 12→37→60→86→115, `dbg_capacity` 4 000→8 700, UK hosting-state air-base levels 50→67 across 1943.6→1944.6 (e.g. 126: 4→7, 338: 4→6) — Fixes 37/38 demonstrably fund it (see R23). Worst single months: 1944.9 −12 (62→51), 1944.8 −12, 1944.2 −11. **The submarine attribution is falsified** (see the note below), so the destruction is land combat. **Retarget the diagnosis: not "why does the USA never deploy" but "why does the USA lose divisions 5–6× faster than the UK".** The composition axis remains stable and is still not the failure.
  - **Submarine hypothesis — TESTED AND FALSIFIED as the cause of the US army cap (2026-08-12).** The German submarine campaign is real and enormous — **9 284 convoys sunk** 1939–1946.6 (peak 1 779 in 1945, never suppressed, 100–190/month in the final months), with **~101 of GER's 102 naval factories on `ger_cruiser_submarine_hull_3` for six years ≈ 12 % of all German industry**, 486 submarines built against 4 capitals and 11 screens, 287 boats at peak against ~329 cumulative losses. It nonetheless cannot be what capped the US army: **ENG absorbed 1.3–1.9× more convoy losses than the USA in every rolling window and still grew 12 → 152 divisions**; USA convoy stock rose **monotonically** 805 → 1 932 → 3 718 → 5 623 → 7 750, where 7 750 is the mod's own scripted cap; USA manpower **never dipped**, sitting welded at 319 000 for 17 consecutive months and 440 000 for 10, including months that lost 12 divisions; and USA infantry equipment **accumulated to 130 376** while the army was pinned at 51. Victim split of the 9 284: ENG 3 630, SOV 2 542, USA 1 393, CAN 702. **Two probe limits worth carrying forward:** the save contains **no in-transit loss accounting of any kind** (no manpower-lost-at-sea field, no destroyed-transport counter; `division.killed` is cumulative *combat* manpower), so "were troops sunk" is only answerable through the monthly division series; and `sunk_convoys_history` is a **rolling 24-month window per save**, so full-campaign coverage needs stitching across ~5 saves.

(b) **GER holds ~100 factories on submarine lines but builds only 10 boats between 1945.6 and 1946.7** (cumulative 476 → 486) — stalled lines rather than de-prioritisation, and very likely the same iron/steel starvation R29 records for GER in that window (steel park 20 active / 43 inactive at 1946.4, iron net −240). Both deserve their own items if they recur.

- **Fix under test:** `edb746d17` (generic Default-layer AIR domain: 10 theatre blocks — enemy AND own side both hold anchor states; capital-theatre excluded) + `124b2a3b6` (pulls raised +10k→+50k AND the ENG.txt Allied suppression family retuned −250k…−1M → −2k…−40k, making the Allied European ledger net-positive in every contested region — worst case Poland/296 = +2k; this removes the `31eaf7e6` root cause). Watch item from the retune: +50k exceeds the engine's hot-front ~35k reference — air abandoning an active non-capital home front for a foreign contested theatre would be the over-correction signature.
- **Pass:** by 1943.6 USA has ≥25% of deployed planes based outside the continental US, with USA wings at bases in NA/Mediterranean states (446/447/452, 458–460, 115/117/156) or Pacific island states (629 Hawaii, 634 Solomons, 633 Marshalls, 639/643/725 Micronesia, 1001–1004 New Guinea, 327/623–628 Philippines). ### R17. Italian campaign: Sicily held, mainland reached, Axis defends

- **Architecture review: `documentation/WA_ITALIAN_THEATRE_REVIEW.md` (2026-08-12). Read it before proposing any fix here, and before re-scoring this item.** Headline: **the Allies are not beaten in Italy, they stand down — and winning is what switches them off.** Every Allied boost in the theatre is gated on the *enemy* holding ground and every suppression on the *Allies* holding it, so capturing Calabria (state 156) swings `area_priority` italy −600 / south_italy −800 in one instant and permanently arms `no_italy_invasion_early` (whose third OR branch has no exit condition). The Italian defection then fires an Allied brake (−80) and a German boost (+100/+100) on the *same two regions*. Confirmed fingerprint: the theatre becomes British — USA 0-4 divisions vs ENG 26-36 through 1945. **Three things the review RULES OUT — do not re-investigate:** AIFC/schwerpunkt selection (measured and refuted — USA anchored in Italy 10 consecutive months, ENG 22 of 32 saves), `CAPS_remote_fronts_*` (Italy is in no sink area), and the `fall_achse` buffer (its own gate makes it dead within one day on the defection path — **criterion (4) below has been crediting it for a front stack it never supplied**).

- **Fix under test (2026-08-12, NOT YET TESTED — the gate-inversion package, §10 of the review):** P1 removed the unbounded third `enable` branch of `ALLIES_no_italy_invasion_early_THEATRE` (the permanent `area_priority italy -200` that armed on holding Sicily + Calabria); P2 added `ALLIES_italy_mainland_push_{THEATRE,FRONT}`, the successor the bridge never had, mutually exclusive with it; P3 scoped both `avoid_italy_overstack` brakes to `is_western_allies_major`, gave them the principle-4 escape hatch (`WA_AI_MILITARY_owns_italy_mainland`) so co-belligerent ITA stops braking its own soil, and dropped the `has_government = democratic` gate; P4 re-homed `ENG_war_against_ITA_3_FRONT` to the Faction layer as `ALLIES_italy_cobelligerent_support_FRONT` so USA/CAN get the only post-flip Italian positive in the mod. INVASION domain deliberately untouched (anti-scatter + Balkan policy ride on it). **Sub-probe, no new instrumentation:** (a) Allied divisions in regions 21/23/238 must NOT fall across the month Calabria (156) flips Allied — the `9a4cd657` signature is 73 → 45 with France still at 0; (b) USA divisions in Italy after the Italian defection must clear a floor of ~10 (observed 0-4 across 1944.10–1945.6, against ENG's 26-36) — the ENG/USA ratio is the single cleanest fingerprint that P4 took; (c) co-belligerent ITA divisions in regions 21/23 must not decline monotonically after the flip (observed 44 → 32 → 13 → 9-11). Score all three before judging the criteria below.

### R19. Starved priority-construction projects age out; no factories burn in hostile states (Fix 34)

- **Fix under test:** Fix 34 on `ai-rework` (`WA_AI_CONSTRUCTION_PRIORITY_core.txt`). Two parts: (a) the stall sweep's `queue^num > 5` gate replaced with `WA_AI_PC_assigned_factories_total > 0` — a project starved of factories for 25+ weeks is now cancelled whenever factories were flowing to *other* projects, regardless of queue length; (b) `WA_AI_PC_assign_factories` skips projects whose target state is hostile-controlled (same controller test as the completion path), freezing their progress instead of burning civ-weeks toward a completion that spawns nothing.
- **Bug being fixed:** `2b607968` @ 1943.9.1: ROM queue `[6,1,7,0]` — naval base slot 7 (prio 5000, 3000/3000, stall 17w) and air base slot 0 (prio 100, target state 143 no longer ROM-controlled, stall 17w, ~140 civ-days already burned) both starved behind prio-9999 railway segments, with the sweep unreachable at queue=4 ≤ 5. The old gate also allowed 5 starved nonrail projects to lock every `< 5` strategy gate shut permanently.
- **Pass:** on any campaign save carrying the fix, mid/late war: (1) no AI country has a queued project with `wa_ai_pc_stall_weeks^id > 31` while its `wa_ai_pc_assigned_factories_total > 0` (**recut 2026-08-15 on `af003548`, 26 → 31**: the shipped sweep bar is `@AI_PC_STALL_CANCEL_WEEKS = 30`, so a project legitimately reads 27–30 while funded and is swept the week it passes 30 — the old `> 26` literal was tighter than the code it verifies and tripped on USA 1944.6 stall 27 and JAP 1945.6 stall 30, both ≤ the bar; a stall of 32+ with funding is the real defect signature); (2) no queued project whose target state is enemy-controlled has `wa_ai_pc_assigned_factories^id > 0`; (3) negative guard — a country with `assigned_factories_total = 0` (collapse/full occupation) keeps its stalled queue uncancelled: stall_weeks > 31 with total = 0 is correct behaviour there, not a failure; (4) railway construction still completes normally — **criterion recut 2026-08-11 for Fix 41:** cancellations now come from TWO legitimate sources: the week-25 sweep AND the overtake lane's revalidation (from ~week 12, counted in `wa_tlm_pc_aging_reval_cancels`). A cancel at 12-24 weeks is only a defect if it is NOT accounted for by the reval counter; spot-check `sweep-cancels = total cancels − reval_cancels` were genuinely starved 25+ weeks.
- **Fix 73 (added 2026-08-14) — railway segments cost 2.67× more; this is the largest single change ever made to PC queue dynamics, and THIS item is what detects the fallout.** The type-13 branch of `WA_AI_PC_get_building_cost` charged `170 + 130 × (1 + existing level)`. Those are **vanilla's** `rail_way` numbers (`base_cost = 170`, `per_level_extra_cost = 130`) — the block was a faithful copy of the base game and was correct when written. This mod re-priced `rail_way` to a flat `base_cost = 800, per_level_extra_cost = 0` (1000 → 800 in `42b53eccb`, Oct 2023) and the AI's shadow table was never updated, so for ~3 years the AI bought rail at the base game's escalating price. It now reads `global.WA_AI_PC_BUILDING_RAILWAY_COST` (800, flat, no level term), which is the same figure `@WA_AI_PC_RAILWAY_BASE_COST` and `WA_AI_PC_estimate_railway_cost` already use to *score* routes — so route selection and route payment finally agree. The removed `(1 + existing)` form also over-counted by one level versus the `base + per_level × existing` convention the air-base and radar branches use; moot at `per_level = 0`, but if `rail_way` ever regains a per-level cost, reinstate the correct form.
  - **Per-segment price by existing connection level:** 0 → **300 becomes 800 (2.67×)**, 1 → 430→800, 2 → 560→800, 3 → 690→800, 4 → 820→800, 5 → 950→800. A *rise* for levels 0–3 (where nearly all construction happens) and a small *cut* at 4+. At a full 20-factory slot (2.5 IC/factory/day) one fresh segment goes from **<1 week to ~2.3 weeks**.
  - **Why it lands here and not on a railway item: this item's own history says majors' PC queues are 90–100% type-13** — `02bd4445` 1944.6: GER **69/75** slots rail, SOV 41/43, JAP 63/64; `a8b29e09` 1946.7: GER 26/29, SOV 41/47. Under winner-takes-most only the top few draw factories, so the other ~65 rails sit starved and aging. Tripling the head-of-queue's duration lengthens the whole tail's wait.
  - **The regression to watch, stated plainly: mass rail cancellation by the week-25 sweep.** The synchronised stall counters this item has flagged for four campaigns sat at **GER 69 slots all at exactly 18 weeks, SOV 36 at 24, JAP 23 at 16** (`02bd4445` 1944.6). SOV at **24** is one week under `@AI_PC_STALL_CANCEL_WEEKS = 25`. If those counters reflect real aging, a 2.67× slower head-of-queue pushes them past 25 and the sweep starts cancelling railway segments in bulk, which the strategies then re-queue — the Fix 41 livelock in a new costume. If they are the queue-wide *reset* artefact this item already suspects, nothing happens. **The fix does not resolve which; the next campaign does, and it is now the highest-value reading in this item.** Mitigations if it bites, in order of preference and NOT shipped: exempt band-1000 projects from the sweep (they are starved by design, not by neglect); raise `@AI_PC_STALL_CANCEL_WEEKS`; cap queued type-13 depth via `@WA_AI_PC_railway_QUEUE_SKIP_THRESHOLD` (currently 12).
  - **What is NOT at risk: the Fix 41 overtake lane.** Its guarantee is time-based, not throughput-based — any project stalled ≥`@AI_PC_AGING_LANE_WEEKS` (12) is served `min(20, pool)` ahead of the sorted fill, and being served resets its stall counter so the next-most-starved takes its place. A slower rail at the head does not weaken that floor; lower-band projects (air bases at 300, refineries at 250, radar at 100) keep their roughly-1-week-in-N turn regardless. This is why R8's and R25's funding probes should be unaffected — if they *do* degrade, the lane is broken and that is a separate defect.
  - **Second-order, benign but visible:** `@WA_AI_PC_railway_QUEUE_SKIP_THRESHOLD = 12` skips route recalculation at 12+ queued type-13 projects. Slower completion means the queue sits at ≥12 far more of the time, so the railway strategies will recalculate routes less often — less churn (which Fix 41 wanted) but slower adaptation to a moving front. And total rail built per civ-month falls ~2.7×; the AI's supply network will visibly lag prior campaigns. **That is the intended consequence of no longer getting rail at 37% of price, not a regression** — do not "fix" it by discounting rail again.
- **Fix 73 probes (added 2026-08-14) — run all four; (2) is the one that decides whether the mitigation lever is needed.**
  1. **Build fingerprint / price is correct.** For any queued type-13 project, `wa_ai_pc_project_cost^<id>` must be **exactly 800**, on every country, at every level. Any 300 / 430 / 560 / 690 / 950 means the build predates Fix 73. Constant-valued and level-independent, so one queued rail anywhere settles it.
  2. **No mass rail cancellation.** Track per-country `wa_ai_pc_stall_weeks` maxima across the campaign as this item already does. **PASS = no country's type-13 slots cross 25 in bulk.** FAIL = a country's rail block sits at 25–26 at one sample and the queue is visibly shorter at the next, i.e. the sweep fired across the band. Cross-check `wa_tlm_pc_aging_reval_cancels`: a spike there is the *lane* cancelling on revalidation (legitimate), a rise in cancellations *without* one is the sweep.
  3. **Rails still complete (criterion 4 of this item, unchanged but now load-bearing).** Type-13 share of the queue should stay high and total `rail_way` levels should still grow campaign-long — just ~2.7× slower per civ-month. **Zero rail completions is a failure; slower rail completion is the fix.** Compare rail levels at matched dates against `02bd4445`, expecting roughly a third of the growth for a similar civ budget.
  4. **The lower bands did not degrade.** R8 probes 2–4 (`wa_ai_pc_air_factories_assigned` on ENG and USA) and R25's refinery liveness should read as they did on `02bd4445`. If they collapse, the overtake lane is not doing its job and that is the defect to chase, not the rail price.
- **Retirement (Fix 73 leg):** RETIREABLE on **2** consecutive campaigns meeting probes 1–3 with the lower bands intact. Probe 1 alone retires after 1 (it verifies a constant).
- **Fix 77 (added 2026-08-14, campaign `be18f9c7`) — the mass rail cancellation Fix 73's probe 2 predicted DID happen, and the sweep was not the cause. The queue is a sawtooth, and the skip gate holds itself shut.** GER's type-13 count ran **80 (1944.6) → 66 → 45 → 24 → 2 (1945.2)**. The diagnosis:
  - **The "80 slots all at stall 0" signature is not a stall reset — the variable does not exist.** `WA_AI_PC_start_project` never initialises `stall_weeks` / `assigned_factories` / `build_time`; they are written first by the weekly maintenance. Absent reads as 0 through `check_variable`, so **a freshly enqueued cohort is indistinguishable from a reset one unless you check for the variable's presence.** All 80 (province, connect) pairs at 1944.6 were **new** versus 1944.5, which held **zero** type-13. **This settles the four-campaign "synchronised stall counter" watch item: it is a cohort artefact, not a reset.** Stalls synchronise iff the projects were never touched — JAP 1946.7 is the control, its 13 *partially built* survivors carry fully desynchronised stalls (0,1,3,4,5,10,11,12,13,20,21,21,22).
  - **`wa_ai_pc_progress` is REMAINING cost, not work done.** `progress == project_cost == 800` means **zero work**, not complete. Verified by `build_time = progress / (assigned × 2.5)` holding exactly. 62 of 66 GER rails were in that state at 1944.8. Reading it the other way inverts the whole diagnosis — this bit an analysis pass in this session.
  - **The queue never builds most of what it queues.** Across five years and every sampled month, **1–7 of up to 90** queued type-13 projects hold factories at any moment (winner-takes-most gives the head ~20 civs; at 800 IC that is ~2.3 weeks per segment). The other ~75 exist only to accumulate stall until something deletes them.
  - **Two removal mechanisms, cleanly separable, and the sweep is the innocent one.** The intermediate attrition (34 projects, 1944.6→1945.1) kills *subsets* of projects sharing an identical stall value, tightly clustered by target state — impossible for a threshold sweep, and the signature of ground changing hands. The terminal collapse (20 projects, 1945.1→1945.2) *is* the sweep: all 20 at `stall_weeks = 30` die together across 9 unrelated states as they tick past `> 30`. **All 20 were on enemy-controlled ground** (SOV 11, FRA 5, YUG 4) — every one queued onto friendly ground at 1944.6, flipped by 1944.10, then **rotted in the queue for 4–6 months**. The sweep was the backstop working. The defect is the 4–6 month latency before anything noticed.
  - **Why nothing noticed: `@WA_AI_PC_railway_QUEUE_SKIP_THRESHOLD` counted DEAD projects as a full queue.** With ≥12 type-13 queued the railway entry point skips route recalculation *and* `WA_AI_PC_railway_validate_queued_projects` — so the dead cohort kept the very mechanism that would have cancelled it switched off, for the whole collapse. `wa_tlm_pc_aging_reval_cancels` froze at 64 from 1944.11 because the overtake lane only revalidates projects the allocator actually walks, and it stops when the pool empties — the starved tail is unreachable by that path too.
  - **The fix, two additive parts.** (a) `WA_AI_PC_start_railway_project` sets `_project_queue_max = @WA_AI_PC_railway_MAX_QUEUED` (12) instead of **0 = uncapped** — it was the only uncapped path into the PC queue. (b) The skip gate counts only **live** type-13 (target state friendly-controlled, the same Fix 34 controller test the allocator and completion path use). The two 12s are deliberately equal; change them together.
- **Fix 77 probes (added 2026-08-14) — run all four.**
  1. **No mass enqueue.** Per-country type-13 count across consecutive saves. **PASS = no country's type-13 count exceeds ~12–15 at any sample**, against `be18f9c7`'s GER 80 / JAP 85. A count above the cap on a v15+ build means the helper's `queue_max` is not being honoured — check no caller sets `_project_queue_max` after it.
  2. **The sawtooth flattens.** Track type-13 count month by month for GER and JAP. **PASS = the count oscillates in a narrow band and never hits 0 for months at a stretch.** Baseline to beat: GER dead at 0 for six months (1943.12–1944.5) and thirteen months (1945.7+, though that tail is a dead-tag artefact — see below); JAP +85 in one month at 1945.11.
  3. **Dead projects clear in weeks, not months.** At a save where a country has lost ground, cross-check queued type-13 target states against the `states={}` controllers. **PASS = few or no queued rails on enemy-controlled ground, and none older than ~1 interval (8 weeks at war).** Baseline: 20 GER rails sat on SOV/FRA/YUG ground for 4–6 months. `wa_tlm_pc_stale_n` (v14) should now carry these cancels; `wa_tlm_pc_sweep_n` should carry correspondingly fewer.
  4. **Rail still gets built — the regression this could cause.** Capping admission must not starve construction. Total world `rail_way` levels must still grow campaign-long (baseline `be18f9c7`: 7 647 → 10 539) and per-country type-13 completions (`wa_tlm_pc_built_by_type^13`, v14) must be non-zero for the majors. **A campaign where rail levels stop growing means the cap is too tight — raise both 12s together, do not revert the live-count gate.**
- **Retirement (Fix 77 leg):** RETIREABLE on **2** consecutive campaigns meeting probes 1–4. Uses only v14/v15 standing metrics, so nothing is deleted on retirement.
- **Dead-tag caveat for this item (2026-08-14):** an annihilated tag keeps its last PC block verbatim in every later save (GER from 1945.7: 0 states, no `units`, `assigned_factories_total` frozen at 14). Those are **not** live readings — check the tag still holds territory before scoring any stall or queue figure from a late save.
- **Probe:** extract `wa_ai_pc_queue`, `wa_ai_pc_stall_weeks`, `wa_ai_pc_assigned_factories`, `wa_ai_pc_assigned_factories_total`, `wa_ai_pc_target_state` for all majors plus 2–3 occupied/front-line minors (ROM is the reference case); cross-check target-state controllers in the save's `states={}` block. **Add `wa_ai_pc_project_cost` and `wa_ai_pc_building_type` to that extraction from 2026-08-14** — Fix 73 probe 1 needs them and they come from the same dump.
- **Threshold:** 3 (narrow — direct variable observation, no behavioural judgement).
- **RETIREMENT HELD at 3/3 (2026-08-12) — do not delete this item without resolving two probe defects first.** (a) **Criterion (4) has never been computable from a save.** Only `wa_tlm_pc_aging_reval_cancels` and `wa_tlm_pc_aging_grants` exist; there is no total-cancel counter, so `sweep-cancels = total − reval` cannot be evaluated — add the total counter or drop the arithmetic from the criterion. (b) **The synchronised stall-counter reset recurs for the third campaign and is now unambiguous:** at 1945.6, SOV shows **14 slots all at exactly 19**, JAP **17 slots all at exactly 23**, GER **9 slots all at exactly 16** (`9a4cd657` had SOV 13@13, JAP 27@14). That is a queue-wide reset, not per-project aging — which means criterion (1)'s "no funded project ages past 26 weeks" may be an artefact of the reset rather than the fix working, i.e. the mechanism under test may not be running at all. Per `../SKILL.md`, a caveat of that shape is the difference between PASSED and NOT CHECKED, so the streak stands but the item does not retire. (c) Separately, **criterion (2) is not discriminating as written** — it tests "is now hostile-controlled", not "was funded while hostile", so a capitulated country's frozen queue (FRA slot 6, funded 1940.7 while FRA still held state 15) trips it forever. Recut it to compare the funding date against the flip date, or exempt countries whose PC block is unchanged since capitulation.
- **Streak:** 1
