### R24. Industrial overextension brake fires and substitutes (Fix 39)

- **Fix under test:** `d729372f9`. Monthly hysteresis flag `WA_AI_industry_overextended_flag` (TRIP: ≥2 of steel/aluminium/rubber/tungsten/chromium at needs=3 with `resource@X < 0`, mils>civs, total factories>100; RELEASE: <2 at needs≥2); `WA_AI_queue_MIC` substitutes under the flag (synth refinery when tech+domestic coal+law≥war_economy, else CIC); research unlock via `WA_AI_RESEARCH_needs_synth_oil/_synth_rubber` OR-branches; new oil writer for `WA_AI_needs_oil` (oil prospecting weights now live — expected campaign-visible change); last two bare `<20` conversion gates floored.
- **Pass:** (1) ENG by ~1943.9: flag set, `wa_ai_overext_dbg_active > 0`, `wa_ai_overext_dbg_mic_redirects_cic` climbing (and `_refinery ≥ 1` given ENG's coal/tech/law); (2) behavioural: ENG active mil-line factory count plateaus ~380-400 instead of the `31eaf7e6` climb to 429, AND ENG UK-isles civilian IC finally moves off its 161 flatline; (3) negative controls: USA and SOV never carry the flag, `dbg_active` absent/0 all campaign; (4) oil writer live: `wa_ai_needs_oil > 0` on at least one oil-importing AI major by ~1941; (5) no country shows zero construction while flagged (the substitution guarantee).
- **Probe:** `var <TAG> "^wa_ai_(overext_dbg|needs_oil|industry_overextended)"` on ENG/USA/SOV/GER/JAP across war saves; ENG mil-line count via `production → military_lines` summed `active_factories`; UK-isles IC sum per the R23/economy method. Criteria (1)/(3)/(4)/(5) are narrow; (2) is behavioural corroboration — cite under F8 if it moves army-supply outcomes.
- **Threshold:** 3 (narrow variable probes; criterion 2 informs but does not gate retirement).
- **Streak:** 0
- **History:**
  - NOT YET TESTED — fix postdates every campaign in the registry.

(1) PARTIAL: ENG trips on schedule (flag first set **1943.7**, `active = 3` by 1943.9, ramping to 34 by 1946.4) — **but `_mic_redirects_cic` and `_mic_redirects_refinery` are ABSENT on ENG in all six saves.** Per `WA_AI_CONSTRUCTION_queue_functions.txt:114-158` the substitution is at the funnel and its CIC branch is an *unconditional* fallback, so one of the two counters increments on **every** call — both absent means `WA_AI_queue_MIC` was never invoked during 34 flagged months. The brake armed; nothing ever asked it to substitute. (3) **FAIL — the negative controls do not hold.** USA carries the flag from ~1942.11, GER from 1943.9, JAP from ~1942.4, SOV from ~1944.11: the brake fires on essentially every major, not on the overextended outlier. USA is the *only* country with redirect counters (`cic` 49→64, `refinery` 1) and they freeze after 1943.12. (4) PASS: `wa_ai_needs_oil = 3` on ENG from 1940.6 — the oil writer is live. Notable that GER/ITA/JAP all read 0 throughout, which is odd for oil-importing majors and worth its own check. (5) Not violated — no country showed zero construction while flagged. **Two diagnoses needed before a re-score: why ENG never routes through `WA_AI_queue_MIC`, and why the TRIP condition catches every major.** Do not re-tune thresholds until the first is answered.

Save evidence: ENG's `WA_AI_construction_timer` set-dates run 1938.5.2 → 1940.3.7 → 1941.6.6 → 1943.1.31 and the flag is then **absent at 1944.6, 1945.6 and 1946.4** — the timer gate is wide open and the event still never fired. GER shows the same silence; USA is the only country that ever recorded redirects. **This is the same unfloored-gate failure Fix 40 and Fix 41 patched *inside* the PC system (`WA_AI_PC_has_project_civs_16/_20`, `WA_AI_PC_can_afford_project`) — still unpatched on the on_action.** Smallest lever: floor `num_of_available_civilian_factories > 1` at `WA_AI_misc_on_actions.txt:70` the way Fix 40 floored the PC gates. (Marked INFERRED-by-elimination in the source audit: the variable itself is not stored in saves, but the other two clauses are demonstrably satisfied.)
  - 2026-08-11 · dedicated brake audit — **independently reproduced the finding above and added the decisive arithmetic. Verdict: (c) both, plus a third reason that matters more than either.** (i) **Unreachable.** ENG's only recurring `queue_MIC` paths are C.10's ENG branch (2–7 calls/firing) and C.10's GENERIC `else_if` (`events/WA_AI_construction.txt:1479-1502`, no `limit`, **8 calls/firing for every non-CHI AI**). Any single C.10 firing while flagged would have produced ≥8 increments; the counter is absent ⇒ **C.10 fired exactly 0 times for ENG across all 34 flagged months.** `WA_AI_queue_MIC_2` has **zero live callers** (definition + comment only — not a bypass), and of the 142 direct `WA_AI_add_MIC` sites, the split is CHI 49 / SOV 28 / USA 65 and **ENG has none**. The repo already documented this engine behaviour when shipping Fix 40 — `PRIORITY_strategies.txt:309-311`: *"the bare engine variable reads ~0 once the vanilla queue saturates"* — and shipped `WA_AI_PC_has_project_civs_20` as the workaround; the C.10 on-action gate was never converted, and `WA_AI_C.100` (`:3901-3933`) is dead for the same reason. (ii) **Trip condition too broad — it degenerates to "major, at war, importing".** `WA_AI_misc_effects.txt:670-720` TRIPs on ≥2 of five resources at `WA_AI_needs_X = 3` with `resource@X < 0`, but `WA_AI_check_resource_needs` (`:482-546`) increments each need on `OR = { resource@X < -threshold, resource_imported@X > threshold }` — **the second arm makes it an import-VOLUME signal, not a scarcity signal**, so any large trade economy pins all five at 3 within days. There is no magnitude or fraction-of-consumption test (contrast `WA_AI_iron_resource_shortage` at `:460-478`, which does use `deficit/needed > 0.05`), and RELEASE keys on the same import-driven variable — **so it is a one-way latch, not hysteresis**. Matches the saves exactly: ENG 34/34 months, USA 40, GER/JAP/SOV all latched. (iii) **Wrong point in the causal chain.** The brake gates *queue insertion*, but ENG's MICs were bulk-ordered by `fire_only_once` events C.60–C.64 (`:2088, :2154, :2189, :2272, :2448` — **220 `queue_MIC` calls between them**, all fired 1937–1943.1) and executed over the following three years. ENG's growth decays exactly like a draining pipeline: **+46, +45, +17, +5**. Every MIC in the +113 was ordered before ENG tripped the flag in 1943.7 — a perfectly-wired insertion-time brake would still have caught nothing. (Focus tree adds 71 arms-factory levels via `add_building_construction`, also mostly pre-1943.) **USA's freeze has an exact fingerprint confirming all of this:** its `_mic_redirects_cic` went 49 → 64 = **exactly +15**, and `WA_AI_C.78` (`:3532-3642`) is `fire_only_once`, `tag = USA`, `date > 1943.6.1`, contains **exactly 15 `WA_AI_queue_MIC`**, and sets the 300-day timer — USA's timer stamp at 1943.12 is 1943.6.2. USA only ever got redirects because it happened to have one MIC-bearing one-shot scheduled *after* its trip date; ENG's five were all scheduled before. **Two caveats before fixing:** un-gating C.10 restarts the GENERIC 8×`queue_MIC` block for *every* non-CHI AI late-war, so it should be paired with narrowing the trip condition; and it cannot retroactively help an already-committed MIC pipeline — for that the existing lever is `WA_AI_priority_convert_MIC_to_CIC` (`queue_functions.txt:642-665`, currently reached only from three narrow strategies), which runs on the weekly PC cadence and is unaffected by the dead C.10 gate.

### R28. The AI trade-law ladder has only two reachable rungs (fix pending)

- **Status: DIAGNOSED, NOT FIXED.** Added 2026-08-11 from `911bed3c`. No fix commit yet.
- **Defect 1 — `WA_AI_can_take_limited_exports` is unsatisfiable for every country, at every date.** Three multi-child `NOT` blocks sit as direct top-level children of the trigger (`common/scripted_triggers/WA_AI_LAW_triggers.txt:1953-1967`), with no `AND` or `if/limit` wrapper. HOI4 evaluates a multi-child `NOT` as **NOR**, so `:1953` requires `is_major AND NOT in_faction AND NOT has_war` while `:1959` requires `is_major AND has_war` — **`has_war` must be simultaneously true and false.** Nothing about tag, date or world state escapes it.
- **Defect 2 — the ladder is short at both ends.** `WA_AI_can_take_closed_economy` is gated `original_tag = USA|GER` (`:2061-2064`). With `limited_exports` dead, the AI-reachable set worldwide is **{`free_trade`, `export_focus`}**, plus `closed_economy` for USA and GER only. `WA_AI_upgrade_trade_law` (`WA_AI_law_effects.txt:140-189`) has three live branches out of four. It is also a **one-way ratchet**: a country that starts on `limited_exports` and downgrades can never return.
- **Empirical proof (this is what makes it certain, not a reading of semantics):** across 21 countries × the full 1936.2–1946.4 campaign, **zero AI-path grants of `limited_exports`**. Every holder is accounted for outside `WA_AI_upgrade_trade_law` — GER and ITA from game start (`history/countries/GER - Germany.txt:291`), CHI from the `CHI_war_of_resistance` focus reward (`common/national_focus/china_nationalist.txt:7071`), SPR from the autarky focus (`common/national_focus/spain.txt:7477-7479`), GRE from game start and **lost by 1938.6, never regained through six more war years**. Meanwhile branches 1 and 2 demonstrably fire — FIN, AST, RAJ, SIA, POL, HUN and USA all flip between `free_trade` and `export_focus` mid-campaign. Only branch 3 never produces a grant.
- **Correction to the first framing — ENG is NOT pinned by the NOR bug.** Clause `:1964-1967` (`NOT = { has_government = democratic  tag = ENG }`) excludes ENG under *both* readings, since ENG is democratic in all six samples. And `WA_AI_can_take_free_trade` requires `NOT = { WA_AI_major_country = yes }` unless `uk_evacuated` (`:1662-1668`), while closed_economy needs USA/GER. **So `export_focus` is ENG's only reachable law even with the NOR bug fixed** — that is the 123-month flatline. Fixing the contradiction unblocks most of the AI world; it does **not** move ENG. Two separate fixes are needed.
- **Refuted, do not re-investigate:** the theory that `WA_AI_can_take_export_focus` lacks a `NOT = { has_idea = export_focus }` self-guard and terminates the `else_if` chain. All four triggers carry their self-guard (`:1695, :1872, :1994, :2068`) and the chain advances normally.
- **Checked and clean:** `WA_AI_can_take_free_trade` (`:1615-1741`) and `WA_AI_can_take_closed_economy` (`:2058-2113`) use only single-child `NOT`s. `WA_AI_can_take_export_focus` has two multi-child `NOT`s (`:1880-1884` inside a TUR-only `if`, consistent with the complementary free-trade rule; `:1815-1836` inside an `else_if` **limit**, i.e. a branch selector, satisfiable but very narrow) — neither is a dead gate.
- **Pass:** (1) at least one AI country acquires `limited_exports` through `WA_AI_upgrade_trade_law` during a campaign (probe the log line at `WA_AI_law_effects.txt:177`, or observe a tag holding it that neither started with it nor took the CHI/SPR focus); (2) a country whose shortage counters pin at 3 on ≥2 resources demonstrably steps DOWN a rung within ~12 months; (3) ENG specifically escapes `export_focus` once it has ≥2 pinned shortages (this needs the separate ENG-clause fix, so score it separately); (4) negative guard — no country ratchets to `closed_economy` while it still has functioning trade partners and no blockade.
- **Probe:** `ideas <TAG> <saves> --match "free_trade|export_focus|limited_exports|closed_economy"` across 1936.6/1938.6/1940.6/1942.6/1944.6 and campaign end, for all majors plus ~6 minors; cross-check any `limited_exports` holder against `history/countries/` and the focus grants above before crediting it to the AI path.
- **Threshold:** 3 (narrow — idea presence across dates).
- **Streak:** 0
- **History:**
  - 2026-08-11 · `911bed3c` · **FAILED — zero AI-path grants in 21 countries over 10 game-years** (table and provenance above). Empirically confirms the NOR contradiction; ENG's own lock is a separate clause.
  - 2026-08-12 · `9a4cd657` · FAILED — **zero AI-path grants of `limited_exports` in ~174 country blocks across six dates.** The 1936.6 holder set (43 tags — GER ITA GRE ISS FSR RIT RUK plus 36 `R??` placeholders) only ever *shrinks*, to 31; the only two additions in ten game-years are **CHI by 1938.6** (`CHI_war_of_resistance` focus) and **SPR by 1940.6** (autarky focus), both excluded by this item's own caveat. GRE loses it by 1938.6 and never regains it. Branches 1 and 2 demonstrably fire (USA free_trade→export_focus between 1940.6 and 1942.6, plus FIN/HUN/AST/RAJ/SAF/TUR/POL/CZE/CRO flips), so the ladder is alive and the middle rung is simply unreachable. ENG is `export_focus` at all six dates while pinned 3/3/3 on aluminium/rubber/steel for 1942.6–1945.6 — criteria 2 and 3 both fail. Criterion 4 passes: `closed_economy` only ever on JAP (blockaded) plus PRC at 1936.6. **New:** SOV holds **none** of the four trade-law ideas at any sampled date — either a genuine gap or a modded law outside the probe set; resolve which before the next scoring.
  - 2026-08-12 · `a8b29e09` · FAILED — **zero AI-path grants of `limited_exports` across 409 country blocks × six dates.** The holder set only ever shrinks: **43** (1936.6) → 42 → 41 → 34 → 32 → **32** (1946.7); the only two additions in ten game-years are CHI (by 1938.6) and SPR (by 1940.6), both focus rewards and both excluded by the item's own caveat. GRE and PRU lose it by 1938.6 and never regain it. Branches 1/2 are alive — `free_trade` 108→141 and `export_focus` 253→232 churn all campaign — so the ladder runs and **only the middle rung is unreachable**. (2)/(3) FAIL: ENG is `export_focus` at all six dates while pinned 3/3/3 on aluminium/rubber/steel for 1943.6–1945.6 — no step down, no escape. (4) PASS: `closed_economy` is only ever JAP (blockaded, all six dates) plus PRC at 1936.6. **Last campaign's open SOV question is resolved: SOV holds no trade-law idea at any date** — its economy ideas are `planned_economy` + `economy_fatigue_N` only. Genuine gap, not a probe blind spot.
  - 2026-08-13 · `02bd4445` · **FAILED** — **zero AI-path grants of `limited_exports` across 409 country blocks × six dates**, and the holder set only shrinks: **43 (1936.6) → 42 → 39 → 32 → 31 → 30 (1946.1)**. The only additions in ten game-years are CHI (by 1938.6, focus; lost again by 1940.6) and SPR (by 1942.6, autarky focus), both excluded by this item's own caveat. Branches 1/2 alive (`free_trade` 108→142, `export_focus` 253→232, USA flips free_trade→export_focus between 1940.6 and 1942.6). (2)/(3) **FAIL** — ENG is `export_focus` at all six dates while pinned 3/3/3 on aluminium/rubber/steel from 1942.6 to 1946.1. (4) PASS (`closed_economy` only JAP + CHI, both blockaded). SOV holds no trade-law idea at any date, as on `a8b29e09`.
  - 2026-08-14 · `be18f9c7` · FAILED — Zero AI-path grants of `limited_exports`, unchanged. Every holder is accounted for outside `WA_AI_upgrade_trade_law`: GER and ITA from game start, **CHI by 1938.6** (`CHI_war_of_resistance`), **SPR by 1942.6** (autarky focus); **GRE holds it at 1936.6, loses it by 1938.6 and never regains it** through eight more years. Branches 1/2 are alive (USA free_trade->export_focus between 1940.6 and 1942.6, TUR export_focus->free_trade, POL/SPR flips), so only the middle rung is unreachable. (2)/(3) FAIL - ENG is `export_focus` at all six dates 1936.6->1946.7 while `wa_ai_needs_bauxite = 3` from 1944.6. (4) PASS (`closed_economy` only ever JAP, blockaded). SOV holds **no** trade-law idea at any date, as on `a8b29e09`/`02bd4445`.
  - 2026-08-15 · `af003548` · FAILED — zero AI-path `limited_exports` grants (12 tags × 6 dates); ENG `export_focus` at all six dates while `needs_rubber = needs_bauxite = 3` from 1942.6 (criteria 2/3 FAIL); branches 1/2 alive (USA free_trade → export_focus 1942.6); (4) PASS (`closed_economy` only JAP). SOV holds no trade-law idea — 5th campaign.

### R29. Refinery activation: self-concealing shutdown, deadlocked setpoints, dead counters (fix pending)

- **Architecture review: `documentation/WA_REFINERY_CONTROLLER_REVIEW.md` (2026-08-11).** Read it before proposing any fix here. Headline: **WA runs a two-tier resource economy the engine cannot express, and this controller is its enforcement layer.** Equipment declares steel 2630× / tungsten 1702× / chromium 1291× / aluminium 1115× / coal 1075× / rubber 831×, but **iron and bauxite exactly once each** — the `trade_fix_equipment_0` dummy. Iron and bauxite are consumed *only* by refineries, so the engine's `PRODUCTION_RESOURCE_LACK_PENALTY` can never bind on them; without the scripted on/off, refineries would produce steel and aluminium regardless of ore and the raw tier would be decorative. **The mechanic is therefore load-bearing for balance, not flavour.** Consequence for this item: ENG's 1944 shutdown was **correct** behaviour — it genuinely lacked bauxite. The defects are all downstream, in the recovery arm (defects C, G) and the masked demand signal (defects A, B). *An earlier draft of the review argued the opposite and proposed retiring the iron/bauxite arms; that option is withdrawn and recorded as withdrawn so the same grep does not lead someone back to it.*

- **Status: DIAGNOSED, NOT FIXED.** Added 2026-08-11 from `911bed3c` after a user in-game observation. Several distinct defects in one system; they need separating before any fix.
- **⚠ OPERATIONAL WARNING — the Refresh button damages AI-country saves.** `force_refresh_button_click` (`common/scripted_guis/refineries_window_scripted_gui.txt:38-48`) runs `recalculate_*` **and then `manage_*`** in the same click, and `manage_*` contains an unconditional "close refineries down to the player target" loop (`WA_scripted_effects.txt:7401-7498` aluminium, `:6808+` steel). For an AI country the target is frozen at its 1936 value (see defect D), so the click **switches off every refinery above that stale target**. Observed: one click on ENG at 1944.4 closed ~39 steel and ~13 aluminium refineries. The auto-adopt guard that should prevent it (`:7159-7167`) requires `open_X_target = previous_open_X_target`, but `previous_open_X_target` is only written at the *end* of `manage_*` (`:7499`), so it is absent (=0) on first use and the guard can only pass when the target is also 0 — which is why the synthetic row (target 0) adopted correctly to 3 while steel and aluminium were culled. **Never press Refresh on an AI country in a save that matters.**
- **Defect A — deactivation is self-concealing (the user's hypothesis, CONFIRMED by construction).** The `*_inactive` building twins (`common/buildings/00_buildings.txt:391/405/419/433/447`) carry **no `local_resources_*` and no `country_modifiers`** — zero production *and* zero consumption. So when a refinery switches off, both legs of `WA_AI_check_resource_needs` (`WA_AI_misc_effects.txt:582-594`) move **down**: `resource@bauxite` rises toward 0 (each closure returns 32, or 35 hydro), and `resource_imported@bauxite` falls because the engine buys less ore. The shortage hides itself.
- **Defect B — the ratio detector reads zero while the industry is shutting down (measured).** `WA_AI_check_resource_shortages` (`WA_AI_misc_effects.txt:428-450`) requires deficit > 5% of total need. ENG at 1944.4: `resource_consumed@bauxite` = 12×32 + 3×35 = 489, `resource@bauxite` = −14.2 ⇒ ratio 0.028 < 0.05 ⇒ **`wa_ai_bauxite_resource_shortage` = 0**, confirmed against the live save value, while `wa_ai_needs_bauxite` simultaneously reads 3. Two qualifications: ENG's `needs_bauxite` survives only via the *import* leg (imports 95–448, always > 25) — a country with domestic ore and idle refineries would read 0 on both; and `WA_AI_bauxite/coal/iron_resource_shortage` are **write-only, no reader anywhere in the repo** (only aluminium/rubber/tungsten/steel/chromium are read, at `WA_AI_LAW_triggers.txt:1851-1855/1982-1986`). The masked signal is also an unused one.
- **Defect C — REFRAMED 2026-08-11 (user correction; a first fix attempt was written on a wrong model and reverted).** The engine's trade AI imports only to bring `resource@X` to **~0 at the current instant** — it never anticipates demand or accumulates a reserve. So the break-even gates in `reactivate_*_refineries_ai` are not sloppy, they are **the only reachable bar**, and the design is deliberately speculative: switch a mill on, let it open a deficit, let the trade AI import to cover. Any "verify you can afford it first" gate (e.g. `resource@bauxite > 32 + margin`) is unreachable by construction and would freeze every AI refinery off permanently — strictly worse than the bug. **The defect is the RACE, not the threshold:** enabling a mill drops the balance by 32 instantly, while the trade response needs time; `bauxite_shortage_ai` activates at `< -26` on a 9-day timeout and closes it first. The 26-restarts-vs-30-shutdowns-with-a-flat-inactive-count signature is **flapping, not deadlock**. A fix must protect the transient the restart creates (grace period after enabling, or a cancel condition the trade AI can actually reach), not raise the entry bar. *(Retained below for the arithmetic, which is independently valid.)*
- **Defect C — REFRAMED AGAIN and FIXED 2026-08-11 (Fix 44).** The maintainer clarified the functional need: **the deficit is a bid.** Iron and bauxite have no direct engine consumer, so the AI only imports them once the balance is deep enough to register; the controller deliberately opens a refinery to create that depth and waits to see whether the market answers (`documentation/WA_REFINERY_CONTROLLER_REVIEW.md` §1). **The open/close cycling is therefore the mechanism, not churn** — a country that cycles is one whose bids are refused, and damping it would remove the only thing that triggers an import. Both my earlier proposals (an affordability gate, then a band controller) are withdrawn for that reason.
  **The actual defect, and the campaign convicts it:** the reopen gate `resource@bauxite > -1` demanded the deficit be *erased* before reopening, so a country could never hold more than **one** quantum of deficit — while bidding needs **two**, the standing one plus the bid. ENG therefore never bid: 19 monthly snapshots all read −26 to −33 with `produced` **exactly −137** every time (identical park), where an open bid should have shown in ~7 of them (p ≈ 3×10⁻⁴ of missing it by chance). What actually ran was: timeout closes the mill → +32 → the `> -1` gate is finally satisfied → the *same* mill reopens within hours → −30. An oscillation that never leaves the 0-to-32 band.
  **Fix 44:** reopen gates become `resource@iron > -25` / `resource@bauxite > -32` (bid room, sized to the trade quantum — `00_resources.txt` `cic` gives 25 iron / 32 bauxite per civilian factory, and one refinery consumes exactly the same, by design per `29f2c1d59`). The bauxite mission's thresholds were re-derived from its own quantum (arm `< -33`, cancel `> -32`); they had been carrying **iron's** −26/−25, which are correct for a quantum of 25 — which is precisely why steel never showed the pathology. Bonus: the same re-derivation fixes a double-close (at −62 the loop now credits +32 → −30, exits, closes **one** mill; under −26 it iterated and closed **two**). Coal untouched — different controller, clear-the-deficit (§1.5).
  **Consequence for defects A/B:** the self-concealing signal is very likely a *symptom*, not an independent defect — the deficit never deepened in the first place. Re-score after a campaign on this build before designing anything for it.
- **Defect C-bis — bauxite closure credits, FIXED 2026-08-11 (Fix 42).** The shutdown loops credited **25 bauxite / 15 coal** per closed `aluminium_refinery`, which actually frees **32 / 20** (`common/buildings/00_buildings.txt`). Under-crediting means the loop keeps closing past the point where the deficit is already covered — systematic over-closure. Corrected to 32/20 in **both** copies: `bauxite_shortage_ai` and the player-facing `bauxite_shortage` (same bug in both; fixing one copy is the mistake the NOR/`surrender_progress` lessons already record). The iron missions (25/20) and coal missions (60) were already correct — bauxite was the outlier. **Independent of Defect C proper**: this is arithmetic, not control design, and it stands regardless of how the restart race is eventually fixed.
- **Duplication hazard documented at source (2026-08-11).** PDXScript cannot read a building's `country_resource_cost_*` back, so every controller hard-codes its own copy. A header block at the top of the refinery definitions in `common/buildings/00_buildings.txt` now lists **every** site that copies these values, plus a one-line reminder above each of the five cost blocks. **Corrected 2026-08-11 after reading the code rather than scanning it — three of the four mismatches first listed here do not exist.** `manage_steel_mills` is **correct on both sides**: it pre-charges the PLAIN cost each iteration and patches the variant inside the branch that knows which one it picked (−15 coal when closing a hydro ⇒ its true 5; −15 more coal when opening a plain ⇒ its true 20). That works because `hydro_steel_refinery` differs from `steel_refinery` **only** in coal, by exactly 15. `manage_alu_mills` copies the scheme, but it does not fit aluminium — hydro differs in **both** bauxite (32→35) **and** coal (20→0), so a single 15-coal patch cannot express it. **The three real mismatches in `manage_alu_mills` are now FIXED (Fix 42).** They all OVER-stated the resource moved, so that loop exited early and closed/opened FEWER mills — the opposite direction to the mission bug, in the same system. Corrected to charge plain and patch hydro on **both** axes: the closing loop pre-charges bauxite 32 / coal 20 (was bauxite 35, hydro's cost, on a path whose first branch closes a 32-cost mill) and its two hydro branches now add +3 bauxite and take back all 20 coal (was −15, netting +5 where hydro frees none); the opening loop keeps its bauxite-35 pre-debit and its two plain branches give back the 3 they were over-charged. `manage_steel_mills` was verified **correct on all four paths** and left alone. **Root cause worth remembering:** the steel routine's "pre-charge one variant, patch the delta" idiom is valid only because `hydro_steel` differs from `steel` on one axis by exactly 15; aluminium's hydro differs on two axes, so the copied one-term patch could never be right — see the lessons-log entry.
- **Still outstanding on this path:** the Refresh button's destructive close-to-stale-target loop (see the operational warning at the top of this item). The arithmetic is now exact; the loop it feeds is still wrong.
- **Defect C (original framing) — the shutdown and restart setpoints do not overlap.** `bauxite_shortage_ai` (`_economy_fatigue.txt:429-489`) closes refineries until `resource@bauxite ≥ −26`; `reactivate_aluminium_refineries_ai` (`:2466-2508`) requires `resource@bauxite > −1`. The band **[−26, −1] is a structural trap**: once anything switches off, nothing switches it back on until an external event pushes the balance non-negative. Measured futility: between 1944.4 and 1946.4 the shutdown mission fires **30 more times** and the reactivation decision **26 more times** while ENG's inactive count sits at 12 and never falls — the two controllers fight across transient windows and shutdown wins every round. Compounding: the mission credits **25** bauxite per closure (`:475`), `manage_alu_mills` uses **35** (`WA_scripted_effects.txt:7193`), the building actually frees **32** (`00_buildings.txt:288`) — the mission under-credits, so it **over-closes**. And the shutdown loop never touches `hydro_aluminium_refinery` (`:481-486`), confirmed in-save (ENG's 3 hydro levels untouched while non-hydro went 12→1).
- **Defect D — the refinery counters are frozen at their 1936 values for every AI country.** `recalculate_*` runs only under `is_ai = no` (`100_wa_on_actions.txt:3396-3411`) or on the Refresh button. ENG reads `current_steel_mills/total_steel_mill/open_steel_mill_target` = **31/31/31 identically at 1936.2, 1939.6 and 1946.4** while real steel levels went 31 → 70; aluminium reads 2/2/2 against a real 2 → 16; synthetic 0/0/0 against 0 → 8. Same for USA (12 vs 52 real) and CAN (4 vs 82 real) — **global, not ENG corruption**. `previous_alu_mills_core`, `current_bauxite`, `need_bauxite`, `current_iron`, `need_iron`, `current_coal`, `need_coal` are **absent from every save**: `manage_*` and `calculate_resource_deficite` have never executed for an AI country. Note the activation *decision* is NOT stale (missions read `resource@X` live) — only the counters are.
- **Defect E — eight reactivation decisions are unreachable by anyone.** `reactivate_aluminium_refineries{,_occupied}` (`:1585/1662`), `reactivate_all_aluminium_refineries` (`:1167`), `reactivate_steel_refineries{,_occupied}` (`:1006/1083`), `reactivate_synthetic_refineries{,_occupied}` (`:2229/2273`) are `visible = { is_ai = yes … }` but their `available` contains `hidden_trigger = { … is_ai = no }` — an AND that can never hold. `reactivate_steel_refineries:1026-1028` even has `is_ai = no` and `is_ai = yes` in the same `visible` block.
- **Defect G — the shortage missions cannot close a hydro refinery, and that is what makes the state unrecoverable (verified 2026-08-11).** All four shortage loops close via `random_controlled_state = { limit = { <plain>_refinery > 0 } disable_<x>_mill = yes }` (`_economy_fatigue.txt:357-362, 491-496`, player twins `:294-299, :424-429`) — plain variant only — while all four `reactivate_*_ai` decisions **prefer hydro** (`:2406-2468, :2537-2599`). **Correct statement of the defect** (an earlier framing of "unbounded one-way loop" is REFUTED, and "no AI path can close a hydro" is too strong): hydro *can* be closed by `on_state_control_changed` (`100_wa_on_actions.txt:2493-2530`, which closes everything in a newly-controlled non-core state — the sole origin of every hydro-inactive level in the campaign) and by the `close_*` surplus decisions, but those need `resource@X > 24/30`, so **no AI path can close a hydro refinery while the country is in an input deficit** — exactly when it needs to.
  - **What actually happens, both readings resolved.** *(A) Substitution* — mechanically real but bounded and empirically absent: the entry window is only `bauxite ∈ [0, 8]` (reopen costs 35, gate needs ≥ 0, mission needs ≤ −27), it self-limits after ~3 swaps into the `[−26, −1]` trap band, and each swap nets bauxite −3 / coal +20 / aluminium **exactly 0**. Direction is mildly *good* (coal is the broader constraint), so it is a futile-action bug, not a drain — and no substitution signature appears anywhere in the campaign (hydro is purely additive: GER plain steel 20→108 alongside hydro 0→2; CAN plain alu 30→67 alongside hydro 0→30). *(B) Impotent mission* — **correct, and it is the terminal state.**
  - **Observed on ENG, 1944.7 → 1946.4 (the strongest single finding in this item).** ENG's active plain aluminium goes 11 → 0/1 with 12 levels inactive, while its **3 hydro aluminium levels stay active throughout, consuming 105 bauxite**, and the balance sits at −14 → −31 — inside the mission's activation band. So for ~21 game-months `bauxite_shortage_ai` re-armed every 9 days, advanced its temp counter (the unconditional pre-charge that guarantees termination), matched nothing with its `limit`, and closed nothing. **A single hydro closure would have freed 35 bauxite and cleared the deficit outright.** The AI had the answer in its own park and no way to reach it.
  - **Steel is immune, for an arithmetic reason — do NOT "fix" its ordering.** `reactivate_steel_refineries_ai` needs `iron > -1`; a hydro steel costs exactly 25, so the post-reopen balance is ≥ −25 and `iron_shortage_ai` activates at ≤ −27 — **the reopen can never reach the activation band**. Plain-first is also weakly dominant for steel relief (same 25 iron, 20 coal vs 5). Only the no-op case applies there.
  - **Cost correction:** `hydro_steel_refinery` costs coal **5**, not zero — only `hydro_aluminium_refinery` is coal-free (bauxite 35 / coal 0). That 5 is precisely why the two families behave differently.
  - **FIXED 2026-08-11 (Fix 43).** All four shortage loops now close **both variants**: plain first (unchanged preference), hydro as an `else_if` fallback. Steel patches −15 coal (pre-charge books the plain 25/20, hydro's true cost is 25/5). Aluminium patches +3 bauxite and takes back all 20 coal (pre-charge books the plain 32/20, hydro's true cost is 35/0). The `close_*` surplus decisions and `manage_*` are untouched. **Termination guard shipped with it:** because the aluminium hydro branch reclaims the full 20 coal, a coal-driven pass holding only hydro mills would leave `tempCoal` static and the `while_loop_effect` would never end — so the hydro branch is gated on a leg flag (`_wa_shortage_bauxite_leg_`) captured **before** the pre-charge moves the counters, using each loop's own threshold (player `< 0`, AI `< -33` — *corrected 2026-08-11: this line read `< -26`, the pre-Fix-44 value; the code was re-derived to `-33` in the same commit and the two do match*). That gate is also correct on the merits: a hydro closure frees no coal, so it cannot help a coal shortage. Steel needs no guard — its hydro closure nets +5 coal, so the counter still advances. Verified: brace balance 0, both guards match their enclosing loop's threshold.
  - **Original analysis of the fix, retained for the reasoning:** Add a hydro `else_if` fallback to the four loops, per the Fix 42 per-axis idiom (aluminium: +3 bauxite, −20 coal, then `disable_hydro_aluminium_mill`; steel: −15 coal, iron already right). **Hazard:** the unconditional pre-charge IS the loop's termination guarantee, so if the aluminium hydro branch takes all 20 coal back while the loop is running on its *coal* leg (`tempCoal < -61`) with only hydro mills present, `tempCoal` stops advancing and the `while_loop_effect` never ends. Gate the hydro branch to fire only while `tempBauxite < -26`. Second decision to make deliberately: closing a hydro buys 35 bauxite for −15 aluminium output — right for ENG, wrong for a country already aluminium-short. Third: the player twins have the identical hole and should be patched together.
- **Incidental — dead code found in the same sweep:** `close_steel_mill_occupied_all` (`_economy_fatigue.txt:1934-1999`) and `close_aluminium_smelter_occupied_all` (`:2176-2237`) have `ai_will_do = { base = 0 }`, no modifiers, and no `activate_decision` caller anywhere in the repo.
- **Defect F — every recovery door is shut by the same test.** With `resource@bauxite` negative, ENG is simultaneously blocked from: `reactivate_aluminium_refineries_ai` (`> −1`), `WA_AI_build_refinery_resource_shortage_aluminium` (Fix 40's `resource@bauxite > −1` AND `resource_imported@bauxite < 40`, `PRIORITY_strategies.txt:390-391` — and ENG can never satisfy the second, its gross domestic bauxite is ≈0), and `WA_AI_resource_extraction`, whose aluminium and steel arms are **commented out** (`:147`, `:150`). The one lever for "buy ore, not metal" — the `trade_fix_equipment_0` dummy-equipment trick (`ship_hull_heavy.txt:694-725`) — is gated on `resource_imported@bauxite < 1` and ENG imports 319.8, so it never fires.
- **What actually happened to ENG (corrects the "shutdown destroyed the deficit" reading):** the balance did **not** recover as refineries closed — it worsened, −14 → −28 → −31, because **bauxite imports collapsed faster (448 → 320 → 192 → 95) than shutdown could compensate**. Chain: import supply collapses → balance negative → mission closes refineries → aluminium output 326 → 79 → aluminium deficit −207 → −477 → balance now permanently below the restart gate. Deactivation is self-concealing in general (Defect A), but in *this* campaign the visible deficit was destroyed by the import collapse, not by the shutdown.
- **The exporters are blind too (1944.4):** USA has bauxite `produced +19,956` with **9,978 flagged `to_export` but only 409 actually exported**; CAN has 759 `to_export` and **exports zero bauxite** while exporting 1,487 aluminium. Both read `wa_ai_needs_bauxite = 0`. ENG itself still **exports 163 aluminium** while running a −207 aluminium deficit. Neither USA nor CAN has a single inactive refinery level — the shutdown pathology is ENG-only among the three.
- **Premise correction for future readers:** the Trade-screen REFINERIES panel rows are **steel, aluminium, synthetic** top-to-bottom (`interface/refinery.gui:205/370/535`), not aluminium-first. The "70" seen on row 1 is 70 *steel* refineries, all active; ENG's aluminium park at 1944.4 was 12 active + 1 inactive + 3 hydro.
- **Instrumentation gap — total (PROVEN by absence).** `*_refinery_inactive` appears in **zero** files under `common/scripted_triggers/`, and under `common/scripted_effects/` only in the player-only recalculate path and a debug tool. **No `WA_AI_*` trigger or effect reads it anywhere.** No `WA_TLM_*` metric touches refineries. The only country-level expression of "N of my refineries are off" would be `total_X_mill − current_X_mills`, and for AI countries both operands are frozen (Defect D). The only way to see it is to sum `*_refinery_inactive` levels out of the `states` block. **Instrumentation is a prerequisite for the fix, not a follow-up.**
  - **Half-closed 2026-08-11 — be precise about which half.** `savegame.py buildings` now sums the `*_inactive` twins per country, so the **analyst** is no longer blind: criterion (1)'s "and `savegame.py` can read it" is satisfied and (2) is now cheaply scoreable on any campaign. The **AI** is still blind — no trigger, effect or `WA_TLM_` metric anywhere in the mod reads `*_refinery_inactive`, so criteria (4) and (5) are untouched and nothing in the game can react to its own switched-off park. Closing that half means a new `WA_TLM_` metric plus a demand signal computed from *intended* capacity rather than realised consumption, which is a game-behaviour change with campaign impact — **not shipped, awaiting a user decision.** A read-only `WA_TLM_` gauge would not fix the AI on its own (telemetry is write-only by contract, `WA_TLM_TELEMETRY_SYSTEM.md` §3.1); it would only make the failure visible in-save without a `buildings` sweep.
- **Pass — Fix 44 sub-probe, scoreable with NO new instrumentation (add 2026-08-11):** the falsifiable test is *does the country ever place a bid*. On ENG (or any import-dependent major) across consecutive monthly saves 1942→end: (a) **`produced` for bauxite must CHANGE between consecutive months** — it encodes the park configuration (1 plain + 3 hydro = −137 in `911bed3c`, identical at all 19 snapshots, which is what proved no bid was ever held); (b) at least one snapshot must show the balance **deeper than one quantum** (`resource@bauxite` proxy below −32, i.e. the park consuming more than imports+production cover by more than 32); (c) the country must not sit in the 0-to-32 deficit band across more than ~2 consecutive monthly saves, since that state *is* the order to bid. **FAILED signature = the `911bed3c` one: flat `produced`, balance pinned between −26 and −33 for months.** If bids are now placed but never accepted (park changes, balance deepens, but active mill count does not grow), that is the NEXT question — the verdict window D, untested to date — and not a Fix 44 failure.
- **Defect H — the bid and the retreat were different sizes. FIXED 2026-08-11 (Fix 45).** Found by re-reading the controller after Fixes 42-44 landed, not from a campaign. `reactivate_aluminium_refineries_ai` prefers the **hydro** variant (35 bauxite, no coal — the preference protects coal, which is the catastrophic resource, §1.5), but `bauxite_shortage_ai`'s retreat loop closes **plain** first (32 bauxite). A hydro bid therefore came back **3 bauxite short** of where it started. Repeated, that walks the balance down into **[−33, −32]** — below the reopen gate (`> -32`) and above the mission's arm (`< -33`) — **a band where neither arm of the controller acts**, i.e. the same shape as the original defect C trap, 31 units narrower. Each cycle also swaps one active plain mill for a hydro one at **zero** change in aluminium output (bauxite −3, coal +20), so it is churn as well as drift. **Iron is immune for the usual arithmetic reason** — `hydro_steel_refinery` costs the same 25 iron as plain, so its bid and retreat already cancel; do NOT mirror Fix 45 to the steel loops. Reachable configuration confirmed in campaign data: **RIT at 1946.4 holds 2 hydro-inactive + 3 plain-active + 10 plain-inactive** (`buildings RIT 1946.4_Apr.hoi4 --match aluminium`); the symptom itself has not been observed, only the setup for it.
  **Fix 45:** the reopen writes `WA_AI_bauxite_bid_hydro` (1 = hydro, 0 = plain) on **every** branch, so it can never be stale; the retreat reads it once, clears it, and closes that variant first — cost equals credit exactly, hydro preference preserved. The marker steers only the **first** closure of a pass: an external collapse that closes several mills is not a refused bid and must not be steered by one. Termination is unaffected — the new branch fires at most once per pass and carries the same bauxite-leg gate as the Fix 43 fallback, so `tempCoal` still advances once the leg flag drops. The mission's `complete_effect` clears the marker (bid accepted). *Rejected alternatives, recorded so they are not re-proposed: moving the mission arm to `< -32` closes the band but makes a hydro bid close **two** mills; reordering the reopen to plain-first makes cost = credit but spends 20 coal per bid where hydro spends none; widening the gate to `> -35` cannot work at all — the 3-unit mismatch between a 35-cost bid and a 32-credit retreat is not expressible in fixed thresholds.*
- **Pass — Fix 45 sub-probe, no new instrumentation (add 2026-08-11):** the control variable is itself save-visible. (a) **Build fingerprint:** `wa_ai_bauxite_bid_hydro` present on ≥1 AI country in ≥1 save (`var <TAG> "^wa_ai_bauxite_bid_hydro" <saves...>`); absent everywhere = pre-Fix-45 build, **probe VOID, never FAILED** (it is `clear_variable`-cleared, so absence also means "no bid outstanding at this snapshot" — check another tag/save before concluding the build lacks it). (b) **No parking band:** no country's net bauxite (`savegame.py resources <TAG> <saves...>`, the **net** column) sits between −33 and −32 across two or more consecutive monthly saves. (c) **No variant swap:** on a country holding both variants (RIT is the witness), `savegame.py buildings <TAG> <saves...> --match aluminium` must not show `hydro_aluminium_refinery` climbing while `aluminium_refinery` falls with the active total flat. (d) **Negative guard:** a country whose imports genuinely collapse still closes mills — inactive levels rise — so the marker has not blocked the retreat. **Threshold 3** (narrow variable/level probe).
- **Pass:** (1) a WA_TLM metric exposes inactive-vs-active refinery levels per resource family, and `savegame.py` can read it; (2) no AI country holds >2 inactive refinery levels for more than ~6 consecutive months while a trade partner has matching ore flagged `to_export`; (3) shutdown and restart setpoints overlap — after the closing loop terminates, the restart gate is satisfiable without an external shock; (4) the AI's need signal for the *input* resource does not fall when refineries deactivate (the masking is closed); (5) counters live for AI countries: `total_X_mill` tracks real building levels within one month; (6) negative guard — a genuinely blockaded country (JAP-class, imports 0) still shuts refineries down rather than running them at a loss.
- **Probe:** three `savegame.py` commands, all added 2026-08-11 precisely because this item had to be investigated by hand:
  - `savegame.py buildings <TAG> <saves...> --match refinery` on ENG/USA/CAN/GER/JAP — sums `steel_refinery`/`aluminium_refinery`/`synthetic_refinery`, their `hydro_*` variants and every `*_inactive` twin, in owned and controlled columns. (Reference reading: `buildings ENG 1946.4_Apr.hoi4 --match refinery` = 1 active + 12 inactive `aluminium_refinery` + 3 active hydro.)
  - `savegame.py resources <TAG> <saves...>` for bauxite/iron/coal — `produced`, `transfer_overlord_subject`, `imported`, **net** (`to_use[0]`, which is what `resource@X` reads), deficit (`to_use[2]`), `to_export` and actually `exported`. The exporters-are-blind evidence (USA 9 978 flagged, 409 shipped) is the `to_export` vs `exported` gap.
  - `savegame.py decisions <TAG> <saves...> --match "bauxite_shortage_ai|reactivate_aluminium_refineries_ai"` — the fire counts are the `random_item` **`count`** field (monotone cumulative), *not* the `days`/`state` of an `active_timed_decision` entry. The command labels the two kinds apart; conflating them is what the flapping-vs-frozen diagnosis turned on.
  Also read `wa_ai_needs_bauxite` and `wa_ai_bauxite_resource_shortage` via `var`.
- **Threshold:** 5 (behavioural — the park staying matched to input supply over a campaign is the outcome).
- **Streak:** 0
- **History:**
  - 2026-08-11 · `911bed3c` · **FAILED** — full evidence above. ENG aluminium park 7 → 11 → 12 active (1942.6→1944.4) then **4 → 1 → 1** with 12 levels inactive from 1945.6; aluminium output 210 → 326 → **79**. Counters frozen since 1936 on ENG, USA and CAN.
  - **Tag-rule violation noted in passing:** `_economy_fatigue.txt` gates on `tag = USA`/`CAN`/`GER` (`:2485-2506`) and `original_tag = USA` (`:2337`) outside `WA_AI_CONFIG.txt`, contrary to AGENTS.md rule 4.

That is precisely the mechanism Fix 44 was written to enable, and it was absent last campaign. **Fix 45 sub-probe PASSED on (a)(c)(d):** `wa_ai_bauxite_bid_hydro` present on ENG (= 0 at 1945.12/1946.1, **= 1** at 1946.2/1946.3), no variant swap, retreat not blocked. **Flapping is gone:** cumulative `bauxite_shortage_ai` 4→7→13 and `reactivate_aluminium_refineries_ai` 0→2→6 across 1944.4/1945.6/1946.3, versus +30/+26 over the same span on `911bed3c` with a frozen inactive count. **First evidence Fix 43's hydro fallback fires:** `hydro_aluminium_refinery_inactive` = 1 at 1946.2/1946.3 with ENG's state count flat at 73, so it is not `on_state_control_changed`.
  - **Why it still FAILS — the unfixed half, exactly as scoped:** (2) RIT holds 13 plain + 2 hydro inactive with **0 active for ≥22 months**; (4) `wa_ai_bauxite_resource_shortage` = 0 at every date including 1946.3 (Defect B, never fixed); (5) counters still frozen at 31/31/31 and 2/2/2 since 1938.6 (Defect D, never fixed). **And Fix 45's criterion (b) is hit exactly on the boundary:** net bauxite = **−32.0** at both 1945.11 and 1945.12 — the dead band between the reopen gate (`> -32`) and the mission arm (`< -33`), the very band Fix 45 was written to prevent. Benign here (the park was fully open, nothing to reopen) and it resolves at 1946.1, but **the predicted band was observed, so Fix 45 is not yet fully validated** — do not retire the sub-probe on this campaign alone.

The reopen gate `> -32` is *satisfied* and it still never reopens — a **subject-scope** case the reactivation path does not cover, and the clearest criterion-(2) violation in the campaign.

Exporters still blind, unchanged: USA 1946.3 bauxite `produced` 21 147 with 10 573 flagged `to_export` and only **380 exported**; CAN 313 flagged, **0 bauxite exported** while shipping 620 aluminium. USA and CAN carry zero inactive refinery levels at every date; JAP aluminium recovers 9 active/3 inactive → 12/0.

### R31. SOV keeps a ≥1000 km multirole fighter across the La-5FN generation (Fix 47)

- **Fix under test:** Fix 47 — `common/scripted_triggers/WA_AI_EQUIPMENT_triggers.txt` + `common/scripted_effects/WA_AI_EQUIPMENT_effects.txt` (new generic context-trigger family and its monthly latching effect), consumed by `common/ai_equipment/SOV_planes.txt`, group `SOV_fighter_mr` (commit hash TBD; authored 2026-08-12, uncommitted when this item was written). Root cause: every design in every `ai_equipment` group is killed by a blanket `has_tech = <next tech> → factor = 0`, so the AI always builds the newest researched airframe regardless of context. Computed `air_range` for the SOV multirole chain, model `(airframe base + Σ module add_stats) × (1 + Σ module multiply_stats)`: La-5 **1166 km** → La-5FN **836 km** → La-7 **1364 km**. The La-5FN generation is a 330 km regression that sits 164 km under the 1000 km target for ~1 year of the Eastern Front. Fix 47 adds `fighter_mr_7_lr` (same `SOV_la_5fn_airframe`, `internal_slot_1 = fuel_tanks_medium`, bomb rack removed → **1017 km**), demotes the short-range `fighter_mr_7` to 0.1 and retains `fighter_mr_6` (La-5) at 0.5 as the fallback — all three only while the latched `WA_AI_EQUIPMENT_needs_long_range_air` holds.
- **LEVER ADDED 2026-08-14 — Fix 70, and it targets the failure mode `f9321934` finally identified.** Three campaigns established that `fighter_mr_7_lr` is **never created**, not created-and-rejected, and that no priority tuning can reach a design the engine never instantiates. Fix 70 creates it explicitly: a new hidden country event `sov_armor.980` in `events/WA_AI_SOV.txt` (the sanctioned Country-layer location) fires on day ~1 for an AI SOV and runs `create_equipment_variant` for "Lavochkin La-5FN (long range)" on `SOV_la_5fn_airframe` with `allow_without_tech = yes` — vanilla-documented as "create the variant even if the type hasn't been unlocked yet" — so the design provably pre-dates any engine auto-design of that airframe. `fighter_mr_7_lr`'s `target_variant` (match_value 1000) should then *match* an existing design rather than having to create one, and its Fix 47 priority ladder finally has something to select. Modules are copied verbatim from that `target_variant`; the two must be changed together or the match breaks silently. `mark_older_equipment_obsolete` left at default `no` on purpose — the short-range La-5FN is the intended design whenever the range latch is shut, and obsoleting it would gate nothing anyway.
- **CORRECTION 2026-08-14 — Fix 70 AS SHIPPED WAS A DEFECT, and Fix 79 (`496c4dae4` → this commit) repairs it. Read this before scoring anything on a `d683fb022`-or-earlier build.** Fix 70 fired `sov_armor.980` on day ~1 with `allow_without_tech = yes`, and that token does not merely waive the tech for the *creation* — **the resulting design is producible**. Campaign `be18f9c7` measured the consequence: the long-range La-5FN (registry id **3207**) ran on SOV's multirole line **continuously from 1936.2 to 1944.2 — 96 months, 1–2 lines, 10 to 93 factories** — while `sov_fighter_multirole_ad_tech_7`, the tech that carries `enable_equipments = { SOV_la_5fn_airframe }`, was only researched **1943.6.9**. The entire Soviet pre-war, Winter War and Barbarossa multirole fighter force was a 1943 airframe. **Fix 79** gates the event on `has_tech = sov_fighter_multirole_ad_tech_7` (plus `sov_fighter_ad_tech_1` for `fuel_tanks_medium`) and **removes `allow_without_tech` entirely** — with the airframe tech in it waives nothing, and leaving a no-op token that re-opens the hole on the next trigger edit is a trap. The event still fires from its own `trigger` + `mean_time_to_happen`, i.e. strictly *after* the tech, so it is not an `on_research_complete` race and it stays `is_ai`-gated (a SOV player's design list is untouched).
  - **Fix 70's "must create it early or lose the race to the engine auto-design" premise is DISPROVED by the same campaign — do not restore day-1 creation on that argument.** `be18f9c7`'s registry holds **both** id 3207 (long-range, `fuel_tanks_medium`, created 1936 by the event) **and** id 7405 (short-range, `self_sealing_fuel_tanks_small`) on `SOV_la_5fn_airframe`, and 7405 is the *later* id — the AI designer created its own design on that archetype years after the event had already put one there. A pre-existing design therefore does **not** block a later creation, in either direction. Whatever the archetype-uniqueness rule is, it constrains which design an `ai_equipment` **group** will instantiate; it is not a veto on `create_equipment_variant`, which is an unconditional effect.
  - **`be18f9c7` also settles the branch (a) prediction below, negatively: the effect is NOT rejected.** Id 3207 exists, carries `internal_slot_1 = fuel_tanks_medium`, and was produced — so `ordnance_equipment` in `fighter_role_slot` on a `small_fighter_multirole_airframe` **is accepted by `create_equipment_variant`**, and the `equipment_effects.cpp:814` failure of the ITA Re.2000 GA and SOV LaGG-3-35 sites has some other differentiator. That triage is retained below for the ITA/LaGG-3 defect it still describes, but it is no longer this item's predicted outcome.
- **BRANCH (a) PREDICTION — SUPERSEDED, retained only for the separate ITA/LaGG-3 `equipment_effects.cpp:814` defect it documents (added 2026-08-14 by that triage, disproved for *this* item the same day by `be18f9c7`, see above).** A repo-wide audit of all **47** `create_equipment_variant` sites in `common/` and `events/` found that exactly **three** sit on the `small_fighter_multirole_airframe` archetype, and they are exactly the three that put `ordnance_equipment` in `fighter_role_slot`: `air_techs_ita.txt:1073` ("Re.2000 GA"), `air_techs_sov.txt:1590` ("Lavochkin LaGG-3-35") — **both of which the engine rejects** with *"Equipment category differs from that of the type"* — and Fix 70's own `sov_armor.980` La-5FN. The other three fighter-airframe sites (`FRA_ms_410`, `USA_p_51d`, `ENG_spitfire_f_mkix`) use `gun_sights` in that slot and are **not** rejected. Everything else was eliminated as the differentiator: the two archetypes `small_fighter_airframe` and `small_fighter_multirole_airframe` are identical in `type`, `type_override`, `allowed_types`, `module_slots` and mission gates (they differ only in name, `air_map_icon_frame`, `interface_overview_category_index` and their mutual `substitute`); every module in both failing designs is legal for the slot it sits in; `parent_version = 1` orphaning is universal across the mod's air variants and vanilla-documented as harmless; required-slot gaps are common and tolerated; and both variants' `icon` GFX are correctly registered under their own airframe in `gfx/interface/equipmentdesigner/graphic_db/00_plane_icons.txt`. **So if the boot test reads branch (a) — "Lavochkin La-5FN (long range)" absent from the 1936 registry — do not debug `sov_armor.980`'s trigger or module list.** The event is almost certainly firing and the effect is almost certainly being rejected for the same reason the ITA and SOV tech variants are, and the fix lives in a shared file (`ordnance_equipment` in `common/units/equipment/modules/00_plane_modules.txt`, or `allowed_types` on the three `small_fighter_*` archetypes in `common/units/equipment/plane_airframes.txt`) — **not** at the variant site. Widening `allowed_types` was deliberately **not** done blind: `# tactical_bomber` is commented out there on purpose, and if the engine does not pin the type through `type_override` a bomb-carrying fighter design would resolve to `cas` equipment, which is a campaign-scale behaviour change across hundreds of `ai_equipment` designs. Discriminate first with `research all` in debug (see F9), which fires every `on_research_complete` and will show whether the mod's *bomber* variants — which also carry `cas`-only bomb modules on archetypes that do not list `cas` — are rejected too. If they are, the rule is `allowed_types`; if only the `ordnance_equipment` ones fail, the rule is that module.
- **Pass, four legs (all must hold):**
  1. **Latch leg.** SOV's `wa_tlm_r47_lrange_n` is > 0 by the first save after SOV enters its main land war, and `wa_tlm_r47_lrange_last_t` keeps advancing (the latch persists, it does not fire once). SOV trips the "deep theatre" leg on `num_of_controlled_states > 79` the moment it is at war, so a zero here means the latch or its wiring is broken, not that the situation never arose.
  2. **Design leg.** From the save after SOV researches `sov_fighter_multirole_ad_tech_7` (La-5FN) until it researches `..._tech_8` (La-7), SOV's `air_fighter_mr` production line sits on a variant whose airframe is `SOV_la_5fn_airframe` **with `fuel_tanks_medium`** (the long-range variant, named "Lavochkin La-5FN (long range)") **or** on `SOV_la_5_airframe` (the retained La-5). A line on a `SOV_la_5fn_airframe` variant carrying `self_sealing_fuel_tanks_small` during that window is the pre-fix behaviour and is a FAIL.
  3. **ANACHRONISM LEG — added 2026-08-14 with Fix 79, and it is the leg that would have caught the Fix 70 defect. Score it BEFORE the design leg; if it fails, the design leg's verdict is void, not favourable.** In every save dated **before** SOV researches `sov_fighter_multirole_ad_tech_7`, **no** SOV production line runs **any** `SOV_la_5fn_airframe` variant — long-range or short — and the "Lavochkin La-5FN (long range)" name is **absent from the `equipments={}` registry** of those saves. A pre-tech La-5FN line is an outright FAIL of the item however good the in-window reading looks: on `be18f9c7` the design leg read PASS purely because a 1943 airframe had been in production since 1936.2, which is the *opposite* of what this item exists to verify. The corollary sanity check: SOV's early-war multirole line should be on the era-appropriate airframe (I-16 / LaGG-3 / Yak family), not on anything Lavochkin-La-5.
  4. **No-thrash leg.** Across the campaign SOV's `air_fighter_mr` line changes variant at most ~3 times in the La-5/La-5FN/La-7 window. More than that means the latch is chattering and the hysteresis is not doing its job — report the flip dates.
- **Probe:** (a) Latch leg: `savegame.py tlm SOV <last-save>` and read `wa_tlm_r47_lrange_n`, `_first_t`, `_last_t`; decode the clock as `year = 1936 + floor(clock/12)`. **Absence contract:** no `wa_tlm_*` namespace at all = pre-WA_TLM build, probe VOID; `wa_tlm_version` present but `< 3` = build predates Fix 47, mark **NOT YET TESTED**, never FAILED. `wa_tlm_r47_lrange_n = 0` **with** `wa_tlm_version = 3` is a real zero reading and scores the latch leg FAILED. (b) Design leg: dump SOV's `production` section (`savegame.py section SOV production <saves…>`); lines identify equipment by numeric `equipment_variant_index`, so resolve the ids through the top-level `equipments={}` registry exactly as R11 and R30 do — **a name grep on `production` alone returns MIO idea names, not equipment; resolve through the registry or the answer is wrong.** Report the resolved variant name and its modules per save from 1942 to campaign end. (c) **Anachronism leg — this is why the design-leg dump must start at 1936.2, not 1942:** run the same registry-resolved production dump over the **whole** campaign and report the FIRST save in which any `SOV_la_5fn_airframe` variant appears on a line, against `sov_fighter_multirole_ad_tech_7`'s completion date from SOV's `technology` section. Cross-check on the registry side by grepping the *earliest* save's `equipments={}` for "long range" — remember the block key is the equipment definition name and only entries carrying `name=` are designed variants. (d) No-thrash leg: the same per-save variant series, counted for changes. (e) **Creation stamp (Fix 79, `wa_tlm_version` ≥ 12):** `savegame.py tlm SOV <last-save>` for `wa_tlm_r31_lrvar_t`. It is an **invocation** stamp, not a success stamp — `create_equipment_variant` returns nothing — so read it only *against* the registry: `0` + no registry entry = `sov_armor.980` never fired (debug its trigger); `> 0` + no registry entry = the effect was **rejected by the engine** (the shared-equipment-file question, not the event site); `> 0` + entry present = the design exists and the verdict belongs to the selection layer. Decode as `year = 1936 + floor(clock/12)`; it must land at or after the tech date. **Absence contract:** `wa_tlm_version < 12` simply lacks the metric — NOT CHECKED, never FAILED. (f) Build fingerprint: `wa_tlm_version = 3` plus git ancestry of the Fix 47 commit; confirm before recording any FAILED. **A build carrying Fix 70 but not Fix 79 (i.e. `496c4dae4` ≤ HEAD < the Fix 79 commit) cannot score the design leg at all** — the anachronism defect is in it by construction; mark the item VOID for that campaign.
- **Threshold:** 3 (narrow — the pass criterion is a discrete design-selection fact, "which variant is the line on", plus a counter reading; it is not an emergent front outcome).
- **KNOWN UNKNOWN carried by this item:** nobody has yet validated in-game how often the engine re-evaluates `ai_equipment` `priority` modifiers, or whether scripted triggers resolve inside them at all (no `ai_equipment` file in the repo used one before Fix 47). If the design leg reads pre-fix behaviour while `wa_tlm_r47_lrange_n > 0`, the latch is working and the **priority modifiers are not being re-evaluated (or the scripted trigger is not resolving)** — that is a different, larger finding than "the gate is mistuned", and it invalidates the whole contextual-switching approach, not just this pilot. Say which of the two it is before proposing a fix.

Do not count it, do not advance the streak, and do not read it as evidence the mechanism works.** Build IN: HEAD `d683fb022`, i.e. Fix 70 (`496c4dae4`) present and Fix 79 absent — the one build window in which this item is unscoreable by construction. 126 monthly saves, 1936.2 → 1946.7. **What happened:** `sov_armor.980` fired on day ~1 with `allow_without_tech = yes`, which does not only waive the tech for the *creation* — the resulting design is **producible**. Registry id **3207** "Lavochkin La-5FN (long range)" (`internal_slot_1 = fuel_tanks_medium`) is on SOV's multirole production line **continuously from 1936.2 to 1944.2 — 96 months, 1–2 concurrent lines, 10 → 93 factories** — while `sov_fighter_multirole_ad_tech_7` is only researched **1943.6.9** (`_8` at **1944.2.16**). The design leg's window (1943.7 → 1944.2) is satisfied only because a 1943 airframe had already been in production for seven years. The whole Soviet pre-war, Winter War and Barbarossa multirole fighter force was La-5FNs; the short-range La-5FN (id **7405**, `self_sealing_fuel_tanks_small`) never received a single factory. **This is what the new anachronism leg exists to catch**, and it is why that leg is scored first.
  - **Two mechanism findings carried out of this campaign, both durable:** (i) **the "engine refuses a second design on an archetype it already holds one for" premise is disproved** — ids 3207 and 7405 coexist on `SOV_la_5fn_airframe` and 7405 is the *later* id, so the AI designer created its own design on that archetype years after the event had put one there. A pre-existing design blocks nothing in either direction, and `create_equipment_variant` is an unconditional effect regardless. (ii) **branch (a) is disproved** — `ordnance_equipment` in `fighter_role_slot` on a `small_fighter_multirole_airframe` **is** accepted by `create_equipment_variant` (3207 exists and was produced), so the `equipment_effects.cpp:814` rejection of the ITA Re.2000 GA and SOV LaGG-3-35 sites has a different differentiator and remains an open, separate defect. **Fix 79** (this commit) gates `sov_armor.980` on `has_tech = sov_fighter_multirole_ad_tech_7`, drops `allow_without_tech`, and adds `wa_tlm_r31_lrvar_t` so the next campaign can tell "never fired" from "fired and rejected". Streak stays 0; status stays FAILED.

### R32. ENG's strategic-bomber aluminium step waits for headroom — in BOTH bomber groups (Fixes 47 + 49)

- **Fix under test:** two changes, ONE item — the gate is only meaningful as a *role* cap, so it is scored as one.
  - **Fix 47** — same trigger/effect family as R31, consumed by `common/ai_equipment/ENG_planes.txt`, group `ENG_heavy_strategic_bomber` (commit hash TBD; authored 2026-08-12, uncommitted when this item was written). Measured per-unit resource step at the Manchester → Lancaster transition (`eng_heavy_strategic_bomber_ad_tech_3`, `start_year 1942`, taken 1943-44 in practice): Manchester Mk IA = **5 aluminium / 5 coal / 2 rubber, ~45.4 IC**; Lancaster B Mk I = **7 aluminium / 5 coal / 2 rubber, ~58.1 IC** — **+2 aluminium (+40%) and +12.7 IC (+28%) per unit**, charged per assigned military factory per day, i.e. **+40…+80 aluminium/day** on the 20-40 factories ENG runs on heavy bombers. Fix 47 gates `heavy_strategic_bomber_3` and `_3_1` (the whole Lancaster generation, because `..._tech_3_1` follows within months) on `WA_AI_EQUIPMENT_can_absorb_aluminium_shock_large`, and defers `heavy_strategic_bomber_2`'s (Manchester) supersession until the gate opens. `heavy_strategic_bomber_4` (Lincoln, 1945) is deliberately ungated as the escape valve.
  - **Fix 49** (2026-08-12, same file, group `ENG_strategic_bomber`) — closes the bypass Fix 47 knowingly left open (this item's old "scope limitation" bullet). Both groups serve `air_strategic_bomber`, and this one reaches 7 aluminium/unit **two years earlier**, ungated, so the Fix 47 gate capped nothing. Verified per-unit costs (archetype `large_bomber_airframe` contributes aluminium 1 / coal 5 / rubber 2 — checked to be **identical** to `large_heavy_bomber_airframe`, so the two groups' figures are directly comparable): Hampden Mk I **5 Al, ~31.1 IC, 2482 km** → Stirling B Mk I **7 Al, ~59.6 IC, 950 km** → Halifax B Mk II **7 Al, ~56.4 IC, 1778 km** → Halifax B Mk III **7 Al, ~62.0 IC, 2036 km** → Windsor B Mk II **7 Al, ~62.4 IC, 3340 km**. The step is Hampden → Stirling (`eng_strategic_bomber_ad_tech_2`, `start_year 1940`): **+2 aluminium (+40%) and +28.5 IC (+92%)**, and it is purely the 2-engine → 4-engine module change (2 Al → 4 Al), the same mechanism as Manchester → Lancaster. Fix 49 therefore gates the **whole 7-aluminium generation** — `strategic_bomber_2` (Stirling), `_3` and `_4` (both Halifaxes) — on the same latch, defers `strategic_bomber_1`'s (Hampden) supersession until the gate opens, hard-cuts the Hampden at `eng_strategic_bomber_ad_tech_5`, and leaves `strategic_bomber_5` (Windsor, 1946) ungated as the escape valve. It also adds `strategic_bomber_2_lr` ("Short Stirling B Mk I (long range)", `internal_slot_1` rear MG → `self_sealing_fuel_tanks_small`, **950 → 1038 km**, same 7 aluminium) behind *both* the aluminium gate and `WA_AI_EQUIPMENT_needs_long_range_air`, because the Stirling generation is also a range collapse under the 1000 km bar. **No new instrumentation:** Fix 49 reuses the Fix 47 latches, so `wa_tlm_version` stays at 3 and the gate leg below is unchanged.
- **Correction of record carried by this item:** the reported symptom was "steel demand roughly doubles". **Plane designs in this mod cost aluminium, coal and rubber — not steel.** The only plane modules with a steel `build_cost_resources` are the `*_engine`-suffixed heavy-armour variants, which no ENG bomber design uses. Score this item on aluminium; a steel reading is not evidence either way.
- **Pass, four legs (all must hold):**
  1. **Gate leg.** ENG's `wa_tlm_r47_alu_large_n` and `_first_t`/`_last_t` are consistent with its net aluminium: the gate is open only in months where the net balance clears roughly +50/day. If `n = 0` all campaign, ENG never had the headroom — that is a **PASS on the gate's own terms** and pushes the verdict onto legs 2-3.
  2. **Role leg (Fix 49, the one that makes the cap real).** While the gate has never opened (`wa_tlm_r47_alu_large_n` still 0) and neither escape tech is in (`eng_heavy_strategic_bomber_ad_tech_4` for the heavy group, `eng_strategic_bomber_ad_tech_5` for the other), **no** ENG `air_strategic_bomber` production line — in either group — sits on a 7-aluminium airframe. The 7-Al set is `ENG_lancaster_airframe`, `ENG_stirling_airframe`, `ENG_halifax_airframe`. The permitted set in that window is `ENG_whitley_airframe`, `ENG_manchester_airframe`, `ENG_hampden_airframe` (5 Al each) and the older `ENG_heyford_airframe`. A Stirling or Halifax line before the first gate-open month is an outright FAIL, and it is the *same* failure a pre-fix Lancaster line would be — do not score it as a separate finding.
  3. **Escape-valve leg.** Once the escape tech of a group is researched, that group's line moves onto its ungated modern airframe — `ENG_lincoln_airframe` (heavy group, 1945) / `ENG_windsor_airframe` (other group, 1946) — **even if the gate never opened**. A group left with no producing line at all, or still on the 1938-40 fallback after its escape tech, means a gate closed with no way out and is a FAIL of the arrangement rather than of the economy.
  4. **Economy leg (the point of the fix).** ENG's aluminium park does not repeat the `911bed3c` collapse in the window where the bomber transitions would otherwise have landed: `savegame.py buildings ENG <saves…> --match aluminium` must not show active levels falling toward 0 with 10+ inactive levels across 1943-1945. *(Shared boundary with R29: if the collapse happens anyway and `r47_alu_large_n = 0`, the bomber transitions were not the cause and this leg is `NOT CHECKED`, not FAILED — say so explicitly rather than double-counting R29's failure here.)*
- **Probe:** (a) Gate leg: `savegame.py tlm ENG <last-save>` for `wa_tlm_r47_alu_large_n`, `_first_t`, `_last_t`, decoded as `year = 1936 + floor(clock/12)`; cross-check against `savegame.py resources ENG <saves…>` for aluminium — read the **EFFECTIVE** column (`to_use[0] + to_use[2]`, what `resource@X` actually compares). **Neither the `produced` line alone** (that mistake nearly produced a false FAIL on R25) **nor the `net` column alone** (that mistake produced a false accusation against Fix 39, retracted in this item's history on 2026-08-13 — ENG 1942.6 is net 807.3 but effective +8.3 against a `> 50` bar). **Absence contract:** no `wa_tlm_*` at all = probe VOID; `wa_tlm_version < 3` = **NOT YET TESTED**, never FAILED; `n = 0` with `wa_tlm_version = 3` is a real zero. (b) Role and escape-valve legs (one reading, two questions): `savegame.py section ENG production <saves…>`, resolving `equipment_variant_index` through the top-level `equipments={}` registry (a name grep on `production` returns MIO idea names, not equipment); report **every** ENG bomber line's airframe per save, **1940 → end** (not 1942 → end: the Stirling window opens in 1940, which is where Fix 49's half of the item lives). Both groups feed the same role, so the reading is per-line, not per-group. (c) Long-range sub-check, report-only: when a line does sit on `ENG_stirling_airframe` while `wa_tlm_r47_lrange_n > 0`, say whether its `internal_slot_1` is `self_sealing_fuel_tanks_small` (the Fix 49 long-range variant, "Short Stirling B Mk I (long range)") or the stock rear MG. This is evidence for R31's KNOWN UNKNOWN about whether `ai_equipment` priority modifiers are re-evaluated at all, not a pass criterion here. (d) Economy leg: `savegame.py buildings ENG <saves…> --match aluminium` (the command sums the `*_inactive` twins; a state block omits `controller=` when controller = owner, which the command already handles).
- **Threshold:** 3 (narrow — a counter reading plus a discrete "which airframe is the line on" fact).
- **Build fingerprint:** `wa_tlm_version = 3` proves Fix 47 only — **Fix 49 shipped no new instrumentation and is invisible to TLM.** Confirm the Fix 49 half by git ancestry of its commit before recording any FAILED on legs 2-3. Save-native corroboration, if available: the string "Short Stirling B Mk I (long range)" can only appear in the `equipments={}` registry of a post-Fix-49 build (absence proves nothing — the variant is only created if it was ever selected).
- **Scope limitation from the original item — now CLOSED, do not re-diagnose:** this item used to record that the parallel `ENG_strategic_bomber` group was ungated and could serve the same role at 7 aluminium/unit from 1940, making the Fix 47 gate a non-cap. **Fix 49 closed it** (see above), and the item was widened rather than split, because two gates on one role are one behaviour. If a scoring session finds the bypass alive, the finding is "Fix 49 did not take", not a new scope limitation.
