### R35. Parallel tank branches select the best researched and affordable design (Fix 50)

- **Fix under test:** Fix 50 — competitive-frontier generation for 11 branched
  tank roles in `ENG/FRA/GER/JAP/SOV/USA_tank.txt` (authored 2026-08-12,
  commit `88e516780`). The selector derives topology from technology `path` edges,
  ranks every design sharing the role, neutralises old file-order supersession
  hooks, and keeps lower positive-priority fallbacks. Resource-heavy ranks are
  held by the latched `WA_AI_EQUIPMENT_can_absorb_*_shock_*` gates. The canonical
  regression is USA medium tanks: the real branches are Sherman and T20/T23,
  not `Sherman -> T20 -> T23`; the generated order is **M4A3E8 > M4A2 > T23 >
  T20 > M4A1 > M3 Lee > M2**.
- **Pass, three legs:** (1) after USA has researched M4A2 and T20/T23, its
  `land_medium_tank` production line resolves to M4A2 or M4A3E8, never T20/T23;
  (2) with `usa_medium_tank_chassis_3_3` researched, an open
  `WA_AI_EQUIPMENT_tun_small_ok` latch selects M4A3E8, while a closed latch
  exposes M4A2 rather than leaving the role empty; (3) across the other ten
  branched groups, every active line resolves to the highest-ranked researched
  design whose resource hold is open, or to a lower/emergency fallback — zero
  branched roles with researched designs may have no production candidate.
- **Probe:** save-native; no WA_TLM added. For USA plus ENG/FRA/GER/JAP/SOV,
  dump each country's `production` and technology/flag sections at 1940.6,
  1942.6, 1944.6 and campaign end. Resolve every `equipment_variant_index`
  through the top-level `equipments={}` registry (a name grep on `production`
  alone returns MIO names and is invalid). Compare the resolved chassis/design
  against `tools/equipment_evaluator/output/parallel_branch_decisions.md`, but
  first filter that ranking to researched enable techs and discard any rank
  whose required `WA_AI_EQUIPMENT_*_ok` country flag is absent. Explicitly
  report USA's medium line, `WA_AI_EQUIPMENT_tun_small_ok`, and whether
  `usa_medium_tank_chassis_3_2`, `_3_3`, `_4`, `_5` are researched at each
  date. Build fingerprint: git ancestry must include Fix 50; generated
  `WA_EQUIPGEN_BEGIN ... kind=priority_factor` comments do not serialize into
  saves and are not a save-native fingerprint.
- **Fix 51 sub-probe (2026-08-12) — the whole tank package moved to the production-line layer.** `tools/equipment_evaluator --domain tanks --apply-production-strategy` now generates `common/ai_strategy/WA_AI_PRODUCTION_COUNTRY_<TAG>_TANKS.txt` for ENG/FRA/GER/JAP/SOV/USA: **57 `production_upgrade_desire_offset` entries over all 11 frontiers**, `+100` on the wanted design and `-100` on every newer chassis, with the Fix 47 resource latches carried across as the block's `enable`. Emission is proved exhaustively (`test_generation.py::ProductionStrategyTests`): every reachable gate state names exactly one wanted design, and **no gate state leaves a branched role empty** — which is leg (3) satisfied by construction rather than by observation. USA medium is the canonical row: `+100` on `_4_2` (M4A3E8) while `WA_AI_EQUIPMENT_can_absorb_tungsten_shock_small` holds, `+100` on `_4` (M4A2) while it does not, `-100` on `_5` (T20) and `_6` (T23) in both states. Score legs (1) and (2) unchanged; also check SOV medium (was T 43, expected T 34 85) and USA modern (was M46, expected T26E4). **Mind the numbering: the tech and equipment tokens are numbered differently** (M4A2 = tech `usa_medium_tank_chassis_3_2` but equipment `tank_usa_medium_chassis_4`). Score legs (1) and (2) unchanged. Note the latch was CLOSED on USA at 1944.6 and 1946.4 on `bec4d829`, so the M4A3E8 branch may stay unobserved again — that is a real reading, not a probe gap. Also re-check the two script bugs fixed alongside: `SOV_heavy_tanks` no longer emits scientific notation (`factor = 9e-06`, which PDXScript reads as **9**, had inverted the T 35 emergency floor above IS 8/IS 4/IS 3 — generator fixed in `tools/equipment_evaluator/emit.py::_pdx_num`), and ENG `medium_tank_4` (Crusader Mk I) now builds `tank_eng_medium_chassis_3` instead of the Covenanter's `_2`.
- **Comet sub-probe (Fix 58, 2026-08-13) — and a RETRACTION of one of this item's own faulty frontiers.** `tank_eng_medium_chassis_5` (Comet) had **no design in any ENG group**, so the AI auto-designed it and ran it with 30+ factories from 1944.6 to the end of `02bd4445`, outside the ranking, the resource gates and the emitted offsets. `ENG_tank.txt` now carries `medium_tank_8` (Comet) — modules taken from the chassis' own `default_modules` and audited slot by slot against `allowed_module_categories` (9/9 mountable, 4/4 required slots filled), deliberately **without** a supersession modifier, because what `eng_medium_tank_chassis_5` leads to is `eng_modern_tank_chassis_1`, a *different role*; hooking the Comet to it would empty ENG's medium role the moment modern tanks land. **The retraction:** with the Comet finally visible, the evaluator ranks it **PRIMARY, rank 8 of 8, above the Cromwell (+1.224 vs +1.157)**. So this item's "ENG medium 1944.6 & 1946.4 Cromwell (100) → **Comet**" faulty-frontier line was a **false accusation — the AI's choice was correct all along**, and the defect was that the mod could not see the design it was choosing. Do not re-score ENG medium as a selection failure. `WA_AI_PRODUCTION_COUNTRY_ENG_TANKS.txt` consequently went from a lone `+100` on the Cromwell to a real two-state selector (Comet while the steel+tungsten latches hold, Cromwell with `-100` on the Comet when they do not), which also closes the "emits only ONE block and no negative offsets" generator defect — by fixing the emitter's *input*, not the emitter. **Pass criterion:** ENG's `land_medium_tank` line resolves to `tank_eng_medium_chassis_5` or `_4_2` and the role is never empty; score it on the design leg like the rest of this item. **Still open in the same role, deliberately not done:** `tank_eng_medium_chassis_7` (M3 Grant), `_8` (Sherman III) and `_9` (Sherman V) remain uncovered — ENG ran 15 factories on `_9` at 1943.6 in this campaign. They are lend-lease leaf techs with no `start_year` and no `leads_to_tech`, i.e. a parallel supply-driven set rather than a succession step, so adding them changes the frontier's topology and is a separate judgement.
- **Ladder-rescale sub-probe (2026-08-13, uncommitted at the time of writing).** The generated priority ladder underflowed on deep frontiers — `SOV_heavy_tanks` emitted `factor = 0.000009`, which fixed-point script numbers cannot tell from `0`, i.e. *never build this design* rather than *emergency fallback*. A configurable floor (`config.json` `frontier_priority.floor = 0.1`, matching vanilla's own smallest `ai_equipment` factor; Expert AI never goes below 0.5) now re-spaces only the underflowing tail; every rank above the knee keeps its exact geometric value. **25 factors rescaled across `FRA/GER/JAP/SOV/USA_tank.txt`** — the deepest, `SOV_heavy_tanks`, goes `10 / 3 / 1 / 0.3 / 0.000009…` → `10 / 3 / 1 / 0.3 / 0.266 / 0.235 / 0.208 / 0.184 / 0.163 / 0.144 / 0.128 / 0.113 / 0.1`. **Pass criterion:** in any campaign where a branched role's better ranks are all shut out (resource latch closed, or the chassis not yet researched), the role still runs *a* line rather than none. That is R35 leg 3 and needs no extra probe — score it there. **Known trade-off to watch, and the reason this is a sub-probe rather than a silent change:** the re-spaced band is flat (all of `SOV_heavy_tanks` ranks 1–9 now sit within 2.7× of each other instead of spanning four orders of magnitude), so if the engine treats priority as a weighted lottery, a country reduced to its emergency floor will pick among old heavies less sharply by rank than before. If a campaign shows a country building, say, a T-35 while an IS-3 is available and unheld, **lower the floor to 0.01 rather than reverting** — it is one config value and the top of every ladder is unaffected.
- **Coverage sub-probe (2026-08-13) — check this BEFORE scoring a leg, it can void one.**
  `ai_equipment` only steers equipment it has a design for; a chassis unlocked inside a role
  with no design in any of that country's groups is auto-designed by the engine and is
  invisible to the ranking, the resource gates and the emitted offsets. The evaluator now
  reports these (`python -m equipment_evaluator --domain tanks`, written to
  `tools/equipment_evaluator/output/coverage_gaps.md`): **39 gaps, 11 in branched roles**,
  including all four post-Cromwell ENG mediums (`tank_eng_medium_chassis_5` Comet, `_7`,
  `_8`, `_9`), `tank_ger_modern_chassis_5`, `tank_usa_modern_chassis_5`/`_6`,
  `tank_usa_heavy_chassis_6`, `tank_sov_heavy_chassis_2_3`. **If a save shows a line on an
  uncovered chassis, that role is NOT a fix failure — it is a design-layer hole**, and it is
  the reason `WA_AI_PRODUCTION_COUNTRY_ENG_TANKS.txt` emits a lone `+100` on the Cromwell
  with no suppressions. Report which faulty frontiers are coverage gaps and which are real
  selection failures; they need different fixes and must not be pooled.

The ROOT CAUSE bullet R30/R31 cite is in this item's history below (`bec4d829` block).

## Recurring cosmetic anomalies — check here before reporting a "finding"

- **The global `spanish_civil_war` flag is never cleared on SCW resolution.** `scw_over` / `nationalist_victory` / `SPR_franco_won` are the authoritative end markers; the war flag still being set says nothing about the war still running (seen resolved-but-set in `31eaf7e6`).
- **`ww2_*` theatre flags carry last-seen dates, not first-set dates.** They are re-stamped on every on_action fire, so their set-dates are useless as a war timeline — use the war/peace flags instead. (In `66d6b53c` all three read 1945.3.1.)
- **Double Trotsky flags** appear in SOV flag blocks; harmless duplication, not a political-path bug.
- **`atomic_research_completed` firing ~1940 is a research-tree marker, not a bomb.** It marks the research step, not weapon availability — do not report early nukes off this flag.
- **`production` stores equipment by numeric variant id.** A name grep (`--grep "tank"`) on the `production` section only hits MIO idea names — resolve `equipment_variant_index` via the top-level `equipments={}` registry before concluding a line doesn't exist.
- **`lend_lease_to_allies_history` misses `send_equipment` transfers.** The WA relief system moves stock instantly without touching the diplomacy IC ledger; zero ledger IC between two countries is NOT evidence of no equipment flow — check `creator=` tags in the recipient's stockpile.
- **`units` line count absorbs the navy** — never use it as an army-size proxy; run `savegame.py army <TAG> <saves...>` instead.
- **`division={` blocks appear in TWO sections.** `units` holds deployed divisions; the `xp_by_template` blocks under `experience_status` are siblings of `units`, not nested inside it. A whole-country-block count over-reports — USA at 1944.1 in `911bed3c` is 97 total vs **80 deployed**; SOV 1942.6 is 409 total vs **373** deployed. **Use `savegame.py army`, which does the `units` scoping for you**; do not hand-count. It also prints the two cross-checks, which are not equivalent: `wa_tlm_comp_div_total` equals the units-only count (49/49 tag×date cells on `911bed3c`; one 1-division monthly-sample lag seen on GER 1946.4), while `num_armies_for_training` tracks it only while the army is stable and **overstates during losses** (ITA 1946.4: 28.0 vs 16 deployed). Use the TLM metric for analysis — but it is WA_TLM, so gameplay logic must never read it.
- **A state block omits `controller=` when the controller is the owner.** Taking the field literally makes a country look like it controls only its conquests (ENG 1946.4 reads 3 controlled states instead of 75). `savegame.py buildings` defaults controller to owner; any ad-hoc `states={}` parsing must do the same.
- **`decision_status` `days` and `count` are different clocks and different populations.** `active_timed_decision` exists only while live and carries `days` (remaining mission time when `state=active`; a re-arm cooldown, on an unrelated clock, when `state=failed` or `re_enable_cooldown`). `random_item` carries `count`, a **monotone cumulative fire counter** — that is the field for "how many times did this fire". Two entries were conflated twice in one session; `savegame.py decisions` labels the kinds apart.
- **`capitulated=yes` at depth 3 is a wargoal property, not capitulation.** It appears inside `<TAG>/diplomacy/wargoals` for countries that are perfectly healthy. Only a **depth-2** `capitulated=` is the country's own status.
- **`resource@X` reads `to_use[0] + to_use[2]` — net available MINUS unmet demand.** Not `produced`, and **not `net` alone**. `savegame.py resources` prints it as the **EFFECTIVE** column. ENG aluminium 1942.6: net `807.3`, deficit `−799.0`, effective **`+8.3`** — a `> 50` latch correctly stays shut on a row whose net column reads 16× the bar. Two mistakes stack here and both have cost a scoring session: reading `produced` alone (nearly a false FAIL on R25) and reading `net` alone (**a false accusation against Fix 39, see R32**). *This entry previously said "net available" and cited ENG **bauxite** in the same save, whose deficit is −1.0 so the two readings coincide — a rule induced from the one resource where they agree.* Verified 2026-08-13 on `02bd4445`: 36 discriminating latch cells, all siding with net+deficit, none with net.
- **`obsolete=yes` in the `equipments={}` registry is descriptive, never causal.** It does not mean only-one-per-archetype (288 (creator, archetype) groups on `02bd4445` hold 2+ non-obsolete members, up to six) and it does **not** gate production — 7.0% of lines and 9.6% of assigned factories run obsolete-flagged equipment in the final save, rising through the campaign, incl. USA's 68-factory `tank_usa_medium_chassis_3`. Also: the registry carries **no `archetype` field** (resolve it from `common/units/equipment/`), `obsolete=no` never occurs (absence = non-obsolete), and the entry's `parent_id` is a design-variant pointer unrelated to the def-level `parent =`. Never conclude "the AI cannot build X" from this flag.
- **`production_upgrade_desire_offset` is not serialized into a country's `ai` section.** No `ai_strategy` entry in a save carries a negative value at all, and no `id=` is ever an equipment token; three ungated controls (incl. `SOV_dont_build_shit_guns`) are equally invisible. A save can therefore never answer "did this strategy arm" — score it on the production lines instead. The `ai`-section reading used for R34's `invade` suppressions does not generalise to this type.
- **`date="1.1.1.1"` in `technology/technologies` is a tech being researched right now, not a duplicate.** It carries `research_points` and no `level=`, plus a twin under the sibling `technology/slots` block. Grepping names across the whole `technology` section reports a false duplicate for every in-progress tech (ENG 6, GER 6, USA 3 on `02bd4445`, matching `slots` 1:1). Start-granted techs carry a real date (`1936.1.1.12`).
- **`wa_ai_overext_dbg_active` is a monotone months-active counter, not a boolean.** Read it as "months the overextension flag has been set", not "currently overextended".
- **This mod sets `AIRBASE_CAPACITY_MULT = 100`, not vanilla's 200** (`common/defines/05_defines.lua:173`). `wa_ai_uk_air_dbg_capacity`'s ×100 is therefore the true engine figure — do NOT "correct" it to 200. (An earlier version of this file claimed the opposite.)
- **`*_dbg_started` counters over-count where the queue-growth guard is missing.** They increment after `WA_AI_PC_start_project`, which declines silently on `queue_max`/dedup. The theatre air-base strategy has the guard; the UK-hosting one does not. Never read such a counter as "projects built" — cross-check against actual building levels.
- **Absent `*_dbg_*` counters mean "never incremented", not "missing instrumentation".** The `WA_AI_supply_line_dbg_*` and `WA_AI_overext_dbg_mic_redirects_*` families are bare `add_to_variable` counters with no zero-init, so absence is a *result*, not a probe gap. Contrast the `WA_TLM_*` metrics, which ARE zero-initialised (`WA_TLM_core.txt:61-62`) — there, a present-and-0 value is a real zero reading. **Per-counter semantics — what each one counts, what it does not, and its known miscounts — now live in `documentation/WA_TLM_TELEMETRY_SYSTEM.md` §2.1; read the row before scoring any `*_dbg_*` value.**
- **Cloud campaigns reuse filenames and overwrite each other.** Saves are named `YYYY.M_Mon.hoi4`, so a new run silently replaces the previous campaign's files in place, leaving a few stragglers with *later* in-game dates than the current run's final save. Group by `game_unique_id`, never by filename or date.
- **Infantry-equipment stockpiles must be summed by ARCHETYPE, not by the vanilla family.** GER builds no `infantry_equipment_*` at all — its rifles are `ger_inf_*` — while BUL and ROM have **no** national family and *do* appear as `infantry_equipment_N` with `creator="BUL"`/`"ROM"`. A vanilla-only filter therefore looks plausible on the recipients and silently returns ~0 on the donor: it produced "GER holds 549 rifles" against a true **101 033** in one `9a4cd657` pass. Resolve ids through the top-level `equipments={}` registry, select on `archetype = infantry_equipment`, and keep `heavy_infantry_equipment` (the `*_hv_inf_*` families) broken out separately — merging the two is what made two contradictory passes look reconcilable.
- **The `strategic_air` walk must count `count=` only at the direct child depth of `air_wings={`.** A second `count=` family one level deeper (per-wing history/data sub-blocks) inflates plane totals by ~2.2× and dumps most of the fleet onto one arbitrary state — it produced ENG 27 638 instead of 6 144 on the first `9a4cd657` pass.
- **`WA_port_demolished` is not a Nero-only marker.** The generic `scorch_earth` decision (`common/decisions/_generic_decisions.txt:2927`) sets the same flag as the on_action (`100_wa_on_actions.txt:3336`), so it appears on SOV states from 1941.7, Sicily/Salerno 1943.7–9 and Libya 1944.4 — 19 states at 1944.6 and 26 at 1946.3. A whole-map census of the flag cannot detect a ROOT-gate leak (R21 criterion 3).
- **A campaign can pass every delivery probe and still lose the theatre.** `9a4cd657` placed the Mulberries on spec, capped Nero, landed the designed 33 divisions in Normandy — and the beachhead was German again by 1944.9.1. When an outcome item fails, check the *basing* and *reinforcement* items before re-opening the delivery ones.
- **Air wings are NOT nested inside `air_base` blocks.** The layout is `strategic_air → <TAG> = { air_wing_pool = { air_base = { id = N } air_wings = { count= tag= mission={ strategic_region= } } } }`, and the ~600 `air_base` blocks that carry `state=` are **siblings that appear FIRST**. Attributing wings to "the last `air_base` seen" silently dumps the whole world's air force onto one arbitrary state. Basing requires a **two-pass join**: build `air_base id → state`, then map `air_wing_pool.air_base.id → state`. Carrier decks are the `air_base` blocks with `carrier=` and no `state=`. Depths: `air_wings` at 3, `count=`/`tag=` at 4, `strategic_region=` at 5, equipment `amount=` at 6. This is **distinct from, and compounds with**, the note above about counting `count=` only at the direct child depth of `air_wings={`.
- **Idle carrier aircraft do NOT sit in the equipment stockpile.** The unassigned stockpile holds 1-258 carrier planes for every country at every date in `02bd4445` — essentially nothing — while **JAP holds 7,000 carrier aircraft off-deck, bound into air wings parked at land air bases**, against 1,380 deck slots. A "who over-produces carrier aircraft" query keyed on the stockpile alone returns a confident **false negative**; measure `stockpile + off-deck wings` against total deck capacity. Also note GER/ITA/SOV hold small static carrier-plane stocks (61 / 5 / 93) with zero carriers and zero carrier factories ever — inert holdings that pollute any stockpile-keyed query.
- **A decoy `countries={` block appears at depth 3 BEFORE the real top-level one** (seen at line ~1 101 690 of `af003548`'s `1944.8_Aug.hoi4`). A parser that seeks the first `countries={` and reads country blocks from there lands inside a nested structure and returns garbage or nothing; anchor on depth 1. (2026-08-15)
- **Naval-invasion orders are NOT under `countries/<TAG>`.** They live in the top-level `theatres → theatre → orders_group → order_instance` block. A per-country extraction sees no invasion plans at all and reads as "no live plan" — R34 leg 1's plan half and R44 legs 2/3 must be scored from that block. (2026-08-15)
- **A province omits `controller=` when its controller equals the state's controller** — the province-level twin of the state-block rule above. Any per-province controller read (R50's Narvik probe, corridor holdings) must default a missing `controller=` to the enclosing state's controller, which itself defaults to the owner. (2026-08-15)
- **A production line omits `active_factories=` when it is 0.** Summing `active_factories` over lines silently drops the zero-factory lines from the count of *lines*; a line with `requested_factories` and no `active_factories` is a live but unfunded line, not a parse gap. (2026-08-15)
- **`savegame.py tlm` mis-renders `wa_tlm_pc_built_by_type^N` as a ring-buffer series** — the indexed variable is a per-building-type array (index = building type id), not a time series, so the tool's history axis is wrong for it (33/40 vs 7 seen on `af003548`). Read it with `savegame.py var <TAG> "^wa_tlm_pc_built_by_type"` instead. (2026-08-15)

### R38. Front execution reaches puppets and ahistorical enemies, not just the one hard-coded tag (Fix 54)

- **Fix under test:** **Fix 54** — `common/ai_strategy/WA_AI_MILITARY_FACTION_{ALLIES,AXIS,CHINA_FRONT}_FRONT.txt` (six exec/grind blocks) plus `WA_AI_MILITARY_posture_has_execute_target` / `_has_grind_target` in `common/scripted_triggers/WA_AI_MILITARY_posture_triggers.txt`, authored 2026-08-13, uncommitted when this item was written.
- **Root cause:** the posture calculus publishes a verdict **per enemy**, but all six consumers named ONE enemy tag in both `enable` and payload, so anything held by a German puppet was invisible. State 29 Nord-Pas-de-Calais is owned **and** controlled by **RBE**, and RCZ ends `02bd4445` on 4 controlled states — below the posture loop's own `is_major = yes OR num_of_controlled_states > 4` cutoff — so no verdict is ever published for it. The `country_trigger` therefore carries a **"too small for a verdict" branch on the execute side only**; absent variables read as 0, so a too-small enemy can never satisfy the level-2 grind test and the two stay mutually exclusive.
- **Pass, three legs:**
  1. **Resolution leg — and it is the one that matters.** Confirm that `ROOT` still means the strategy owner and `@PREV` the candidate enemy **inside a `country_trigger`**. The parse was boot-tested clean on 2026-08-13, but the probe blocks were `always = no`, so `@PREV` was lexed and **never evaluated**. Evidence of resolution: in a campaign reaching 1944+, at least one state owned or controlled by a German puppet (RBE / RCZ / RBL / RPO / RGR / VIC) in metropolitan France or the Low Countries changes hands to an Allied country. **If NO puppet-held state ever changes hands while Allied divisions are present and posture > 0, the substitution is not resolving and the fix is inert — say which of the two it is before proposing anything.**
  2. **Coverage leg.** States **17 Franche-Comté, 28 Alsace-Lorraine and 32 Isère** are Allied-controlled in at least one monthly sample, having been never-Allied in all 21 samples of `02bd4445`.
  3. **No-regression leg.** The deliberate widening (a faction member now also executes against non-principal enemies where the posture says battles are winnable — ENG vs JAP, say) does not produce order thrash: no major shows its `front_control` target set changing every month, and GER-vs-SOV / CHI-vs-JAP front movement is not slower than `02bd4445` (GER divisions in France, SOV controlled states, JAP controlled states as the coarse check).
- **Probe:** per-state controller walk over the 24 metropolitan French states plus the Low Countries, **monthly from the D-Day month, recording OWNER and CONTROLLER separately** (a state block omits `controller=` when the controller is the owner — default it to owner); `savegame.py army` for the no-regression leg. **No telemetry fingerprint exists for Fix 54 — confirm the build by git ancestry.** Save-native corroboration is weak by construction: file-defined `ai_strategy` blocks do not serialize into saves, and a whole-save scan of `02bd4445` found only 14 distinct string `id=` values, all unit-role tokens, so the strategy itself cannot be observed directly. **Score it on behaviour.**
- **Threshold:** **5** (behavioural — whether a theatre's enemies are reachable is an outcome, and leg 1 is a mechanism question that one campaign could answer by luck).
- **Streak:** 2
- **Status:** PASSED
- **History:**
  - 2026-08-14 · `f9321934` · **FAILED overall — but leg 1, the one that matters, PASSED.** Leg 1 **PASS, and the substitution resolves**: ENG/USA/SOV all publish per-enemy verdicts against German puppets (`wa_ai_military_posture_vs_rbe/_rbl/_rde/_rgr/_rit/_rno/_rpo/_ruk` all present at 1944.6), and **state 29 Nord-Pas-de-Calais — owned and controlled by RBE for the entire war — is French-controlled at 1946.8** (`29:RBE/FRA`). Fix 54 is not inert. Leg 2 **FAIL, 1 of 3**: state 17 Franche-Comté is FRA-controlled at 1946.8 (never-Allied in all 21 samples of `02bd4445`), but **28 Alsace-Lorraine stays German-controlled to the last save** (16 German divisions still in it) and **32 Isère never flips**. Caveat: the campaign ends mid-collapse with the front still moving, so this is a truncation as much as a failure. Leg 3 **PASS**: no order thrash, and the other fronts move (SOV controlled states 145 → 220, GER 99 → 61).
  - 2026-08-14 · `be18f9c7` · PASSED — **All three legs, and the `country_trigger` question is settled affirmatively.** Leg 1 PASS - state **29 Nord-Pas-de-Calais (RBE-owned, RBE-controlled since 1940) flips to FRA control between 1944.11 and 1944.12** and is FRA-owned by 1945.7; RBE's 6 Vlaanderen and 34 Antwerpen flip to FRA at 1945.1 (BEL by 1945.7), RHO's 7 and 35 to HOL by 1945.7. The substitution resolves and is not inert. Leg 2 PASS **3 of 3** - 32 Isere Allied from 1944.10, 17 Franche-Comte from 1945.3, 28 Alsace-Lorraine from 1945.7, all three never-Allied in `02bd4445` and only 1 of 3 landing in `f9321934`. Leg 3 PASS - no order thrash, and the other fronts move faster rather than slower (SOV controlled states 155->227, GER 108->**0**, JAP 58->49).
  - 2026-08-15 · `af003548` · PASSED (legs 1, 2; leg 3 partial) — Leg 1 PASS: state 29 (RBE) flips to FRA 1944.8, reverts 1944.9 (the beachhead rollback), flips again **1945.12** and holds; RBE 6/34 and RHO 7/35 all Allied by 1946.4. Leg 2 PASS: 32/17/28 all Allied at 1946.4. Leg 3: no monthly thrash (SOV `posture_vs_ger` 1 in all 30 months); **CHI-vs-JAP half NOT CHECKED** (JAP controlled 60 → 69, CHI 25 → 23 — score it next time, the direction is adverse).

### R40. The AI does not research the equipment dead-ends it was building (Fix 57)

- **Renumbered R37 → R40 on 2026-08-14.** Two items were opened as `R37` on 2026-08-13 (this one and the posture-hysteresis item for Fix 55). The posture item keeps R37 because it was written first in the file; this one takes the next free number. Any earlier note citing "checklist R37" for the equipment dead-end work means this item.

- **Opened 2026-08-13** after campaign `02bd4445`, as the *third* lever tried on the R30/R33/R35 family. It is the first one that does not try to steer a choice: it removes the option.
- **Fix under test:** Fix 57 — three new Country-layer files, `common/ai_strategy/WA_AI_RESEARCH_COUNTRY_{USA,SOV,JAP}.txt`, using `ai_strategy` type **`research_weight_factor`** at `value = -100` (vanilla `_documentation.md`: *"Factor the ai_will_do value for the specified technology with this … -30 means 30 % decrease"*, so −100 zeroes the weight). WA had **zero** prior uses of this type. Seven entries:
  - **USA** `usa_medium_tank_chassis_4` (T20) and `_5` (T23) at −100; `usa_medium_tank_chassis_3_3` (M4A3E8) at **+100**.
  - **SOV** `sov_medium_tank_chassis_5` (T-43) at −100; `sov_medium_tank_chassis_3_4` (T-34-85) at **+100**.
  - **JAP** `jap_submarine_5` (Ro-101) and `jap_submarine_6` (Ha-201) at −100.
  Chosen over editing `ai_will_do` directly because those blocks are **tool-managed** (`tools/ai_will_do_replacer_all.py`) and a hand-edited `factor = 0` would be silently reverted by the next regeneration. `ai_strategy` is outside that pipeline.
- **The stranding check, which is the load-bearing part of this item.** Suppressing a technology also suppresses everything downstream of it. `usa_medium_tank_chassis_5` (T23) leads to `usa_modern_tank_chassis_1` (M26 Pershing), and the entire USA heavy line (chassis 2–7) hangs off *that* — **15 equipment tokens**. `sov_medium_tank_chassis_5` (T-43) likewise gates the whole SOV modern line (T-44 → T-54, 5 tokens), which `02bd4445` shows is live (13 factories on `tank_sov_modern_chassis_1_2` at 1946.1). Both survive **only** because the technology graph genuinely forks and rejoins: `usa_modern_tank_chassis_1` is also reachable from `usa_medium_tank_chassis_3_3`, and `sov_modern_tank_chassis_1` from `sov_medium_tank_chassis_3_4` — i.e. from the very branches being steered onto. The `+100` boosts exist to keep those now-sole paths prioritised and **must not be removed independently of the suppressions**. JAP is the clean case: `jap_submarine_5` leads only to `jap_submarine_6`, which is a terminal leaf, so the cost is exactly the two coastal hulls.
- **LEVER REPLACED 2026-08-14 — the item now tests Fix 69, not Fix 57.** After `f9321934` failed all five research suppressions, the `research_weight_factor` half of Fix 57 was **removed** and replaced by a hard gate on the technologies themselves: `allow = { NOT = { is_ai = yes } }` on `usa_medium_tank_chassis_4`/`_5` (`common/technologies/armor_usa.txt`), `sov_medium_tank_chassis_5` (`armor_sov.txt`) and `jap_submarine_5`/`_6` (`naval_jap.txt`).
  - **SCOPE CORRECTION 2026-08-14 (campaign `be18f9c7`) — the gate covers EIGHT technologies, not five.** A sweep of `common/technologies/` for `allow = { NOT = { is_ai = yes } }` returns exactly eight hits, **all eight introduced by the single Fix 69 commit `69ac9be36`** (verified by `git log -L` on each site; the folder held no such gate before it). The three this item never listed:
    - **`sov_medium_tank_chassis_4`** (T-34M, `armor_sov.txt:2941`) — the prerequisite of the T-43, gated explicitly rather than relying on its successor's gate, exactly as the commit message says it did for `jap_submarine_6`. **Caveat for a reader of that file: its comment block is a verbatim copy of the T-43's and therefore says "hard-gate T-43" over a tech the same line labels `#T 34M`.** Redundant-but-harmless, not a second dead-end.
    - **`ger_mechanized_infantry_6` and `_7`** (`armor_ger.txt:176`, `:486`), carrying the older in-line comment `#don't produce it, it's crap` rather than a `# Fix 69:` block. **Intentional, not a slip** — commit `69ac9be36` closes with "Also gates ger_mechanized_equipment_6/7 for the AI" (the message names the *equipment*; the techs are `ger_mechanized_infantry_6/7`).
    - **No-stranding re-verified for both additions, from the graph, not inherited.** SOV: `sov_medium_tank_chassis_4`'s only outgoing edge is to `_5`, which is itself gated, so it adds no new suppression surface. GER: the mechanized ladder forks at `ger_mechanized_infantry_1` into `_2` and `_6`, and `_5` has **two** incoming edges — from the gated `_7` **and** from `_4` on the ungated `_2 → _3 → _4` prong — so `_5 → _8 → _9` stays reachable for the AI. Same fork-and-rejoin shape that makes the USA and SOV medium gates safe. This is the construct vanilla uses for the same job — `jungle_warfare` in vanilla `common/technologies/infantry.txt` carries `allow = { always = no }`, commented "only from focus!!!". It gates on `is_ai` alone so it needs **no country tag** and leaves human players able to research these normally. The `+100` boosts on `usa_medium_tank_chassis_3_3` and `sov_medium_tank_chassis_3_4` are **kept** and must not be removed independently of the gate. `WA_AI_RESEARCH_COUNTRY_JAP.txt` is now a comment-only tombstone (JAP had no boost, and nothing downstream needed one). **Why the value was not simply re-tuned:** vanilla's strongest suppression is `-500` (HUN `air_torpedoe_1`), but the token factors the technology's `ai_will_do` score and under the documented formula `-100` already reaches zero — `-500` only clamps there. The leading explanation for the failure is that down-weighting is multiplicative and WA's `ai_will_do` idiom is `factor = 1` plus `factor = 0` gates, so the AI routinely faces an all-zero menu with a spare slot to spend; the dates support it (USA took T20 in the very month it carried `WA_AI_unused_research_slots`, set 1942.9.1, and T23 in the very month it carried it again, set 1943.12.1). **That explanation is a hypothesis, not a measurement** — but it is not on the critical path, because Fix 69 does not depend on it being right.
- **Build fingerprint for Fix 69, and its one weakness.** `allow` is **not** save-visible: nothing in a savegame says a technology was gated. Confirm the build by git ancestry of the Fix 69 commit (`69ac9be36`), then read the research leg — on a build carrying Fix 69 the eight techs must be *absent*, and absence is the whole signal. This means **a FAILED research leg is unambiguous but a PASSED one is only as trustworthy as the ancestry check**, so do the ancestry check first and say so in the evidence line.
- **Pass, three legs:**
  1. **Research leg.** USA never researches `usa_medium_tank_chassis_4`/`_5`; SOV never researches `sov_medium_tank_chassis_4`/`_5`; JAP never researches `jap_submarine_5`/`_6`; GER never researches `ger_mechanized_infantry_6`/`_7`. **Eight techs, not five** — see the scope correction above; the GER pair and `sov_medium_tank_chassis_4` ship in the same commit and fail the same way, so score them together even though the first five are what the item was opened for. Any of them appearing with a `level=` in the country's `technology/technologies` is an outright FAIL of the lever itself.
  2. **Substitution leg (the point of the fix).** USA's `land_medium_tank` lines resolve to `tank_usa_medium_chassis_4` (M4A2) or `_4_2` (M4A3E8); SOV's to `tank_sov_medium_chassis_3_4` (T-34-85); JAP's `naval_submarine` line to `jap_submarine_hull_4`/`_3`. This is R30/R33/R35's design leg, re-scored here for the countries Fix 54 covers.
  3. **No-stranding leg (the regression this could cause).** USA still reaches `usa_modern_tank_chassis_1` and at least one `usa_heavy_tank_chassis_*`; SOV still reaches `sov_modern_tank_chassis_1`. **Later than before is expected and is not a failure** — pre-fix the USA reached the modern line via T23 (researched 1943.5.25) and now reaches it via M4A3E8 (1944.2.7 in `02bd4445`), so a delay of roughly 9 months is the accepted cost. A campaign where the USA never reaches modern or heavy tanks at all IS a failure and means the `+100` boost is not enough.
- **Probe:** save-native, no new instrumentation. (a) Research leg: dump `technology` for USA/SOV/JAP/GER and report presence + date of each of the **eight** gated techs (`usa_medium_tank_chassis_4`, `usa_medium_tank_chassis_5`, `sov_medium_tank_chassis_4`, `sov_medium_tank_chassis_5`, `jap_submarine_5`, `jap_submarine_6`, `ger_mechanized_infantry_6`, `ger_mechanized_infantry_7`), **scoping to `technologies={}` and selecting on the presence of `level=`** — an in-progress tech carries `date="1.1.1.1"` and a twin under the sibling `slots={}` block, which a whole-section grep misreads as a duplicate. (b) Substitution leg: resolve each line's `equipment_variant_index` through the top-level `equipments={}` registry (a name grep on `production` returns MIO names). (c) No-stranding leg: same `technology` dump, reporting the research dates of `usa_modern_tank_chassis_1`, any `usa_heavy_tank_chassis_*` and `sov_modern_tank_chassis_1`, compared against `02bd4445`'s baseline.
- **Threshold:** 3 (narrow — every leg is a discrete "was this researched / which chassis is the line on" fact).
- **Retire together with R35 legs 1–2 if all clear** (R30 and R33 were merged away on 2026-08-16 — R30 into R41, R33 into this item as the JAP submarine leg below). Note in the retirement that the durable rule — *check what a technology leads to before suppressing it; the only research lever that binds for the AI is the hard `allow = { NOT = { is_ai = yes } }` gate, `research_weight_factor` does not* — belongs in `wa-lessons-learned`.
- **JAP submarine leg — absorbed from R33 (JAP builds ocean-going submarines, Fix 48) on 2026-08-16.** R33 scored its two post-hard-gate passes (`be18f9c7`, `af003548`, streak 2/3) on this item's lever, not on Fix 48: `jap_submarine_5` (Ro-101) and `_6` (Ha-201) are gated at `naval_jap.txt:3941` / `:4005`, so the coastal hulls can no longer be selected. Leg carried forward, scored here from now on: JAP's `naval_submarine` line carries **zero factories on `jap_submarine_hull_5`/`hull_6`** at every save (registry-resolved via `equipments={}` — a name grep on `production` returns MIO idea names), Hei `hull_4` from 1938.9 on. Record it carried: Fix 48's design ladder 10/60/120/140/5/3 (`JAP_naval.txt`, plus the inert GER Type XXIII demotion in `GER_naval.txt`) changed nothing on `bec4d829`, `02bd4445`, `f9321934` — the line flipped to coastal Ro-101 at 1942.11–1943.10 and to Ha-201 from 1944.5–1944.12 in every one, exactly as pre-fix. GER's half is preventive only: GER never runs a `naval_submarine` line (routed to `cruiser_submarine` by `WA_AI_CONFIG_focus_on_cruiser_submarines`), confirmed on six campaigns — do not score it. **Open question carried, candidate for its own item:** the JAP `naval_submarine` role sat at **exactly 1 factory for 95–100 months** on both passing campaigns, so the fleet leg (Otsu/Hei becoming the largest classes) is a dead signal — the role ratio, not hull selection, is the real question. Last R33 entry, verbatim: `af003548` · PASSED — 19 samples 1940.6 → 1946.3: only `jap_submarine_hull_4` (Hei) ever carries factories, zero on `hull_5`/`hull_6`; fleet plain subs 48 → 57 vs 48 baseline, csubs flat at 8.
- **Streak:** 2
- **Status:** PASSED
- **History:**

Streak stays 0.
  - **What this establishes about the layer, and it is the point of the campaign.** `research_weight_factor` was the third distinct layer tried on this family and the first that removes an option instead of steering a choice. It did not remove it. The design layer (`ai_equipment` priority — Fixes 46/48/50) and the production-line layer (`production_upgrade_desire_offset` — Fix 51) were already settled negatively; the research layer now joins them. **One thing is better here than with Fix 51:** a researched technology carries a `level=` and a date, so this lever's *outcome* is fully save-observable and needs no isolated in-game test to convict — unlike Fix 51, where the block's arming is not serialized at all. What is still unseparated is *why*: `research_weight_factor` factors the technology's `ai_will_do` **weight**, so a −100 factor is only a veto if the resulting weight is what actually gates selection. ### R39. AIFC only hands a schwerpunkt to an army that has one (Fix 56)

- **Fix under test:** Fix 56 - `common/scripted_triggers/WA_AI_AIFC_triggers.txt`, one term added to `WA_AI_AIFC_should_select_sector`, plus the mirrored constant in `common/scripted_effects/WA_AI_AIFC_core.txt` (authored 2026-08-13, uncommitted when this item was written). New `@WA_AI_AIFC_MIN_DIV = 12`, applied as `has_army_size = { size > @WA_AI_AIFC_MIN_DIV }`. **Root cause:** every eligibility term in that trigger measured TERRITORY, never capability - `num_of_controlled_states > 2` is satisfied by a Reichskommissariat with nine states and no army. On `02bd4445` at 1944.9, **10 of 28 sector holders could not have executed a schwerpunkt**, four of them (IPI, RBE, RGR, RNO) with **zero deployed divisions** on 2-4 state corridors. Same class of error Fix 43 removed from `surrender_progress` in the same trigger. `has_army_size` was already present there but only as the second arm of the Fix 43 `OR`, where it can only ever widen the gate.
- **This is a PERFORMANCE fix, not a behavioural one - do not score it as if front behaviour should change.** The wasted sectors were verified inert: Layer 4 writes only ROOT-scoped `force_concentration_*` (which modulate a surplus a 0-division army does not have), armour steering is separately gated on `has_army_size = { size > 2 type = armor }` (confirmed empirically - no small holder carried `wa_ai_aifc_armor_boost`), and AIFC writes no `front_control` / `invade` / `*_unit_request`. The saving is the weekly work: a country with **no** sector re-selects **every week**, and the Fix 28 expeditionary fallback it reaches is an **uncapped** `every_country / every_controlled_state / every_neighbor_state` walk over ~800 states. FRA ran it ~250 consecutive weeks on `02bd4445` for zero output; ~32 tags were in that bucket at 1944.9.
- **Pass, three legs:**
  1. **Exclusion leg.** No country holding `wa_ai_aifc_sector_anchor` has fewer than 13 deployed divisions, at any sampled save. Zero tolerance - the term is a hard conjunction, so a single violation means it did not take.
  2. **No-major-regression leg.** Every major that held a sector on `02bd4445` still holds one at the equivalent date: GER, SOV, ENG, USA, JAP, ITA, CHI, PRC. **This is the leg that matters** - the measured margin at the chosen threshold is thin on the losing side (nearest survivor on the `> 4` measurement was MEN at 11, and 12 deliberately drops it), so a major losing its sector means the constant is wrong, not the approach.
  3. **Age-cycle leg (inherited from retired R1).** On the countries that still hold sectors, `wa_ai_aifc_sector_age` still cycles 1-5 and is not pinned at 1. Fix 56 must not have broken what Fix 31 fixed.
- **Probe:** whole-country sweep per save - for every tag, read `wa_ai_aifc_sector_anchor` / `_sector_age` presence via `savegame.py var <TAG> "^wa_ai_aifc_"` and pair it with the deployed division count from `savegame.py army <TAG> <saves>` (units-scoped; `wa_tlm_comp_div_total` is the authority, `num_armies_for_training` overstates during losses). Sample at least 1942.6, 1944.6, 1944.9 and one late save. **The `02bd4445` pre-fix baseline for direct comparison is in the R1 retirement note**: 23 / 25 / 28 / 23 sector holders at 1942.6 / 1944.6 / 1944.9 / 1946.1, of which 8 / 6 / 8 / 5 held fewer than 10 divisions. No new instrumentation - the probe is save-native.
- **Threshold:** 3 (narrow - the pass criterion is a discrete "does any under-strength country hold a sector", a counting fact off one sweep, not an emergent outcome).
- **Threshold-choice note, carried deliberately:** the blast radius was measured at `> 4` (10 of 28 holders dropped at 1944.9, no major affected, 7-division margin to the nearest survivor). **12 is a user decision (2026-08-13) and is tighter than what was measured** - it additionally drops MEN (11 divisions) and the marginal FRS (6), ITL (6) and YUN (8). No major falls below it in any sampled save (smallest at-war major reading is ITA at 19). **Small genuine belligerents were never measured at this bar** - FIN in the Winter War, GRE, YUG. If a campaign shows one of those losing a sector it could have used, relax the constant rather than removing the term.
- **Known adjacent defect, NOT fixed by Fix 56 and not this item's to score:** annexed tags are never iterated by the weekly on_action (`common/on_actions/WA_AI_misc_on_actions.txt:112-116` wraps the call in `is_ai = yes` + `has_capitulated = no`), so `WA_AI_AIFC_core.txt:64-68`'s "not eligible -> clear sector" branch is unreachable for exactly the population it was written for. Result: **frozen stale sectors on dead tags** - DEN reads `anchor=58 age=1 corr=4 eq=0.92235` byte-identical in all seven `02bd4445` saves from 1941.6 to 1946.1; SHA, SIC, PER and BRM likewise. It means leg 1 must count only tags with `num_of_controlled_states > 0`, or it will fail on corpses Fix 56 cannot reach.
  - **"Harmless (the tags do not exist)" was WRONG, corrected 2026-08-14 on `f9321934`.** The gate is `has_capitulated = no`, not "is annexed", and a **capitulated-but-alive** tag hits it just as hard. FRA carries `capitulated=yes` (in the country block's `diplomacy={}` sub-block, written only when true) in **every** save of `f9321934` while owning 60 states and controlling 50, so it is never iterated, its May-1940 sector (`anchor=34` Wallonie, `age=4`) freezes for the whole campaign, and it shows up under leg 1 as a live 5-division sector holder that Fix 56 cannot reach. Leg 1 must therefore also excuse tags with `capitulated=yes`, or the exclusion must move from the on_action to the effect.
- **Streak:** 2
- **Status:** PASSED
- **History:**
  - 2026-08-14 · `f9321934` · **FAILED on all three legs. Sector holders did fall 28 → 19 at 1944.9, so the term took — but it took on the wrong tags.** Leg 1 **FAIL**: three holders sit below 13 deployed divisions at 1944.9 — SHA (0) and SIC (0) are annexed corpses the item already excuses, but **FRA holds a sector with 5 deployed divisions while controlling 50 states and being very much alive**, which is exactly the case `@WA_AI_AIFC_MIN_DIV = 12` was written to exclude. Leg 2 **FAIL, and this is the serious one**: **ENG holds no sector at all from 1944.6 through 1946.4** (`wa_ai_aifc_sector_anchor` absent in the 1944.6 / 1944.9 / 1945.6 / 1946.4 samples; present 1942.6 anchor 273, 1943.6 anchor 663, and again 1946.8 anchor 47) — i.e. the Royal Army loses its schwerpunkt for **precisely the 23 months of the Normandy stall**. ENG ran 125–131 divisions throughout, so `has_army_size > 12` is NOT the cause; some other term in `WA_AI_AIFC_should_select_sector` is rejecting ENG and it needs diagnosing before this item can be re-scored. Leg 3 **FAIL**: FRA's sector is **frozen** — `anchor=34, age=4` byte-identical in all seven samples 1942.6 → 1946.8, never re-selected, on a tag that is alive and iterated. This is the stale-sector defect the item's own "known adjacent defect" note describes, but on a **live** tag, not an annexed one, so the `num_of_controlled_states > 0` exclusion does not cover it. GER / SOV / USA age-cycle normally (1–4).
  - 2026-08-14 · `f9321934` · **DIAGNOSIS of legs 2 and 3 (same session, no re-score — the verdict stays FAILED, streak 0).**
    - **Leg 2 is NOT an eligibility failure. `WA_AI_AIFC_should_select_sector` passes for ENG in all six sampled saves** — AI in every save (`player_countries={BHU}`), one war (vs GER, from 1939.9.2), `capitulated` absent throughout, 74 controlled states (76 at 1946.8), 116–143 divisions. The Fix 43 escape hatch (`states > 20 AND army > 40`) carries the surrender `OR` on its own, so `surrender_progress` is moot — and it is **not serialized in a save at all**, so no probe can ever read it (record that before someone tries). ENG therefore entered `WA_AI_AIFC_select_sector` **every week** and the selection returned **zero candidates**.
    - **The rejecting term is `has_capitulated = no` applied to the ALLY in the Fix 28 expeditionary fallback** (`common/scripted_effects/WA_AI_AIFC_helpers.txt:95`, mirrored in the validity check at `WA_AI_AIFC_core.txt:210`). Native collection found nothing because **ENG controls zero continental-European states for the whole window** — its 74-state set is the British Isles, the colonies, and the Italian North Africa it *annexed* between 1943.6 and 1944.6 (273, 448–451, 661–663, 708 all flip ITL→ENG **owner**), none of which touches an enemy-held state once ITA changes sides. The fallback then walks `every_country` filtered on `has_capitulated = no`, and **every large ENG continental concentration from 1944.5 on sits on FRA-controlled soil**: 735 French Alpes (16 div, 1944.5), then 15 Lower Normandy (15 div at 1945.6, 17 at 1946.4) and 1 Corsica (9 div). **FRA is `capitulated=yes` in every save** while controlling 50 states, so the walk skips it, `_aifc_cand` stays empty, `_aifc_best_id = 0` and the `else` branch calls `WA_AI_AIFC_clear_sector` — for ~118 consecutive weeks. The other allied controllers ENG stacks >2 divisions behind are all in dead ends: DWI 309 Suriname, UKT 547 Kenya / 1099 N. Tanganyika, GRE 164 Dodecanese (whose *only* map adjacencies are to neutral Turkey — `7003;9941`, `10007;10122`).
    - **Both ends of the window confirm it, and both are native-contact events, not fallback events.** ENG's sector was *always* native: 1942.6 anchor **273 Libyan Desert** and 1943.6 anchor **663 Cyrenaica** were ITL-held next to ENG-controlled Egypt; 1944.3 anchor **157 Abruzzo** and 1944.4 anchor **986 Puglia** were next to ENG-controlled **117 Campania** (ITA-owned, ENG-controlled, 7 ENG divisions). Loss month is **May 1944**, one month after Italy surrenders (1944.4.14) and the month ENG hands over its Italian holdings and re-embarks — the 1944.5 save is the only one of the four with ENG divisions on unmapped (sea) provinces, **19 of them**. Recovery is 1946.8 and is a single-variable confirmation: ENG acquires **187 Aegean Islands**, whose province 3401 carries the map's *only* special adjacency to Thessaly's province 7127 (`3401;7127;sea` in `map/adjacencies.csv`) — and the new anchor is **47 Thessaly**. The sector returns exactly when ENG controls a state of its own next to an enemy, and not before.
    - **Leg 3 (FRA frozen) is the same word at a different site: `has_capitulated = no` on ROOT in `common/on_actions/WA_AI_misc_on_actions.txt:115`.** FRA is capitulated all campaign, is never iterated, and its May-1940 sector never ages, never clears, never re-selects. That also disposes of leg 1's live-tag violation: FRA's 5-division sector is a **pre-Fix-56 fossil Fix 56 can never reach**, not Fix 56 failing to take.
    - **Performance finding, on top of the behavioural one.** Because eligibility *passes*, ENG paid the full body — the native walk over 74 controlled states × their neighbours, then the **uncapped** `every_country / every_controlled_state / every_neighbor_state` fallback over the 33-member Allies faction — every week from 1944.5 to 1946.7 for zero output. This is the exact cost Fix 56 was written to remove, on a tag Fix 56 does not touch.
    - **Causal verdict on the Normandy stall: RED HERRING.** (a) AIFC issues no attack orders — it writes only `force_concentration_*`; the execute orders came from the posture layer and sat at level 1 the whole time. (b) Layers 1–2 are **not** sector-gated, so ENG kept `force_concentration_factor` ≈ 15 % base + 3 (british_professionalism) + up to 24 (armour/operations tracks); only Layer 4 is gated on `WA_AI_AIFC_has_sector`, so with no sector ENG still concentrated, just on the engine's own target scoring instead of a scripted axis. (c) **The sign is wrong**: a Normandy-anchored sector publishes +50 front factor there, −50 elsewhere, +50 target weight on the corridor and −99 outside it — it would have pushed *more* divisions into the state that already held 42→79 Allied divisions against ~11 defenders. Over-concentration was the pathology; the missing sector mitigated it. (d) Only real loss is the scripted armour steering (+400/−150), absent for the window (`wa_ai_aifc_armor_boost` gone in lockstep) — but ENG keeps a static `front_armor_score` +100 vs GER/ITA/ITL (`WA_AI_MILITARY_COUNTRY_ENG_FRONT.txt:730-756`), so the bias survived at a lower value. **Demote AIFC from the causal list in the campaign headline; keep it as a real defect worth fixing on its own merits.**
    - **Two fixes shipped the same session, both tracked by R42 — do not re-score legs 2 or 3 against them here.** **Fix 65** (`WA_AI_AIFC_helpers.txt` section 1b + the lockstep validity mirror in `WA_AI_AIFC_core.txt`) removes the ally-side `has_capitulated = no`, addressing leg 2. **Fix 68** (`WA_AI_misc_on_actions.txt`) lifts `WA_AI_AIFC_update_sector` out of the `is_ai = yes` + `has_capitulated = no` Priority-Construction block into its own `is_ai = yes` block, making the effect's "not eligible → clear sector" branch reachable for capitulated tags and addressing leg 3. The `has_capitulated = no` in the PC block itself is untouched — it is load-bearing for the other six calls in there.

Leg 2 PASS and **the serious `f9321934` regression is gone** - ENG holds a sector at **every** month 1944.4->1945.7 (anchors 162->109->785->22->23->162->31->104->8->28->997), alongside GER, SOV, USA, JAP, CHI, ITA, PRC. Leg 3 PASS - ages cycle 1-4 on every holder, no frozen sector anywhere. Note for the next scoring: ENG loses its sector at 1945.8, 1946.4 and 1946.5, consistent with the diagnosed mechanism (no ENG-controlled state adjacent to an enemy once the European war ends), so not a regression - but it shows the fix does not cover the own-soil-adjacency half of the selection problem.

Streak 2 of 3.

### R36. The AI puts its escorts to sea (convoy escort is manned, screens are not parked)

- **Opened 2026-08-13** from a user report ("ENG/USA font-ils assez de convoy escort? j'ai l'impression qu'une grande partie des escorteurs reste au port"), which the `bec4d829` saves confirm outright. The pathology is **not** production and **not** admiral supply; it is mission assignment. Opened without a fix; **Fix 71 was authored the same session** and is now the fix under test.
- **Fix under test (current): Fix 53**, authored 2026-08-13 after `02bd4445` proved Fix 71's mechanism but not its outcome. Two layers, deliberately separable by the probe below.
  - **Fix 53a — script layer, Allied corridor users only.** (i) `WA_AI_NAVAL_FACTION_ALLIES.txt`: the north-corridor block ended with `naval_mission_threshold MISSION_CONVOY_ESCORT value = 5000`, **sign-inverted against the block's own purpose**. A *positive* threshold RAISES the score bar a mission must clear — vanilla says so in its own comment (`JAP.txt:970`, `value = 150 #puts our threshold at 250`, inside a block named `conserve_fuel_for_usa_fight` that exists to stop escorting), and WA's own DEFAULT block drives patrol/strike *negative* under the comment "Fixes the AI not putting out its strike force and patrols". So WA's Atlantic escort plan ended with "and never escort": +5000 on an engine base of 100, **50× the type's documented ceiling** (`WA_AI_MILITARY_SYSTEM.md:106` gives −100..+100), undocumented in the block's own header (which declares "range: 0 to +2000, Additive per region" and describes `naval_dominance` only). Pre-Fix-52 it reached CAN/RAJ/AST/NZL — R36's "27 hulls, mostly at anchor" — and **Fix 71 handed it to ENG and USA**. Now split into its own block `WA_AI_NAVAL_FACTION_ALLIES_convoy_escort_threshold` at **−100** (the documented floor, putting the bar at 0), because a country-wide mission threshold is not a per-region rule. (ii) `WA_AI_NAVAL_DEFAULT.txt` `legacy_AI_naval_mission_fix`: de-duplicated. Each id was written twice, once quoted once bare; **both spellings are legal** (vanilla uses bare at `ENG.txt:1379`, quoted at `JAP.txt:970`) and the type is **Additive**, so the pairs summed — strike force was running at −1000 and patrol at **−2000**, double the intended values. Patrol's bar was therefore −1900 against escort's +100: the asymmetry behind "88 USA ships on patrol across its own convoy coasts against 51 on escort". Intended single values kept as-is, so this removes an artefact without re-tuning the two missions.
  - **Fix 53b — defines layer, global (every country, including the JAP/GER control cases).** `common/defines/05_defines.lua`: **all four convoy-escort SCORE terms were zeroed** while patrol kept its full vanilla score (WA's patrol overrides are commented out, so vanilla's `NAVAL_MISSION_PATROL_NEAR_OWNED = 500` / `_NEAR_CONTROLLED = 140` apply). Restored to vanilla 1.18: `NAVAL_MISSION_ESCORT_NEAR_OWNED` 0 → **300**, `NAVAL_MISSION_ESCORT_NEAR_CONTROLLED` 0 → **200**, `CONVOY_ESCORT_SCORE_FROM_CONVOYS` 0 → **15**, `CONVOY_ESCORT_MUL_FROM_NO_CONVOYS` 0 → **0.02**. With every term at 0 the *only* remaining escort score source was transient region convoy danger, which is why escort tracked recent sinkings and nothing else, swung 20–140 hulls month to month, and regressed late-war as raiding fell off. Also `MAX_MISSION_PER_TASKFORCE[CONVOY ESCORT]` 1 → **4** (vanilla): at 1 an escort task force may cover a *single* region, so covering N regions costs N task forces against a hard cap of 0.7 of all screen task forces — that ceiling **is** the coverage leg (ENG 15 of 42, USA 5 of 63). Escort was the most region-hungry mission in the mod; patrol and convoy raiding both sit at 1.5.
  - **Fix 54 — defines layer, global. The screen-reallocation valve.** Added after the 1942.10 paired probe (below) showed Fix 53's pre-registered escalation condition met. `MIN_SUPPORT_SHIP_RATIO` 1.0 → **0.7** and `MIN_SUPPORT_SHIP_TO_SPARE` 3.0 → **1.0**, both vanilla. These are **one mechanism, set in one commit** (`8b736abd1`, 2024-12-20) and they form a **one-way valve**: fill a task force's screens to 1.0 of its desired complement, release them only above 3.0. Vanilla's band is 0.7 → 1.0; WA's was 1.0 → 3.0, so any screen that ever joined a strike, patrol or parked task force was captured permanently. That is what ENG's 282-340-ship admiral-less holding fleet is *made of*, and it is the first explanation on file for the **late-war regression** — rebuilt strike forces absorb the escorts and never give them back. Restoring only one half leaves the ratchet. Blast radius is bounded to reallocation between a country's own task forces: no mission scoring, threshold, region assignment or production path is touched. **Signature that separates it from Fix 53:** 53 moves escort *score* (escort task forces appear and grow), 54 moves the *stock* (Fleet 3 shrinks, escort share climbs toward the engine's own 0.3 floor) — and it should be visible on GER and JAP too.
  - **Still NOT changed, and now the sole remaining candidate:** `NAVY_PREFERED_MAX_SIZE` = 80 (vanilla 25) — **the direct answer to "what governs how many region-holding fleets the AI maintains"**: missions and regions are fleet-level, so fleet count *is* the number of region-holding slots, and tripling the merge target thirds it. Held back because it is a different mechanism from the valve (fleet count vs screen flow) and reshapes every navy in the game. Also unchanged and noted: `MISSING_CONVOYS_BOOST_FACTOR` 0.0 vs vanilla 50.0 (production-side, comment line visibly mangled by an old merge), `MIN_MAIN_SHIP_TO_SPARE` 1.0 vs vanilla 0.7 (capital half of the same valve, same commit — restore it too if capitals show the same hoarding), `REGION_CONVOY_DANGER_DAILY_DECAY` 5 vs vanilla 2, and the known USA 244 `avoid = 2000` residual below.
  - **Fix 59 — script layer, id corrections only (2026-08-14). No tuning, no gating, no new behaviour.** An id audit of the three Atlantic-relevant naval files against `map/strategicregions/` and `map/definition.csv` found four entries that could not do what they said. (i) **`atlantic_south_corridor` had `naval_dominance id = 237` commented "Azores Region"; 237 is the Far South Pacific.** Strategic-region ids match their `STRATEGICREGION_<n>` loc keys for 381 of 383 regions — the only two exceptions are this pair (`237-Azores Region.txt` declares `id = 112`, `112-Far South Pacific.txt` declares `id = 237`; the filename prefix is the loc key, not the id). So the south corridor had a dominance term in the South Pacific, none on the real Azores, and it stacked against the `avoid = 2000` that `legacy_ENG_ignore_japan_until_germany_is_dealt_with` correctly puts on 237. Now `id = 112`. (ii) **`atlantic_north_corridor` `naval_dominance id = 36` (Greenland) removed** — land-only, inert; see the leg-0 correction above. Its surrounding waters (44, 244, 50, 247) were already in the list, so the corridor loses nothing. (iii) **`USA_legacy_USA_operation_torch_landing` `naval_avoid_region id = 220` removed** — region 220 is Chahar, 24 land provinces. (iv) **`strike_force_home_base id = 234` (Northern Borneo, land) removed** from `USA_legacy_USA_democracy_on_JAP`, and the matching dead avoid on 234 from the ENG blanket list. No replacement id was invented for (iii)/(iv). Also in the same pass, comments that named the wrong body of water were corrected (173 is the Norwegian Trench not the "Eastern North Sea", 174 the Voring Plateau not the "Norwegian Coast", 55 the Newfoundland Sea not the "East Coast", 93 the Banda Sea not the "Java sea" — the Java Sea is 309 and is listed separately, 101 the Bay of Bengal, 71 the Central Indian Ocean, 99 the East Indian Ocean, 218 the Ionian Sea, 29 the Strait of Sicily). **What this changes for scoring:** the Azores (112) now carry a real +70 dominance for corridor users, so leg 0's south set becomes testable where it previously was not; and the USA now holds dominance 70 and an `avoid = 2000` on 112 simultaneously in the Torch and invade-France windows (`USA_legacy_USA_operation_torch_landing`, `USA_legacy_USA_invade_the_axis_avoid_regions_france`) — that is a Country-layer wall queued for the follow-up pass, not a reason to revert the id. **Build fingerprint: none in telemetry** — confirm by git ancestry, exactly as for Fix 71.
  - **Fix 60 — script layer, Allied corridor users only (2026-08-14). Three additions, all in `WA_AI_NAVAL_FACTION_ALLIES.txt`, all additive and independently falsifiable.** Ships together with Fix 59; 59 is id truth, 60 is the plan. (i) **The pull half of the corridor pair.** The Atlantic plan only ever pushed: `naval_dominance` on the corridor, `+1000` avoid on the gaps, and *nothing* pulling fleets onto the corridor — so the only `naval_avoid_region` values a corridor region ever carried were the legacy Country walls pointing the wrong way (`+2000` on 244 from `USA_legacy_USA_smart_pacific_fleet` **and again** from the ungated `ENG_legacy_ENG_atlantic_sea_zone_corridors`, `+2000` on 57 from `USA_legacy_USA_smart_atlantic_fleet`). Added `naval_avoid_region = -1000` on all 16 corridor regions (7 north, 9 south) — negative is an attraction, WA's own idiom (`ENG_protect_home` −10000, USA Torch −2000), and −1000 mirrors the +1000 on the gaps so the pair is symmetric. **Sized for the world after the walls come down, not against them**: 244 still nets +3000 for the USA until the follow-up pass retires them, so if the corridor leg moves for ENG and not for the USA, that asymmetry is the reason and it is not evidence against the pull. (ii) **`gibraltar_approaches`** — `naval_dominance` 70 plus the −1000 pull on 249 Alboran and 68 Algerian Coast, gated on Gibraltar's controller not being at war with us and on the Axis not holding Spain/Morocco, i.e. the strict complement of `spanish_waters_unsafe`. The southern route previously had **no** "hold this open" term past the Iberian Coast — only a contingency for when it was already lost. Note ENG sums to 170 on 249 (its Country Gibraltar block writes 100); legal under Additive, out of the type's 0-100 band, and the Country line is on the promotion list rather than deleted here so ENG's Gibraltar behaviour does not change on two axes at once. (iii) **`atlantic_home_base_eastern` / `_western`** — the first Faction-layer `strike_force_home_base` in the mod. 43 Western Approaches was claimed by nobody; 55/54 are gated on `capital_scope = { is_on_continent = north_america }` (archetype, not a tag list) and yield to the Country owner through `WA_AI_MILITARY_country_owns_sfhb_55` / `_54`. **Those two ownership triggers are situational, not the bare `tag = USA` the rest of `WA_AI_MILITARY_PHASE5_ownership_triggers.txt` uses**, because the owning block is itself conditional — a bare tag would have locked the USA out of the anchorages in exactly the historical case this fix serves, which is the Fix 71 failure repeated. **This is the item's most direct answer to the parked-escort reading:** `USA_legacy_USA_protect_eastern_Waters` requires Britain to have fallen or the USA to be fighting outside the Allies, so **in the historical case the USN has no Atlantic strike force home base for the entire war** — the same shape as the four USN escort task forces found in the Gulf of Aden, the Solomon Sea, the Algerian coast and the central NE Pacific. **Falsifiable prediction, score these separately:** leg 0 should move for both navies (pull + home base), and the USA should hold ≥1 north-corridor region *before* any Country-layer wall is touched; if leg 0 moves but legs 1-3 do not, region assignment is not the escort bottleneck and the remaining candidate is `NAVY_PREFERED_MAX_SIZE`. **Main regression risk, stated up front:** `strike_force_home_base` broadens where capital ships may base, so watch ENG's Home Fleet — if ENG's North Sea/Channel presence drops after this build, 43 is the suspect and the eastern block is separable from everything else in the fix. **Build fingerprint: none in telemetry** — confirm by git ancestry.

Every removal below has a named owner at another layer — none of it is a loosening. **(a) Two ungated blocks in the ENG country file, deleted.** `ENG_legacy_ENG_azores_safe` and `ENG_legacy_ENG_atlantic_sea_zone_corridors` carried **no `allowed` block at all**, so despite their names they applied to everyone `is_in_faction_with = ENG` — and the second did not even exclude the USA. Re-homing, not restoring the leak. **(e) Two consequential repairs inside `azores_fallen`, found by summing the net per region rather than reading the diff.** Fix 60's −1000 corridor pull silently cancelled it: at its old `value = 1000` on 112 the net was **exactly zero**, so the moment the Azores fell the deterrent vanished entirely instead of shrinking — raised to 2000 for a net +1000. And its `NOT = { tag = USA }` was removed: it paired with the now-deleted `azores_safe`, and with the corridor pulling every user toward 112 the USN was the one navy being drawn into hostile Azores waters with no deterrent. `WA_AI_NAVAL_FACTION_ALLIES.txt` now contains **no `tag =` gating term of any kind**. **Post-fix net avoid on the corridor, verified by summing all writers across all four naval files:** 244 **−1000**, 57 **−1000**, 112 −1000 (safe) / +1000 (fallen), 47 −1000 (safe) / +1000 (Azores lost) / +2000..+4000 (Axis in Iberia), gaps +1000 each. **Two residuals left deliberately, both documented in place:** ENG's Country `naval_dominance id = 249 value = 100` (ENG reads 170 on Alboran with `gibraltar_approaches`; legal under Additive, out of band, queued for promotion rather than retired in the same commit that introduced the Faction block) and ENG's `med_is_lost` +2000 on 68 (a legitimate situational override — if Egypt is gone, do not push into the Algerian coast — netting +1000 against the Gibraltar pull). **Build fingerprint: none in telemetry** — confirm by git ancestry.
