Structural; no new behaviour intended, three deliberate widenings called out below.** Fixes 59-61 corrected the ids, built the plan and removed what cancelled it; this one removes the duplication that made the naval files unreadable. **Five blocks promoted into `WA_AI_NAVAL_FACTION_ALLIES.txt` and deleted from the Country files:** `war_against_axis` → `italian_coastal_seas` (existed **twice**, byte-identical payload and enable, only the `allowed` tag differing — that ENG+USA pair is exactly `WA_AI_CONFIG_MILITARY_is_anglo_major`); `german_bight` (also twice); `ENG_USA_baltic_trap` (a two-tag `allowed` list, and its **name** already said it was a faction rule); and the two `dday_avoid_regions_*` blocks, whose `allowed` was an **eight-tag list** — ENG/FRA/USA/RAJ/CAN/SAF/AST/NZL — which after Fix 61 added FRA and SAF is now exactly `WA_AI_CONFIG_MILITARY_is_allied_naval_corridor_user`. Both lists were answering the same question. **Three widenings, each strictly additive and each a design-principle-1 repair:** (i) `GER = { has_war_with = ENG }` dropped from both D-Day blocks — the state-controller checks already establish that the coast is held by someone at war with us, and the Germany-vs-Britain test added nothing on the historical path while silently disabling the rule on every other one (Italian-held French coast, Britain already out, a Germany that never went fascist); (ii) `german_bight` asked whether Hamburg's controller was in faction with *itself* (`is_in_faction_with = ENG` in one copy, `= USA` in the other), rewritten as the generic "not one of us"; its USA-only `date < 1942.3.1` blanket was dropped as redundant, since before that date the ownership test returns the same answer in any timeline where the block can fire; (iii) `is_in_faction_with = ENG` → `WA_AI_MILITARY_is_allies_member`, which resolves to faction-with-ENG **or** faction-with-USA, so the rules survive Britain's fall. **One block on the promotion list deliberately NOT promoted:** `ENG_legacy_ENG_try_to_avoid_the_med` gated by exclusion (everyone but GRE/YUG/TUR/ITA/USA) and so leaked faction-wide like the two blocks Fix 61 deleted — but the USA exclusion is load-bearing, not incidental: the USN is the navy that must sail **into** the Ionian to invade Sicily, and promoting it would have re-introduced a `tag =` term into the Faction file that Fix 61 had just finished removing. "Everyone except the invader and the Med locals" is a residue, not an archetype, so it stays Country-layer and is now gated `tag = ENG`. **Consequence, recorded rather than hidden: CAN, RAJ, AST, NZL, FRA, SAF and the Allied minors lose the Ionian wall** — judged acceptable on negligible Med fleets; if a minor is later seen loitering in the Ionian, the fix is a CONFIG archetype trigger, not a return to gating by exclusion. **Also retired here:** ENG's `naval_dominance id = 249 value = 100`, the only `naval_dominance` in either Country naval file, superseded by Fix 60's `gibraltar_approaches` at 70 for all corridor users. **ENG's Alboran weight moves 170 → 70** — the one number in this fix that changes for a country already in the plan, so if ENG's Gibraltar posture shifts on this build, that is the line. **Net result across the three files: FACTION 15 → 20 blocks, ENG 19 → 12, USA 30 → 27, with zero regions now written by both Country files in the Atlantic.** (41 regions are still written by both, all Pacific/Indian Ocean — ENG's 68-region `ignore_japan…` blanket against the USA's Pacific blocks. Different countries, different enables, genuinely different intent; a future Allied-Pacific promotion, not part of this one.) **Build fingerprint: none in telemetry** — confirm by git ancestry.
  - **Fix 63 — hygiene, and one audit that overturned its own planned repair (2026-08-14).** **(a) Duplicated `ai_strategy` entries removed.** `naval_avoid_region` is Additive per region, so an id listed twice inside one block doubles: `USA_fight_the_japanese` listed 72 Straits of Malacca, 73 Gulf of Thailand and 93 Banda Sea twice; `USA_bad_torpedos_suck` listed 84 Bismarck Sea and 88 Bering Sea twice; `GER_legacy_GER_dont_have_enough_subs` listed 62 Gulf of Guinea twice — all running at 4000 against 2000 everywhere else in the same block. Same class of defect as the doubled quoted/bare mission-threshold writes that Fix 53a found in `WA_AI_NAVAL_DEFAULT`. A scan of all `WA_AI_NAVAL_*` files now returns zero in-block duplicates. **(b) `WA_AI_CONFIG_is_in_allies` listed `is_in_faction_with = USA` twice** — harmless as an OR, but this trigger gates a large share of the Allied AI and a reader should not have to wonder whether the third line was meant to be a third country. **(c) The Bay of Biscay repair was DROPPED after computing it, and that is the finding.** The plan was to make `torch_disable` and `torch_landing` mutually exclusive. Enumerating all six writers of region 42 per campaign window first showed why not: the USA's net is **+6000** (Dec 1941–Aug 1942), **+4000** (Torch prep), **+2000** (both Torch blocks live), **+4000** (transient mixed North African control), **+8000** (after Torch succeeds) and **0** once France is liberated and every writer aborts. The two Torch `-2000` "open the Bay" terms are outvoted in **every** window they can fire in, so the intent has never once taken effect; the planned change would have moved the transient window from "avoid" to "avoid" while leaving a comment claiming a contradiction had been resolved. The two blocks are also near-exclusive already by their own state tests (disable wants North African states friendly, landing wants them hostile). Deleting the two negatives would likewise be behaviour-neutral and was **not** done: it settles whether Torch should open the Bay, which is a design decision for the user, not hygiene. The arithmetic table and the geographic argument (the Bay lies off the route from Britain or the US to Morocco, and was German U-boat transit water under Luftwaffe cover) are recorded in the block's header so the decision can be taken later on evidence. **(d) `WA_AI_MILITARY_SYSTEM.md` corrected.** The `naval_avoid_region` range read "0 to +500", which no file in the mod has ever respected; measured over the 402 entries in `common/ai_strategy/` the real distribution is 2000 ×326, 1000 ×29, 100 ×23, **-1000 ×18**, 200 ×3, -2000 ×2, -10000 ×1. The doc now states the working convention (nudge / soft wall / hard wall / pull / force-open), that **a negative value is an attraction and not a weaker avoidance**, and the rule that follows from Additive: sum every writer for a region before concluding what the AI will do. `naval_convoy_raid_region` corrected the same way (-1000..+1000). **No probe of its own** — (a) and (b) are visible only as a behaviour change in already-covered regions, (c) changed nothing, (d) is documentation. Score it through R36's existing legs.
  - **Fix 86 — defines layer, global, escort-only (2026-08-15, campaign `af003548`). Two engine terms, both pre-registered.** The `af003548` naval extraction closed the "why does the USN park" question: every naval `ai_strategy` that could move escort already reaches the USA (the −100 escort bar, the corridor `naval_dominance` 80 + −1000 avoid, no USA `naval_avoid_region` on any Atlantic corridor id), and the two ENG-only script differences (`WA_AI_ENG_convioy_escort_test` +1000 dominance on 8 regions; ENG's Country `strike_force_home_base` set) are shown non-causal — ENG's escort set is **exactly its `per_region_danger` set** (7 of 8 danger regions escorted, none without), not the probe's region list. The USA at 1944.6: 63 convoy-demand regions, 22 pure-screen 20-hull TFs parked at Norfolk with `mission=0`, 14 idle admirals, fuel 100%, **0 escort TFs** — and its `convoy_escort_presence_history` shows it *did* escort 48/69/243 for hundreds of ticks and dropped each as danger decayed to 0. Vanilla's own define comment says protection importance "will scale with convoy danger up to" `CONVOY_DANGER_FOR_MAX_IMPORTANCE`: **escort objectives are created where a country's OWN convoys were recently sunk, and only there** — the Fix 53b score terms rank objectives that exist, they do not create them. GER sinks British convoys (ENG danger 22 825 on 43) and almost no American ones (USA 385 on 43), so the USN has nothing to react to. Fix 86 (`common/defines/05_defines.lua`, values as committed 2026-08-16 after the local probe): `REGION_CONVOY_DANGER_DAILY_DECAY` 5 → **4** (the local probe ran at 2; 4 keeps most of the memory gain without hoarding), `REGION_THREAT_PER_SUNK_CONVOY` 50 → **100** (playtest note: the mission assigner behaves inconsistently while a region's threat sits around 50 — one sinking now lands clear of that band) and `CONVOY_DANGER_FOR_MAX_IMPORTANCE` 400 → **50** (vanilla 400, never overridden) — importance saturates outright at ONE sunk convoy. Blast radius: escort objective generation only — region avoidance reads the 25 000 `REGION_THREAT_LEVEL_TO_AVOID/BLOCK` bars, the escort share stays capped 0.3–0.7 of screens, raiding/patrol/strike scoring do not read either term; symmetric on GER/JAP. **Pre-registered Fix 86 leg (score with the four legs above):** (i) USA `per_region_danger` non-zero on ≥ 4 Atlantic corridor regions (43/44/55/243/247/54/57) at the first wartime summer save after US entry; (ii) ≥ 1 USA escort task force holding a corridor region at 3 consecutive 6-month anchors (`navy USA --fleets`); (iii) control: GER/JAP escort share does not exceed 0.7 of screens (the cap holds). If (i) holds and (ii) does not, the objective generator is not danger-driven and the next probe is the engine path invisible to saves — not another define. Probe additions: `section <save> USA strategic_navy --grep "per_region_danger|convoy_escort_presence"` distilled per region. Also recorded, not done: `WA_AI_ENG_convioy_escort_test` (`WA_AI_NAVAL_COUNTRY_ENG.txt`, `always = yes`, +1000, self-labelled probe from `289f1318f`) is shown non-causal by this reading and is the only ENG naval Country block the USA cannot get — retire it in a hygiene pass once Fix 86 has one campaign of readings, not in the same commit.
- **Fix previously under test:** Fix 71 — `common/ai_strategy/WA_AI_NAVAL_FACTION_ALLIES.txt` (three blocks) and `common/ai_strategy/WA_AI_NAVAL_FACTION_COMINTERN.txt` (one), authored 2026-08-13. **Verdict: mechanism CONFIRMED on `02bd4445`, outcome not delivered — do not revert it.** Root cause: WA's Atlantic escort plan is a coherent three-part set — `atlantic_north_corridor` (`naval_dominance` +80 on 43 Western Approaches, 55 Newfoundland Sea, 50 Labrador Sea, 247 Labrador Basin, 36 Greenland, 243 Icelandic Basin, 244 Greenland-Iceland Rise, 44 Denmark Strait), `atlantic_south_corridor` (+70 on 54, 57, 237, 47, 48, 61, 371, 370, 53) and `atlantic_off_corridor_avoidance` (`naval_avoid_region` +1000 on the nine mid-ocean gaps) — and **all three carried `NOT = { WA_AI_MILITARY_COUNTRY_owns_naval_atlantic_corridor = yes }` in their enable**, a trigger that resolves to the tag list ENG/USA/GER. The entire plan was therefore disabled for the only two navies that could execute it; its actual recipients were CAN/RAJ/AST/NZL, **27 hulls at 1942.6, mostly at anchor**. Not a de-duplication of Country-layer work: `naval_dominance` is documented **Additive per region** (`WA_AI_MILITARY_SYSTEM.md:105`, and each block's own header says so), the USA naval file contains **no** `naval_dominance` at all, ENG contains exactly one (region 249 Alboran, Gibraltar-gated), and mod-wide the only strategy touching a north-corridor region for the USA is an `avoid = 2000` on 244. GER in the excluded list is dead weight — it can never pass the blocks' `allowed` gate (`WA_AI_CONFIG_MILITARY_is_allied_naval_corridor_user` = ENG/CAN/RAJ/AST/NZL/USA) — which is what identifies the trigger as a geopolitical classification misused as an exclusion list. The fix **deletes the exclusion line only**; the situational trigger the enable was reaching for (`WA_AI_MILITARY_FACTION_owns_naval_atlantic_corridor`, defined at `WA_AI_MILITARY_triggers.txt:65` and referenced nowhere) was deliberately *not* swapped in, so the change alters who is eligible and nothing else. The blocks are migration-era content (`89bb5f79f` "Migrate naval AI strategies"), never `legacy_`-prefixed, i.e. never validated in a campaign.
- **Known residual, NOT fixed by Fix 71 — score it before blaming the fix.** `WA_AI_NAVAL_COUNTRY_USA_legacy_USA_smart_pacific_fleet` still sets `naval_avoid_region id = 244 value = 2000` (Greenland-Iceland Rise) while at war with Japan and `date < 1945.1.1`, i.e. across almost the whole Atlantic campaign. Both entries are Additive and of different types, so nothing cancels at parse time, but the two layers pull opposite ways on that one region and the USA leg of the north corridor is effectively **7 regions of 8** until 1945. 244 looks like a stray id in a block otherwise made of Pacific regions (113, 111, 306). Left alone deliberately — it is a legacy Country-layer block, and widening the fix would have made the experiment unreadable.
- **Established mechanism (measured, not inferred).** Missions are a **fleet-level** property in this engine: a task force gets a mission if and only if its fleet has at least one `strategic_region={}` assigned. Across every sample of `bec4d829`, ships in region-less fleets are **100% idle** and ships in fleets holding regions are ~0% idle — the correlation has no exceptions in 10 game-years. Everything the AI does not deploy piles into one or two giant region-less holding fleets (ENG "Fleet 7" = 18 task forces / 362 ships at a single province; USA "Navy Group 1" = 17 TF / 420 ships). The throttle is therefore **how many region-holding fleets the AI maintains**, and that number is small, volatile month-to-month, and drifts down late war while the navy and the convoy demand both grow.
- **Pass, four legs (all must hold), scored on ENG and USA:**
  0. **Corridor leg (Fix 71's own leg, and the one that reads the mechanism).** From the first wartime save in which the country is at war with the Axis, ENG and USA fleets hold at least **half** of the north-corridor regions (43, 55, 50, 247, 243, 244, 44) between them, and the USA holds at least one of them itself. **Region list corrected 2026-08-14 by Fix 59: it was eight ids, including 36 Greenland — a region whose eight provinces are all type `land` in `map/definition.csv`, so no fleet can ever hold it and no `naval_dominance` can apply to it. Scoring "half of 8" against a pool where one is unattainable understated the leg. The pool is 7; the corresponding `ai_strategy` line has been removed from `atlantic_north_corridor`. Any pre-2026-08-14 scoring of this leg used the 8-region list — re-read it as 7 before comparing.** `bec4d829` pre-fix: **zero** for the USA at every sample; ENG held only 243 (and 48 from the south set). If this leg moves but legs 1-3 do not, `naval_dominance` steers region assignment but does not by itself produce escort missions — say that explicitly, because it redirects the whole investigation to what converts an assigned region into an escort task force.
  1. **Escort-manned leg.** In every 6-month sample from the first save where the country is at war at sea until campaign end, ships on `escort` are at least 25% of the country's screen hulls (destroyer + frigate + light_cruiser). `bec4d829` peaks at 302/573 (53%, ENG 1942.6) and ends at **40/738 (5%) for ENG and 0/595 (0%) for USA**.
  2. **Not-parked leg.** Idle share (`none` + `reserve` missions) stays below 50% of the navy in every wartime sample. `bec4d829` runs 22%-85% with a late-war floor around 65% and monthly swings of 30 points.
  3. **Coverage leg.** The country's fleets cover at least a third of the strategic regions in which it has `required_convoys` demand. `bec4d829`: ENG 15 of 42, **USA 5 of 63** at 1946.4.
- **Probe:**
  - `savegame.py navy ENG <saves…>` and `savegame.py navy USA <saves…>` — one line per save with the mission split, screen counts, idle share, and how much of the idle sits in leaderless fleets. Add `--fleets` on the last save for the per-fleet picture (this is where the region-less holding fleet becomes obvious). **The mission ids are an empirical map, documented in `_NAVAL_MISSIONS` in that script** — no game file names them; re-read that comment before trusting a mission label.
  - `savegame.py tlm ENG <last-save> --match "^wa_tlm_nav_"` — the WA_TLM naval family: `nav_port_pct` (share of screens sitting in home ports, the script-side cross-check of leg 2), `nav_conv_threat`, `nav_conv_ws`, `nav_conv_killed`, and the quarterly `nav_port_pct_hist` on its own `nav_hist_t` axis. **Absence contract, now version-gated at 5:** no `wa_tlm_*` = probe VOID; `wa_tlm_version < 4` = the naval family predates the build; **`wa_tlm_version = 4` = `nav_screens`, `nav_port_pct` and all 40 `nav_port_pct_hist` samples are ARTEFACTS, not measurements** (see the v4 note below) — in both cases score legs 1-3 from the `navy` command alone and mark the telemetry leg NOT CHECKED, never FAILED. From `wa_tlm_version = 5` they are real.
  - **The v4 telemetry defect, found 2026-08-13 on `02bd4445` and fixed in the same session.** `WA_TLM_nav_screens` and `WA_TLM_nav_convoys` were written from `num_ships_with_type@screen_ship` and `…@convoy`. **Neither is a legal target.** The engine accepts a *sub-unit def type* (`destroyer`, `frigate`, `light_cruiser`, `heavy_cruiser`, `battleship`, `carrier`, `light_carrier`, `submarine`, `cruiser_submarine`) or one of four aggregates `carrier`/`capital`/`screen`/`submarine`; `screen_ship` is the sub-unit **category** name from `common/units/ship_*.txt`, and `convoy` is not a ship at all (convoys are equipment — there is no `ship_convoy.txt`). Both returned a silent 0 on every country, which forced `nav_port_pct` down its `else = 0` branch, so **R36's designated independent cross-check did not exist on any v4 build**. Verified in-save on `02bd4445`: `@submarine` and `num_ships` are exact to the hull while `@screen_ship` and `@convoy` read 0 on ENG/USA/GER/JAP. v5 sums `destroyer + frigate + light_cruiser` (the exact `screen_ship` membership in this mod, and exactly what `savegame.py navy` counts) and reads convoys with `num_equipment@convoy`.
  - **`has_convoys_war_support` reading 0 is a REAL zero, not a second bad token.** It is a legal variable read (listed in the engine's `dynamic_variables_documentation.md`, auto-generated from the same-named trigger). It measures a **rate, not a lifetime tally**: at most −0.01 war support per week accrues from convoys being raided *right now*, decaying 0.025/week, capped at −0.3 (`05_defines.lua:134-137`). Observable range in `nav_conv_ws` units is 0-30 and it returns to ~0 within a couple of months of the raiding stopping — so 0 across all four majors after 11 500 lifetime sinkings is expected. Use `nav_conv_killed` (raider side) plus convoy efficiency for cumulative loss.
  - **Cross-check the two against each other before scoring.** They are independent readings: the `navy` command depends on the empirical mission map, `nav_port_pct` does not (it counts hulls in our own naval-base states via `ships_in_state_ports`). If the save says 69% idle and `nav_port_pct` reads ~10%, **the mission map is what is wrong** — fix the map, do not score the item. `nav_screens` must equal the command's `screens=` exactly; v5 makes that an identity by construction (both are `destroyer + frigate + light_cruiser`), so a mismatch on a v5 save means the parse is wrong, not the metric.
  - **F9 boot check owed before the first v5 scoring, now narrowed to ONE token:** `num_equipment@convoy`. `convoy_threat` and `has_convoys_war_support` are confirmed legal variable reads, and the v5 `nav_screens` sum uses only sub-unit def types already proven in this repo (`WA_AI_misc_effects.txt:770-778`, `usa.txt:12486-12593`). Boot to 1936 and confirm `wa_tlm_nav_convoys > 0` wherever `wa_tlm_nav_ships > 0`; if it reads 0, drop the metric and its registry row rather than shipping a second silent zero.
- **Threshold:** 3 — the legs are counting facts off a mechanical reading, not an emergent front outcome. But see the KNOWN UNKNOWN: the *cause* is not yet localised, so a PASS here would be luck rather than a fix taking.
- **KNOWN UNKNOWN — RESOLVED FAVOURABLY 2026-08-13, first by the 10-day local probe and then at full campaign length by `02bd4445`: `naval_dominance` does steer fleet-to-region assignment, and on `02bd4445` every corridor region held was held by an escort-mission task force.** The open question is no longer *whether* WA can put fleets where it wants them; it is how many region-holding fleets exist and why escort assignment decays — see the Fix 53 diagnosis in the history below. The original wording is kept for the record: Fix 71 is a bet on a mechanism that is not proven. Fleet-to-region assignment is an engine AI decision, and **nobody has demonstrated that `naval_dominance` drives it.** The case for it is circumstantial but tight: it is the only WA lever that expresses "we want presence here", the block's own comment states exactly the missing intent, and its region list coincides precisely with the regions the USA never covers while the USA does cover — unprompted — its own coasts. If the corridor leg above stays flat after the fix, `naval_dominance` is not the steering wheel and this item's diagnosis needs a different lever, not a retune. **The experiment has a free control group:** CAN/AST/NZL received the plan and have no fleet; ENG/USA have the fleet and, pre-fix, no plan.

Static analysis, not yet validated in game — record the FAILED above as standing until a v5 run scores it.** The residual is not one lever, it is a chain in which every stage is set against convoy escort: (1) **escort has no score.** All four escort score terms are 0 in `05_defines.lua` (`NAVAL_MISSION_ESCORT_NEAR_OWNED`, `_NEAR_CONTROLLED`, `CONVOY_ESCORT_SCORE_FROM_CONVOYS`, `CONVOY_ESCORT_MUL_FROM_NO_CONVOYS`) against vanilla 300 / 200 / 15 / 0.02, while **patrol keeps its full vanilla score** because WA's patrol overrides are commented out. (2) **escort's bar is the highest in the mod.** Base 100, plus **+5000** from the north-corridor block that Fix 71 had just un-gated for ENG/USA, while patrol's bar sits at −1900 (−1000 intended, doubled by a quoted/bare copy-paste on an Additive type). That is the shape behind "88 USA ships on patrol across its own convoy coasts against 51 on escort". (3) **coverage is capped by construction.** `MAX_MISSION_PER_TASKFORCE[CONVOY ESCORT] = 1` vs vanilla 4 — one region per escort task force, against a hard ceiling of 0.7 of all screen task forces. (4) **the region-holder count itself**, which is what the item asked for: missions and strategic regions are *fleet*-level, so the number of fleets is the number of region-holding slots, and `NAVY_PREFERED_MAX_SIZE = 80` vs vanilla 25 thirds it — the direct structural explanation for one 302-ship holding fleet instead of a dozen deployable ones. Fix 53 changes (1), (2) and (3) and leaves (4) plus `MIN_SUPPORT_SHIP_TO_SPARE` = 3.0 as documented candidates, because restoring escort *scoring* is what makes the allocator ask for screens in the first place, and changing four levers at once makes the next reading unscoreable.

Leg 2: ENG idle 63/56/51/…/**17 (1942.6)**/…/59% (1946.5); USA idle 48–81%, **80% at 1944.6** (492 of 713 ships in one region-less "Navy Group 4", zero escort TFs). Telemetry leg NOT CHECKED (`nav_port_pct` 15.6% vs 47% idle; `nav_screens` matches the parse exactly). 100% of idle in admiral-less region-less fleets at every sample. **Convoy war: GER lifetime kills 561 / 1 477 / 3 783 / 6 871 / 8 934 / 10 163 / 11 413 (1940.6 → 1946.3) — worse than `f9321934`'s 10 802 and ~3× `be18f9c7`'s 3 960**; ENG `nav_conv_threat` pinned 100 for all 72 war months; ENG free-convoy pool 0 at 1943.4/1943.9/1943.10 and 40 at 1942.9, recovering to 3 186; Allied U-boat kills ≈2 801 (ENG 1 997, USA 804, **CAN 0**) ≈ 4.1 convoys per boat; GER subs 102 → 148 (1943.6) → 110, never suppressed. **Verdict: lost 1942–43, contained by shipbuilding 1944–46, never won.** The `be18f9c7` "won anyway" reading was not the fix taking — it did not reproduce.

  - 2026-08-16 · `af003548` **local probe of Fix 86** (`USA_1944_10_01_11.hoi4`, resumed from cloud `1944.7_Jul` on v16, single save 1944.10.1) · **MOVED — the largest single delta of the probe; leg (ii) met at one anchor, leg (i) NOT met as written, cap control holds.** USA escort hulls **29 → 120** (4.6 % → 19 % of screens), idle 79 % → 61 %, escort TFs 2 → 6, **USN holds 43 Western Approaches with two escort TFs** (leg ii, 1 of 3 anchors), `nav_conv_threat` 23 → 100. ENG escort **160 → 441 (65 % of screens, at the 0.7 cap)**, idle **58 % → 15 %**, `nav_port_pct` 15.05 → 6.10, escort TFs 11 → 17 over 12 regions, and the 361-ship admiral-less "Fleet 14" collapsed to **20 ships**. USA `per_region_danger` 2 → 7 non-zero regions with 43 at 15 → **458** — but six of the seven are Med/Indian/Pacific (327, 349, 69, 308, 104, 233), so leg (i) reads **1 of 7** north-corridor ids: recut leg (i) to "≥ 4 regions anywhere USA convoys run" or drop it — the geography of danger follows where the USA is actually raided, which is not the ENG corridor. Control (iii): GER 0/27, JAP 44 % → 29 %; USA `Navy Group 4` still 338 ships/18 TF region-less (from 480/20). Not scored — cloud campaign next.
### R41. SOV's submarine output leaves the coastal boat entirely — via the role, not the design (Fix 64)

- **Fix under test:** Fix 64 — `common/scripted_triggers/WA_AI_CONFIG.txt` (`WA_AI_CONFIG_focus_on_cruiser_submarines` gains `original_tag = SOV`) plus `common/ai_equipment/SOV_naval.txt` (group `SOV_naval_cruiser_submarine`, priorities of `cruiser_submarine_3` and `_4` swapped, 90 ↔ 120). Authored 2026-08-14; root cause and the two failed attempts before it are R30's. Both levers already tried on this exact case moved **zero factories** — the design layer (Fix 46) and `production_upgrade_desire_offset` (Fix 51) — and research suppression is impossible because `sov_submarine_3` is a 1936 START tech. Fix 64 therefore stops trying to make the `naval_submarine` role pick a better hull and **empties the role**: the CONFIG entry excludes SOV from `WA_AI_PRODUCTION_focus_on_submarines` (`WA_AI_PRODUCTION_navy.txt:22`) and hands it `WA_DEFAULT_production_navy_main_focus_on_cruiser_submarines`, which sets `naval_submarine` to **0** (base 25 − 25) and `cruiser_submarine` to 100. GER has run that path since long before this fix, so the routing itself is proven; only its application to SOV is new. The second half of the fix keeps the newly-live cruiser ladder off the K (`sov_cruiser_submarine_hull_4`, 3080 IC, 59 IC per strength point): the S / Srednyaya (`hull_3`, 1000 IC, 5620 km, str 22, vis 14) becomes the mainstay at 120 and the K drops to 90.
- **Pass, three legs:**
  1. **Role leg (the fix itself).** SOV's naval production sits on the `cruiser_submarine` role: at least one line on a variant whose hull is `sov_cruiser_submarine_hull_*`, and **zero factories on any `sov_submarine_hull_*` variant**, at every save from the first one with SOV naval lines. A Malyutka line anywhere is an outright FAIL and means the role ratio never reached production — which would retire the whole "route the role" approach, not just this fix.
  2. **Design leg.** That cruiser line is on `sov_cruiser_submarine_hull_3` (S), not `hull_4` (K). A K line is a **partial**, not a total, failure — record it as "role leg PASS / design leg FAIL", because the two halves live in different layers and the routing can take while the ordering does not. This is also the first clean test of whether `ai_equipment` priority governs **first design creation on a role that has no existing line**, the one case R30/R31/R35 left open: every previous reading was about *converting* a running line.
  3. **Fleet leg.** SOV's `wa_tlm_r64_csubs` grows well past its **19**-boat 1936 OOB baseline (`history/units/SOV_1936_naval_mtg.txt`), while the plain-submarine count stays at or below its 1936 baseline of **91** (39 Shchuka + 3 Pravda + 49 Malyutka), attrition only. Rising csubs with a flat plain-sub count is the fix working; both flat means nothing is being built at all and legs 1-2 are the reading that matters. **SETTLED 2026-08-14 on campaign `be18f9c7` — `wa_tlm_nav_subs` IS inclusive of cruiser submarines, so `r64_csubs` is a SHARE of it, never an independent population.** Both sub-unit defs carry `type = { submarine }` and `submarine` is simultaneously a def name and one of the four engine aggregates; the aggregate wins. Measured against hull-resolved fleets: **SOV `nav_subs` 101 = 93 cruiser + 8 plain** (`r64_csubs` = 93 exactly, `1946.7_Jul.hoi4`), **GER `nav_subs` 18 = 14 cruiser + 4 plain** (`r64_csubs` = 14 exactly, read on `1945.6_Jun.hoi4` — GER's last save with a `units` section; see the annihilated-tag gotcha in `wa-savegame-analysis`). The arithmetic that follows: **plain hulls = `nav_subs` − `r64_csubs`**, and the leg's "plain-submarine count stays at or below its 1936 baseline of 91" must be scored on that difference, **not on `nav_subs` itself** — a rising `nav_subs` with a rising `r64_csubs` is the fix working, and reading `nav_subs` as the Malyutka count would score it as a failure. The question is closed; do not re-derive it per campaign.
- **Probe:** (a) and (b) are save-native, the same reading as R30: `savegame.py section SOV production <saves…>`, resolving each line's `equipment_variant_index` through the top-level `equipments={}` registry — **a name grep on `production` alone returns MIO idea names, not equipment; resolve through the registry or the answer is wrong.** Report the hull behind every SOV naval line, per save, from the first line onward. (c) `savegame.py tlm SOV <saves…>` for `wa_tlm_r64_csubs` and `wa_tlm_nav_subs`. **Absence contract:** no `wa_tlm_*` namespace = probe VOID; `wa_tlm_version` present but `< 7` = the metric does not exist in that build, mark **NOT YET TESTED**, never FAILED. **Token control, mandatory before scoring leg 3:** read `wa_tlm_r64_csubs` on **GER** in the same save. GER has been on the cruiser role since before this fix, so GER > 0 proves the engine token resolves; **GER = 0 and SOV = 0 together means the token is bad** — the v4 `nav_screens` failure mode — and leg 3 is NOT CHECKED, not FAILED. (d) Build fingerprint: `wa_tlm_version >= 7` (the metric ships in the v7 bump; later bumps are additive) plus git ancestry of the Fix 64 commit; confirm before recording any FAILED.
- **Threshold:** 3 (narrow — two discrete "which role / which hull is the line on" facts plus a counter reading; not an emergent outcome).
- **Known cost, deliberate, NOT a finding to report as a failure:** the cruiser hulls cost 4–12× the Malyutka per boat, so SOV's submarine **count** will fall sharply even where the fix works perfectly. Score hull identity and the csub trend, never absolute fleet size. If SOV instead builds almost no submarines at all (say < 30 csubs by 1943 on a healthy economy), that is a **separate** finding — the yards went somewhere else, or the cruiser hull is unaffordable at SOV's dockyard count — and it belongs in the history as its own observation, with the naval-factory count and what else those yards were building.
- **Geographic caveat carried by this item:** 5620–7500 km boats are wasted in the Baltic and the Black Sea, which is where most of the Soviet navy starts. This fix buys hull quality, not deployment — if the boats are built and then parked, that is R36's mechanism, not this one's, and should be reported there.
- **Absorbed from R30 (SOV builds ocean-going submarines, Fix 46) on 2026-08-16.** R30 was superseded by its own clause ("SUPERSEDED FOR SOV BY FIX 64 … the question moves to R41") and had scored NOT CHECKED on both post-Fix-64 campaigns; its fleet leg is leg 3 above (plain hulls = `nav_subs` − `r64_csubs` ≤ the 91 baseline). Record it carried: **Fix 46** (design-priority ladder 120 Shchuka / 5 Malyutka + `ship_radar` on the Shchuka, `SOV_naval.txt`) changed nothing on `bec4d829`, `02bd4445`, `f9321934` — the `naval_submarine` line sat on `sov_submarine_hull_3` (Malyutka) in every save, Malyutka fleet 49 → 702 / 769 / **1 056**, Shchuka pinned at 39; **Fix 51** (`production_upgrade_desire_offset` ±100 on `hull_1`/`hull_3`, `WA_AI_PRODUCTION_COUNTRY_SOV.txt`) likewise moved zero factories on `02bd4445`, and the lever is not serialized into a save (three ungated controls invisible), so "did not arm" vs "engine ignored it" is undecidable from a campaign. Two mechanism claims retired there, do not revisit: `obsolete=yes` is neither an archetype-uniqueness rule nor a production gate; and "priority is evaluated downstream of obsolescence" was wrong. Post-Fix-64 fleet leg PASSED on `be18f9c7` (Malyutka 49 → 43 → 0, never above baseline) and `af003548` (plain subs 91 → 81). Durable rule for `wa-lessons-learned`: *`ai_equipment` priority ladders must be capability-ordered, because `<tag>_<class>_hull_N` numbering is class-historical* — and priority does not steer a running line at all.
- **Streak:** 0
- **Status:** FAILED
- **History:**
  - 2026-08-14 · `be18f9c7` · FAILED (design leg) - role and fleet legs pass — Role leg **PASS**: **zero factories on any `sov_submarine_hull_*` in all 126 saves**, SOV naval output being entirely `sov_cruiser_submarine_hull_*` from 1936.2. Fleet leg **PASS**: cruiser subs 15 -> 24 -> 46 -> 71 -> 103 (1946.1) -> 92, matching `wa_tlm_r64_csubs = 93`; plain subs 91 -> 8, never above baseline; token control OK (GER `r64_csubs = 14`). Design leg **FAIL**: after two months on `hull_3` (S, 3 factories) the line is **`sov_cruiser_submarine_hull_4` (K) from 1936.8 to 1946.7 - 120 months, 299 line-instances, 2 to 6 lines, up to 53 factories** - the exact hull the fix demoted to 90, while `hull_3` at 120 is never selected again. **Mechanism, and it means the design leg was never an `ai_equipment` question: `sov_cruiser_submarine_4` (K) is a 1936.1.1.12 START tech in this build, so both designs are enabled on day 1, and `common/national_focus/soviet.txt:7677` seeds two K lines via `add_equipment_production` ("K-1"/"K-2") which show up at 1936.8.** The K line predates and outlives any priority comparison - the 120-S / 90-K ordering never got a first-design-creation decision to make. **Do not re-tune the ladder before dealing with the focus-seeded lines.** **Settles this item's UNVERIFIED question: `wa_tlm_nav_subs` IS inclusive** - SOV 101 = 93 csubs + 8 plain, GER 18 = 14 + 4; read `r64_csubs` as a share.
  - 2026-08-15 · `af003548` · FAILED (design leg; role + fleet pass — 2nd consecutive, identical) — Role leg PASS: **zero `sov_submarine_hull_*` factories anywhere**. Design leg FAIL: the line is `sov_cruiser_submarine_hull_4` (K) at every date, 2–5 lines up to 42 f, `hull_3` (S) never — the focus-seeded K lines again, as diagnosed on `be18f9c7`. Fleet leg PASS: csubs 74 → 105 → 138 vs the 19 baseline; GER token control 106/110. Do not re-tune the ladder before dealing with `soviet.txt:7677`'s seeded lines.

### R42. AIFC sees the ally soil an expeditionary army stands on, and a capitulated tag's sector does not freeze (Fixes 65 + 68)

- **Opened 2026-08-14** out of R39's leg-2 and leg-3 diagnosis on campaign `f9321934`. Read that diagnosis first — this item verifies the fixes, R39 owns the evidence. **Both fixes are the same word (`has_capitulated`) misused as a capability test, at two sites with different scopes**, which is why they are scored together.
- **Fix under test (a):** **Fix 65** — `common/scripted_effects/WA_AI_AIFC_helpers.txt` (section 1b, the Fix 28 expeditionary fallback) and its lockstep twin in `common/scripted_effects/WA_AI_AIFC_core.txt` (the Fix 28 validity mirror). The ally filter's `has_capitulated = no` is removed; `exists = yes` replaces it in the `every_country` walk. Two removed lines, one added; everything else in the diff is comment. **Root cause:** `has_capitulated` answers "did this government fall", not "does this country exist" and not "is this territory usable". A capitulated tag keeps faction membership, keeps owning and controlling territory, and regains control of liberated home soil — so it is normally the controller of the exact ground a liberating expeditionary army stands on. On `f9321934` that cost ENG its sector for **~118 consecutive weeks** (1944.5 → 1946.7) while every division it had ashore stood on FRA-controlled France and FRA read `capitulated=yes` in every save. The real guard was always the sibling `divisions_in_state = { size > 2 }` term, which still excludes allied ground ROOT is not on.
- **This is NOT a front fix and must not be scored as one.** AIFC issues no attack orders, and Layers 1–2 (`force_concentration_factor`) are not sector-gated, so ENG kept concentrating throughout the outage — it lost a scripted *axis*, not concentration. A Normandy-anchored sector would have pushed **more** divisions into a state already holding 42→79 against ~11 defenders. Expect sector bookkeeping to change and the front not to. **If a campaign shows the Normandy/Western stall persisting with the sector restored, that is this item PASSING, not failing.**
- **Pass, three legs:**
  1. **Coverage leg (the fix itself).** In any campaign where an Allied expeditionary power (ENG or USA) has ≥3 divisions ashore on a **capitulated** ally's controlled soil, that power holds `wa_ai_aifc_sector_anchor` in the same save. Concretely for a `f9321934`-shaped run: ENG holds a sector at every sampled save from the first one with ENG divisions in France onward. Absent while the ashore-condition holds = FAIL.
  2. **No-churn leg.** The restored sector **ages**: `wa_ai_aifc_sector_age` on ENG cycles 1–5 across consecutive monthly samples rather than pinning at 1. Pinning at 1 means the validity mirror in `_core.txt` is rejecting what selection accepted — i.e. the two sites fell out of lockstep, which is the specific way this fix can be half-applied.
  3. **No-regression leg.** Countries that already held sectors keep them and keep age-cycling (GER, SOV, USA, JAP, CHI), and no country gains a sector anchored on a front it has no troops near — spot-check that each new holder's anchor is adjacent to territory where it has ≥3 divisions.
  4. **Thaw leg (Fix 68).** No **live** capitulated tag holds a frozen sector: for any tag with `capitulated=yes` and `num_of_controlled_states > 0`, `wa_ai_aifc_sector_anchor` is **absent** in every sampled save. On a `f9321934`-shaped run that is specifically FRA, which held `anchor=34, age=4` byte-identical across all seven samples. A capitulated tag that still shows an anchor, or shows the *same* anchor+age in two consecutive samples, means the call never moved out of the gated block. **Annexed corpses (DEN, SHA, SIC, PER, BRM) are NOT in scope** — whether `on_weekly` iterates non-existent countries is unverified, so score them neither way; if you happen to establish it, record the answer on this item.
- **Probe:** `savegame.py var ENG "^wa_ai_aifc_"` and the same for USA across ≥6 monthly saves spanning the landing and after (the regex is matched against the whole `name=value` string — anchor with a trailing `=` or `\^`, never `$`). Pair with `savegame.py army ENG <saves>`. For leg 1's precondition you need division locations vs state controllers, which `savegame.py` has no command for: parse `location=` on `division={}` blocks **inside `units` only**, map province→state via `history/states/<ID>-<Name>.txt` `provinces = {}`, and read each state's controller remembering that **a state omits `controller=` when the controller equals the owner**. Capitulation is readable: `capitulated=yes` sits in the country block's `diplomacy={}` sub-block and is **written only when true**. **`surrender_progress` is NOT serialized anywhere in a save** — do not plan a probe around it.
- **Threshold:** 3 (narrow — every leg is a discrete "does the variable exist / does the counter move" fact off one sweep).
- **Fix under test (b):** **Fix 68** — `common/on_actions/WA_AI_misc_on_actions.txt`. `WA_AI_AIFC_update_sector` is lifted out of the Priority-Construction block (gated `is_ai = yes` + `has_capitulated = no`) into its own `is_ai = yes` block. **Root cause:** that gate made `WA_AI_AIFC_core.txt`'s "not eligible → clear sector" branch **unreachable for exactly the population it was written for** — a capitulated country was never iterated, so its sector could not age, revalidate, or clear, and simply froze at whatever was selected the week before the capital fell. **The `has_capitulated = no` in the PC block is deliberately NOT removed**: it is load-bearing for the six other calls in there (training modifier, PC factory assignment, PC project progress, railway queue, posture, lend-lease), all of which would newly run for every capitulated tag. AIFC is the only one of the seven whose effect is designed to be entered while ineligible, so it is the only one that can safely move out. **This clears a capitulated tag's sector; it does not grant it one** — `has_capitulated = no` still stands in `WA_AI_AIFC_should_select_sector`, which is where that judgement belongs.
- **Cheap regression to watch for Fix 68:** the newly-iterated population is every capitulated AI tag (13–19 per save on `f9321934`). Each pays a failed cheap trigger plus an idempotent clear and an armour reconcile over empty books — the expensive selection body sits behind the else branch and is not reached. If a campaign shows a **capitulated tag acquiring** a sector, the trigger-file term was lost and that is an outright FAIL of Fix 68, not a widening.
- **Streak:** 2
- **Status:** PASSED
- **History:**
  - 2026-08-14 · `be18f9c7` · PASSED — **All four legs.** Leg 1 PASS - ENG has >= 3 divisions ashore on capitulated-FRA-controlled soil continuously from 1944.5 and holds `aifc_sector_anchor` in **every one of those saves** (785 Picardy 1944.7, 22 at 1944.8, 23 Champagne-Ardenne 1944.9 with corridor 23/19/25/30/33, 8 Luxembourg 1945.3, 28 Alsace 1945.4/5). **The ~118-week outage of `f9321934` does not recur.** Leg 2 PASS - ENG age cycles 2/3/3/4/4/4/2/2/4/1/4/4/1/1/4, never pinned at 1. Leg 3 PASS - GER/SOV/USA/JAP/CHI/PRC all keep sectors and age-cycle, every new holder's anchor sitting on ground where it has large stacks (USA anchors 20/23/22/850/17/28/42 with 150-200 divisions in France). Leg 4 (thaw) PASS - **every live capitulated tag holds no anchor at all** (FRA, YUG, POL, BEL, CRO, BUL, HUN) and no capitulated tag ever *acquires* a sector, so the regression watch is clean too.
  - 2026-08-15 · `af003548` · PASSED (all four legs) — FRA `capitulated=yes` controlling 2/3/9 French states at 1944.9/1945.6/1946.3 with ENG 27/21/16 divisions on it; ENG holds `aifc_sector_anchor` in **EVERY** monthly save 1944.1 → 1946.4; ENG age 1/1/1/3/4/2 (cycles); no capitulated tag holds an anchor. Streak 2 of 3.

### R43. Carrier aircraft are built for the decks that exist, not for land airfields (Fix 66)

- **Opened 2026-08-14** after campaign `f9321934`. **R31/R32 are the `ai_equipment` `priority` items and R36 the naval one; none of them owns this.** This item is deliberately NOT an `ai_equipment` item — see the layer note below.
- **The defect, measured.** Walking the top-level `strategic_air` block (wings whose `air_base` id has no `state=` in the depth-1 `air_base={}` map are on carrier decks, all others land-based), every carrier navy over-builds carrier aircraft and parks the surplus on land, where a carrier plane is a poor substitute for a land one:

  | date | JAP land:deck | ENG land:deck | USA land:deck |
  | --- | --- | --- | --- |
  | 1938.6 | 0.66 | 0.00 | 0.53 |
  | 1940.6 | 1.47 | 1.25 | 0.65 |
  | 1942.6 | 2.97 | 2.28 | 1.46 |
  | 1944.6 | 3.84 | 3.83 | 0.84 |
  | 1946.8 | **6.25** | **2.09** | **0.45** |

  At 1946.8 JAP holds 1 380 carrier planes on deck and **8 619 on land**, 75% of its entire air force; ENG 969 / 2 026, **52% of the RAF is naval aviation** in a campaign where it flew 379 planes over the D-Day beaches against 6 483 German. **92% of JAP's and 94% of ENG's land-parked carrier planes carry NO MISSION** — measured for `cv_*` specifically, not inherited from the campaign-wide 87%/66% figures — so the surplus is *not* doing useful work from shore. Deckless minors (SOV 99, ITA ~90, NZL 83, SWE 42, SPR 20) are flat starting stock for ten years and are **not** part of this defect; the `has_navy_size` gate already excludes them correctly.
- **Root cause, and it is not what the symptom suggests.** Carrier-plane output is set **entirely** by the deck-blind `equipment_production_min_factories` floors in `WA_AI_PRODUCTION_DEFAULT_cv_plane.txt`. At **both** 1944.6 and 1946.8, every carrier-fighter and carrier-naval-bomber line of ENG, JAP and USA ran at `requested_factories = active_factories =` **exactly 8** — six independent cells, one number, which is the additive sum of the base floor (2) and the carrier-major floor (6). USA's carrier-CAS line sat at exactly 1, its own single floor. Over the same span USA's fleet grew **4 → 47 hulls** while JAP's froze at 20: identical production, opposite absorption. Vanilla's own comment on the type is the confirmation — *"Forces the AI to allocate this many factories … it doesn't take into account how many factories are actually available"* (`common/ai_strategy/documentation.info:410`).
- **Two levers were ruled OUT before the fix was written, both from documentation rather than another campaign:**
  - **`ai_equipment` `priority` is the wrong layer** (as this file already established after `bec4d829`). The `cv_*` design groups declare **dedicated roles** (`air_cv_fighter`, `air_cv_naval_bomber`), so they never compete with a land role; cutting their priority changes *which airframe* the AI designs, never *how many*. **Fix 66 therefore does not depend on the R31/R32 KNOWN UNKNOWN** — it places no scripted trigger inside a `priority` modifier and does not touch `common/ai_equipment/` at all.
  - **`unit_ratio` cannot over-build.** `documentation.info` (### UNIT RATIOS, AIR) says land-based and carrier plane types are pooled **completely separately**, and the carrier pool total is computed from deck capacity × `WANTED_CARRIER_PLANES_PER_CARRIER_CAPACITY_FACTOR` (1.5, `00_defines.lua:2796`) **minus the carrier planes already in airwings** — land-parked ones included. `unit_ratio` only splits that total between the cv roles. The `a5ea1fb84` 150/100 split is therefore **not** the cause and is left untouched. It also means a saturated country's engine demand is **already zero**, which is why the floor is the only thing still building. (`equipment_production_factor` was rejected too: its documented id namespace is `script_enum_equipment_category`, which contains no `cv_*` entry — the existing `id = cv_cas` uses at `World_Ablaze_production_air_strategies.txt:110,125` are probably already no-ops.)
- **Fix under test:** Fix 66 — `common/scripted_triggers/WA_AI_PRODUCTION_carrier_planes.txt` (six new triggers) + `common/ai_strategy/WA_AI_PRODUCTION_DEFAULT_cv_plane.txt` (the two floor blocks split into four per-role blocks so each can be braked independently). A role's floor is dropped once `num_deployed_planes_with_type@cv_small_fighter_airframe` / `@cv_small_naval_bomber_airframe` exceeds a bar banded on `has_navy_size = { type = carrier }`. Bars are **90 fighter / 60 naval-bomber planes per hull**, i.e. 2× the ~45/30 deck slots per hull implied by the measured 57–86 planes-per-hull capacity and the 150:100 split — deliberately well above the engine's own 1.5× target so the brake trips only on genuine surplus. **Per-role on purpose:** ENG's surplus is naval bombers (1 799 of its 2 026 land pile) while its carrier fighters are genuinely scarce (227), so a combined test would brake the wrong half. Against the measured data the bars brake JAP on both roles, ENG on naval bombers only, and USA on neither.
- **Why a hull-count rule alone was not enough, and why this needs a plane count.** JAP (4.83× the engine target, 20 hulls) and USA (0.96×, 47 hulls) are *both* large carrier fleets. No rule keyed on hulls alone separates them — the discriminator is planes-vs-decks. That is what forced the `num_deployed_planes_with_type@` read, and it is the one unproven piece.
- **KNOWN UNKNOWN, and the probe is built to settle it.** Nobody has confirmed in-game that `num_deployed_planes_with_type@` resolves for the `cv_*` airframe archetypes. The same spelling on this very archetype is already live at `common/decisions/ITA.txt:957`, but that is precedent, not proof, and it is exactly the class of token that read a silent 0 for the whole of WA_TLM v4 (`nav_screens`). **Fix 66 fails SAFE by construction:** an unresolvable token reads 0, 0 never exceeds a bar, no clause fires, the brake never trips and behaviour is exactly what it is today — it cannot empty a deck. But "never armed" and "armed and ignored" look identical in the plane counts, which is why leg 0 exists.
- **Pass, four legs (leg 0 gates the rest):**
  0. **Arming leg.** `wa_tlm_nav_cv_f` and `wa_tlm_nav_cv_n` are **non-zero on ENG, JAP and USA** in a war year. **Both zero on a carrier major means the token is bad, the brake was never live, and NOTHING else in this item may be scored — mark it NOT CHECKED, never FAILED.**
  1. **Brake leg (the headline).** JAP's land:deck ratio **never exceeds 2.0** at any monthly sample, against 6.25 on `f9321934`; ENG's never exceeds 2.0, against 2.09. Equivalently: total carrier planes ≤ 2× (1.5 × deck capacity), with on-deck count used as the deck-capacity measure (valid whenever a land surplus exists, since decks are then full).
  2. **No-empty-decks leg (the regression this could cause, and the one that matters more than leg 1).** No carrier navy ends the campaign with **deck utilisation below 80%** — i.e. on-deck planes ≥ 0.8 × the on-deck count that country reached at its own peak hull count. USA specifically must not fall below its `f9321934` trajectory: ≥ 1 813 on deck at 1944.6 and ≥ 4 040 at 1946.8, with its carrier-fighter and carrier-naval-bomber lines still funded. **A carrier navy with empty decks is a worse outcome than the land surplus this fix removes.**
  3. **Minors-untouched leg.** SOV, ITA, NZL, SWE, SPR carrier-plane counts stay at their flat starting values (99 / ~90 / 83 / 42 / 20). Any movement means the gate leaked to deckless countries.
- **Probe** (run verbatim; an extraction agent needs nothing beyond this):
  1. `python .claude/skills/wa-savegame-analysis/scripts/savegame.py tlm <TAG> <last save> --match "^wa_tlm_nav_cv_"` for ENG, JAP, USA — leg 0. **The light-carrier question is SETTLED 2026-08-14 on campaign `be18f9c7`: `type = carrier` DOES count light carriers, so `wa_tlm_nav_cv_hulls` is fleet + light decks combined.** Measured on `1946.7_Jul.hoi4`: **JAP 21 = 16 fleet + 5 light**, **ENG 20 = 18 + 2**, **USA 52 = 52 + 0** (USA is the degenerate case that would hide the answer on its own). Consequence for the bars: a navy's hull band counts light decks at the same weight as fleet decks even though a light carrier's capacity is much smaller, so the per-hull bars are **loose** for a light-carrier-heavy fleet — the safe direction (it under-brakes), but it is why JAP bands high. Do not re-derive this per campaign; re-check only if the engine's aggregate list changes.
  2. The deck/land split, at 1940.6, 1942.6, 1944.6, 1945.6 and the last save, for **every** country with a non-zero `cv_*` count — legs 1 and 3. Script: `.claude/skills/wa-savegame-analysis/scripts/cvair.py`, shipped with this item (`python .claude/skills/wa-savegame-analysis/scripts/cvair.py --miss <saves…>`); it reproduces the `f9321934` figures exactly. If rewriting it: wings live at `strategic_air → <TAG> → air_wing_pool → air_wings`; a wing is **on a carrier deck** iff its `air_base` id has **no `state=`** in the depth-1 `air_base={}` map. **Anchor on depth** — `count=` also occurs one level deeper inside combat-history `enemy={}` records, and counting both over-reports ENG by 7×.
  3. Carrier-aircraft production lines at 1944.6 and the last save for ENG/JAP/USA — legs 1 and 2. **Aircraft lines are in `military_lines`, not `air_lines`** (that block does not exist in this save format; scoping to it returns zero for everything — this corrects the `wa-savegame-analysis` gotcha, which names three sibling blocks of which only `military_lines` and `naval_lines` are real here). Resolve every `equipment_variant_index` through the top-level `equipments={}` registry and select on **archetype** — the three carrier archetypes are `cv_small_fighter_airframe`, `cv_small_bomber_airframe`, `cv_small_naval_bomber_airframe`. **Never select on the variant `name=`:** `eng_nav_bomber_equipment_1..5` and `usa_cas_equipment_5..8` carry carrier archetypes despite non-`cv_` names. The pre-fix signature to compare against is **exactly 8 active factories on every carrier fighter and carrier naval-bomber line**; the fix has bitten when a braked role's line count or factory count falls below that.
- **Threshold:** **5**. This is an outcome measured over a whole campaign, not a discrete "did the variable get written" fact, and leg 2 is the dangerous side — one campaign where a carrier navy happens to keep full decks is not evidence the brake is safely calibrated. (Leg 0 is discrete, but it is a gate, not the item.)
- **Residual, deliberately not fixed and worth watching:** the bars are banded on hull count using each band's **upper** bound, so a fleet sitting just above a band boundary is braked against a bar sized for a fleet up to twice its size. That biases towards *not* braking, which is the safe direction, but it means the brake releases later than the engine's own 1.5× target would. Re-tune only after leg 0 comes back positive — tuning a gate that never armed is the mistake this file has already recorded twice.
- **Streak:** 0
- **Status:** FAILED
- **History:**
  - 2026-08-14 · `be18f9c7` · FAILED (leg 1, ENG half) — Leg 0 **PASS** - `wa_tlm_nav_cv_f`/`_n` non-zero on all three (ENG 1091/1200, JAP 1476/2066, USA 3050/1910), so the `num_deployed_planes_with_type@` token resolves and the brake was live. Leg 1 **split** over 21 samples 1940.6-1946.7: **JAP PASS - land:deck peaks at 1.84 (1942.6) and never exceeds 2.0**, ending 1.43 against 6.25 on `f9321934`; **ENG FAIL - 2.24 (1942.3), 2.81 (1942.6), 3.16 (1942.9), then a flat 2.59 plateau 1943.3-1944.3**, only falling under 2.0 from 1944.12 and ending at 1.31 (vs 2.09 before). ENG's land pile is pinned at exactly 2 200 for 1943.6-1944.3 then declines, so the brake does bite - roughly two years late. Leg 2 **PASS** - USA on-deck 2 820 at 1944.6 (bar 1 813) and 4 360 at 1946.7 (bar 4 040) with carrier-fighter/nav-bomber lines still funded; JAP ends 1 459 on deck against a 1 460 peak, ENG 993 against 1 000; no deck utilisation below 80%. Leg 3 **PASS with drift** - NZL 41 and SWE 33 dead flat, but CAN 71 -> 100, SOV 96 -> 100 and SPR 11 -> 19 with zero decks (small enough to be lend-leased carrier-archetype equipment with non-`cv_` names, as the probe warns, but it is movement where the minors-untouched leg expects none). **Sub-question settled: `type = carrier` DOES count light carriers** - `wa_tlm_nav_cv_hulls` JAP 21 = 16 fleet + 5 light, ENG 20 = 18 + 2, USA 52 = 52.
  - 2026-08-15 · `af003548` · FAILED (leg 1, ENG half — 2nd consecutive) — Leg 0 PASS (`nav_cv_f`/`_n` ENG 1 721/1 190, JAP 1 800/1 195, USA 1 503/653 at 1944.6). Leg 1: **JAP PASS** — land:deck peaks 1.42, ends 1.18 (best yet); **ENG FAIL** — 2.81 (1942.6), 2.57 (1944.6) > 2.0. Leg 2 PASS generically (all navies end at their on-deck peak; USA 1 727 at 1944.6 misses the 1 813 `f9321934` bar by 5% — note, not a fail of the ≥80% clause). Leg 3 PASS with drift again (CAN 42 → 60, SPR 11 → 21). ENG's naval-bomber surplus is the same half that failed on `be18f9c7`; the brake bites late on ENG specifically.

### R44. A scripted landing freezes AI invasion planning in its own theatre — and only its own theatre (Fix 67)

- **Fix under test:** Fix 67 (2026-08-14, commit `<FILL AT COMMIT>`). New generic system: `WA_AI_DIVISION_spawn_invasion` — the single effect all ~90 scripted landings in `events/WA_AI_invasions.txt` execute through — now stamps a 90-day freeze flag on the invader and every AI member of its faction, keyed to the landing state's macro-theatre (WEST = europe/africa/middle_east, EAST = asia/australia). Three Default-layer blocks in `WA_AI_MILITARY_DEFAULT_INVASION_landing_freeze.txt` consume it with `invasion_unit_request value = -200` scoped by a `state_trigger` on the invasion target's continent. Control panel: `common/scripted_triggers/WA_AI_LANDING_triggers.txt`. Spec: `documentation/WA_AI_MILITARY_SYSTEM.md` §10. Same change removes the superseded `AI_naval_invasion_fix` decision (USA/ENG-only, hard-dated 1944.2.1–1944.3.15, payload `naval_invasion_capacity = -50` — a live modifier, not the dead one its author's comment claimed).
- **Motivation, stated honestly — this fix is NOT backed by a measured failure.** It is motivated by design intent (a scripted landing should not be diluted by the AI planning a competing beachhead) and by the existence of the broken hack above. On `f9321934` the Normandy lodgement was crushed back to state 15 for 15 months, but the causes identified were the lost Battle of the Atlantic (ENG convoy stockpile at 0 for 19 months), UK air-base capacity (7 000 against 10 021 planes at 1944.6), and ENG losing its AIFC schwerpunkt for exactly the stall window. **No campaign has yet shown a competing AI invasion diverting force from a scripted landing.** If this item's leg 3 keeps reading "the freeze armed and nothing changed", that is a legitimate outcome and the honest disposition is to retire it as *inert but harmless*, not to hunt for a benefit.
- **Pass, four legs:**
  0. **Arming gate (discrete — if this fails, legs 1–3 are NOT CHECKED, never FAILED).** On any campaign reaching a scripted landing, `wa_tlm_r44_freeze_n > 0` on the invader **and** on at least one other faction member, with `wa_tlm_r44_freeze_last_t > 0`. A `_freeze_n` of 0 on GER at 1940.6+ (Weserübung) means the marker never fired — most likely the `var:_invasion_state` scope hop or `is_on_continent` on a landing state; fix before scoring anything else.
  1. **Theatre split holds.** USA's `wa_tlm_r44_freeze_west_n` and `_east_n` are **both non-zero** by campaign end, and `_east_n` substantially exceeds `_west_n` (the Pacific calendar is far denser). This is the leg that proves the scoping is real rather than a blanket in disguise: a blanket implementation would show all stamps in one bucket.
  2. **The Pacific is not stalled by a European landing.** In the 90 days after D-Day (or whichever WEST landing fires), the USA still creates or executes naval invasions against Pacific targets. Read from the save's naval-invasion orders — `a8b29e09` and `02bd4445` both demonstrate that plan creation dates and targets are save-native and readable (see R34's history), which is the second signal this probe rests on.
  3. **The freeze bites where it is supposed to.** In the 90 days after a WEST landing, no *new* AI-planned naval-invasion order is created by a faction member against a European/African/Middle-Eastern target that is not the scripted operation's own beach. Pre-existing orders surviving is expected — `invasion_unit_request` throttles the request, it does not purge a plan.
- **Probe** (run verbatim):
  1. `python .claude/skills/wa-savegame-analysis/scripts/savegame.py tlm <TAG> <last save> --match "^wa_tlm_r44_"` for **GER, JAP, ENG, USA** — legs 0 and 1. **Absence contract:** `wa_tlm_version < 10` ⇒ the family does not exist in that build ⇒ **NOT CHECKED, never FAILED**. `wa_tlm_version >= 10` with `_freeze_n = 0` and `_freeze_last_t = 0` on a country that demonstrably landed somewhere is a real failure of leg 0.
  2. Naval-invasion orders for ENG and USA at the save before, the save of, and each of the three saves after every scripted landing month (Weserübung 1940.4, Torch 1942.11, Husky 1943.7, Anzio 1944.1, D-Day 1944.6, Dragoon 1944.8, plus the USA Pacific ops) — legs 2 and 3. Record **target state, target continent, and creation date** for each order; the continent is what makes the leg scoreable.
  3. Cross-check the arming against the landings that actually happened: `wa_ai_invasions_dbg_adds` on the same country should track `wa_tlm_r44_freeze_n`'s own-country share, since both increment inside the same `has_relation_modifier` guard. A large divergence means one of the two write sites is not on the path it is documented to be on.
- **Threshold:** **5** (behavioural). The mechanism half is discrete and would justify 3, but legs 2 and 3 are campaign outcomes over a 90-day window, and the item's real risk is the one the design measurement flagged — that a freeze of this shape stalls a theatre it was never meant to touch. One campaign where the Pacific happens to be quiet anyway is not evidence the scoping is safe.
- **Known interactions to check before calling a leg FAILED:**
  - `WA_AI_MILITARY_ALLIES_dday_prep_INVASION` puts `+1000` on states 15 and 1016 from 1944.3.1. Anzio (1944.1.21, europe) freezes WEST until ~1944.4.21, so the two overlap by ~7 weeks and the Normandy beach nets `+800`. An invasion order against 15/1016 surviving the freeze in that window is **correct behaviour**, not a leak.
  - `WA_AI_MILITARY_INV_freeze_when_home_threatened` (same type, same value, `WA_AI_MILITARY_DEFAULT_INVASION.txt`) also emits `-200` and is Additive. A country under both reads `-400`; that is a doubled floor, not a bug.
  - `WA_AI_MILITARY_COUNTRY_ENG_INVASION_legacy_ENG_post_dday_invasions_hold` still suppresses `invade` on RGR/GRE until 1944.11.1 on its own calendar. It was **deliberately not absorbed or deleted** by this fix (deleting `ai_strategy` blocks has already caused a boot CTD once — see `wa-lessons-learned`, 2026-08-09 — and it needs a launch test this change could not run). Do not attribute its suppressions to R44.
- **Streak:** 2
- **Status:** PASSED
- **History:**
  - 2026-08-14 · `be18f9c7` · PASSED (legs 0, 1 and 3; leg 2 NOT CHECKED, non-discriminating) — Leg 0 PASS - `wa_tlm_version = 11`; GER `_freeze_n = 3` (`first_t` 1940.4 Weserubung, `last_t` 1942.8), ENG and USA both `_freeze_n = 25` (`first_t` 1942.11 Torch, `last_t` 1945.4), JAP 14. Leg 1 PASS **and the split is real, not a blanket**: GER 3 west / 0 east, JAP 0 west / 14 east, USA/ENG 11 west / 14 east (both non-zero, east > west). Leg 3 PASS - in the 90 days after the Normandy landing **no new AI naval-invasion order against a European/African/ME target is created by ENG or USA**: the 1944.7/8 saves show only the pre-existing 1944.4.22 and 1944.5.14 orders, 1944.9 and 1944.10 show **zero**, and fresh European planning resumes exactly at 1944.11.14/11.15/11.18. Leg 2 not scoreable - USA created no Pacific order in the window, but it had created none in the six months before D-Day either, so the freeze cannot be identified as the cause; this is the "Pacific happens to be quiet anyway" case the threshold note warns about. **Telemetry caveat: ENG and USA carry byte-identical freeze counters** (25/11/14, same stamps), expected from faction-wide stamping but meaning leg 0's "invader **and** at least one other faction member" test is only genuinely discriminating on GER/JAP.
  - 2026-08-15 · `af003548` · PASSED (legs 0, 1; legs 2, 3 NOT CHECKED) — Leg 0 PASS: GER `_freeze_n` **1** (1940.4 Weserübung only; be18f9c7 had 3), JAP 14 (1938.10 → 1942.5), ENG/USA 23 each (1942.11 → 1945.4) byte-identical. Leg 1 PASS, marginal: USA/ENG **11 west / 12 east** — east does not "substantially exceed" west this run, but both buckets are non-zero and the split is real. Legs 2/3 need the top-level `theatres` order block, which was not extracted (see the anomalies list) — score them next time from that block. Streak 2 of 5.

### R45. Scripted ship variants get their historical names (Fix 71)

- **Fix under test:** Fix 71 (2026-08-14, commit `<FILL AT COMMIT>`). Four new data files, `common/units/names_ships/{BUL,ICE,NOR,POR}_ship_names.txt`, copied verbatim from the 1.18 vanilla install. No existing file edited.
- **Root cause, and the reason this is a whole class and not one typo:** `descriptor.mod` line 84 declares `replace_path="common/units/names_ships"`. The mod ships **12** files there; vanilla ships **~65**. For every replaced folder HOI4 loads *only* the mod's files, so the other ~53 countries' ship name groups do not exist in the running game at all — including groups that mod script actively references. A repo-wide sweep of every `name_group =` outside `common/units/names*` found **six live references to five undefined groups**: `BUL_DD_HISTORICAL` (`common/national_focus/bulgaria.txt`, the one the 2026-08-14 log caught), `ICE_CA_HISTORICAL` (`common/national_focus/iceland.txt`), `NOR_DD_HISTORICAL` ×2 (`common/decisions/NOR.txt`), `POR_DD_HISTORICAL` ×3 and `POR_CL_HISTORICAL` ×3 (`common/decisions/POR.txt`). All five exist in vanilla; all five were deleted by the `replace_path`. Only the BUL one reached the log, because the other four sit behind decisions/focuses that campaign never took — **the log is a lower bound on this defect class, never an inventory.** Restoring the four vanilla files matches the mod's own convention: the 12 curated files are vanilla files with WA edits on top, and Greece already does the exact thing Bulgaria was trying to do (`common/national_focus/greece.txt:2922` names a frigate hull from `GRE_DD_HISTORICAL`, a `ship_hull_light destroyer` group, with no complaint).
- **Pass, two legs:**
  1. **Log leg (discrete).** After a boot, `logs/error.log` carries **no** `equipment_effects.cpp` *"Invalid name group"* entry. The 2026-08-14 06:25 baseline carried exactly one (`bulgaria.txt:3551`, `BUL_DD_HISTORICAL`); its disappearance is the fix fingerprint.
  2. **Naming leg (behavioural, cheap).** In any campaign where BUL completes the Drazki refit focus, the four destroyers it spawns carry names from the historical list (`Drazki`, `Smeli`, `Khrabri`, …) rather than the engine fallback. Same check applies to NOR's refitted destroyers and POR's *Vouga*/*Atlântico* classes if those campaigns reach them.
- **Probe:** (a) Log leg — grep `logs/error.log` for `Invalid name group` after the boot; **absence contract:** a log that carries no `equipment_effects.cpp` lines at all is a boot-only log and scores leg 1 only if the mod loaded (check for the usual `dlc.cpp` header lines). (b) Naming leg — read ship names out of the save's `units`/`navy` section for BUL/NOR/POR, or observe in-game. (c) Regression guard — re-run the name_group sweep (every `name_group =` in `common/` + `events/`, minus `common/units/names*`, cross-checked against the groups the mod actually defines) after any change to `common/units/names_ships`; the only remaining unresolved hit should be `AUS_BB_HISTORICAL` in `common/national_focus/austria.txt:15273`, which sits inside a **fully commented-out** `create_equipment_variant` block and does not exist in vanilla either — dead code, not a defect.
- **Threshold:** **3** (narrow — the pass criterion is a discrete log fact plus a name string).
- **Known interaction:** these groups carry `for_countries = { BUL }` etc., and `common/decisions/POR.txt` creates the *Vouga* and *Atlântico* variants **in ITA scope** while asking for the POR groups (the `NOR.txt` author's own comment asserts the effect "will grab the appropriate country-specific name list anyways"). That assertion is **unverified** and was deliberately not changed by this fix. If leg 2 reads fallback names for the POR classes specifically while BUL and NOR read correctly, the cause is `for_countries`, not a missing group — report it as a separate finding.
- **Streak:** 2
- **Status:** PASSED
- **History:**
  - 2026-08-14 · `be18f9c7` · PASSED — Log leg: `logs/error.log` (2026-08-14 20:31, mod loaded) carries **zero** `Invalid name group` entries against the pre-fix baseline's one; `common/units/names_ships/` now ships 16 files including BUL/ICE/NOR/POR. Naming leg: **BUL completed `BUL_begin_the_refit_program`** and its `ship_names_tracker` carries historical Drazki-class names (`Strogi`, `Shumni`, plus `Podvodnitsa 1/2`); NOR reads `HNoMS Harald Haarfagre / Tordenskjold / Eidsvold / Norge`; all five previously-undefined groups (`BUL_DD_`, `ICE_CA_`, `NOR_DD_`, `POR_DD_`, `POR_CL_HISTORICAL`) appear in their countries' `available_groups`. Regression guard: a full `name_group =` sweep over `common/` + `events/` returns **no undefined references**. Caveat: POR's 204 ships carry only `ship_name={ type= name_order= }` with no `post_mortem` override strings, so the `for_countries` / ITA-scope interaction in `common/decisions/POR.txt` remains unverified - not evidence against the fix.
  - 2026-08-15 · `af003548` · PASSED — zero "Invalid name group" in a mod-loaded local `error.log` carrying 8 `equipment_effects.cpp` entries (local install log; the cloud box writes none); BUL/NOR/POR carry the restored groups, BUL/NOR ships carry historical names, POR again has no `post_mortem` overrides to read (the `for_countries` question stays open). Streak 2 of 3.

### R46. The Allies can build logistics on an ally's soil — Tunisia after Torch, Normandy after D-Day (Fix 74)

- **Opened 2026-08-14** from campaign `f9321934` (bhutan), user report: at 1944.3 Egypt runs full rail toward Tobruk while the Tunisia→Tripoli axis has none. Same root cause as the Free-French Normandy stall already logged in `wa-lessons-learned` from `31eaf7e6` — this item covers both theatres.
- **Fix under test:** **Fix 74** (commit `<FILL AT COMMIT>`). Three new triggers in `common/scripted_triggers/WA_AI_CONSTRUCTION_triggers.txt` (`WA_AI_PC_country_cannot_build_own_logistics`, `WA_AI_PC_can_build_logistics_here`, `WA_AI_PC_is_logistics_build_partner`, plus the extracted `WA_AI_PC_railway_land_frontline_candidate`); the land-war body extracted to `WA_AI_PC_railway_land_consider_frontline` and driven from three candidate populations; the three overseas Part-B scans and both port searches wrapped in the partner walk; `WA_AI_PC_process_port_upgrades` and `WA_AI_PC_create_frontier_port` regated. Doc: `documentation/WA_AI_RAILWAY_SYSTEM.md` § "Coalition logistics".
- **Root cause:** the PC *executor* has always accepted allied ground (`WA_AI_PC_start_project` passes on `CONTROLLER = { is_in_faction_with = ROOT }`; rail/hubs/ports are `allied_build = yes`; pathfinder type 2 walks allied provinces) — only the *selectors* were ROOT-or-subject gated. `_project_build_for_ally = 1` was set in `railway_core.txt` and read nowhere: designed, never implemented. So a corridor that ended up on an ally's soil had **no builder at all**, and the one country that could build there was capitulated and therefore switched off.
- **A second, independent defect was found and fixed in the same pass:** the Fix 27 subject loop sat in the enemy's scope (no `ROOT = {}` wrapper inside `for_each_scope_loop = { array = _relevant_enemies_ }`), so `every_subject_country` walked the **enemy's** puppets and its `is_subject_of = ROOT` test could never pass. **Fix 27 has been dead since it was written.** Score leg 3 separately — it is a different claim from the ally leg and could pass or fail on its own.
- **Pass, three legs:**
  1. **Coverage leg (the fix itself).** In a campaign where an Allied power (ENG or USA) is at war with a land-bordering enemy across ally-controlled ground, that power shows `wa_tlm_r74_ally_rail_ally_n > 0`. The `f9321934` baseline is **zero railway projects of any kind on ENG and USA for the entire campaign**, so any `_routes_n > 0` is already movement; `_ally_n > 0` is the fix.
  2. **Behavioural leg (the point).** Rail level in the Maghreb rises above its 1–2 baseline while the Tunisia→Tripoli front is live — specifically state 458 Tunisia, and/or a naval-base upgrade at Bizerte (province 11969, starts at 5). Equivalent Normandy form: rail or port level rises in an Allied-held, FRA-controlled French state after the landing.
  3. **Fix 27 revival leg.** A country with subjects holding a frontline (ENG/UKE, ITA/ITL, ENG/RAJ) shows railway projects targeting **subject-controlled** states. Absent in every campaign to date because the loop never ran.
- **Probe:** `savegame.py tlm ENG --match r74` and the same for USA/ITA across ≥4 monthly saves spanning the North African and French fronts (`_ally_n <= _routes_n` by construction; `_first_t`/`_last_t` decode via `global.WA_TLM_clock`). Pair with `savegame.py var ENG "^wa_ai_pc_"` to read the queue's `building_type` 13/14 entries and their `target_state`, and check whether any target state is one ROOT does not control. Rail levels are **not** in a state's `buildings={}` block — they are province-scoped under the top-level `provinces={}` map, and the save also carries a top-level `rail_way={}` block of per-province edge levels; state→province mapping comes from `history/states/<ID>-*.txt`. **Absence contract:** `wa_tlm_version < 11` = pre-Fix-74 build, probe **void**, never FAILED.
- **Threshold:** 3 (narrow — every leg is a discrete "did the counter move / did the level rise" fact off one sweep).
- **Regression watch, in priority order:**
  - **Duplicate spend.** Two faction members may now queue the same segment. `WA_AI_PC_start_railway_project` dedups against `global.WA_AI_PC_railway_connection_level_*` and the builder's own queue, but **not across countries**. The ally leg is gated on the controller being unable to build for itself, which bounds this; if a campaign shows two majors with railway projects on the same `target_province`, the gate is too loose.
  - **Fix 27 going live is a behaviour change, not a no-op.** Subject frontlines now compete for the per-enemy route budget (`@WA_AI_PC_railway_MAX_ROUTES_PER_ENEMY = 4`). Population order in the dispatcher is ROOT → subjects → allies precisely so ROOT's own soil cannot be crowded out; verify GER/SOV/JAP home-front rail did not shrink.
  - **`every_country` cost.** The ally leg and the three overseas scans each walk `every_country` with a cheap trigger, per relevant enemy, per railway execution (8 weeks at war). Watch for late-game tick slowdown in a save with many faction members.
  - ~~**Eligibility literals are now triplicated.**~~ **Retired by Fix 75 in the same session:** `WA_AI_PC_country_cannot_build_own_logistics` is now the strict `NOT` of `WA_AI_PC_country_can_build_own_logistics`, which is also what the on_action calls, so the ally leg and the eligibility gate cannot disagree. `railway_core.txt` remains authoritative for the constants the effect body itself reads.
- **The third cause found in the same diagnosis is now R47, not this item.** FRA's fossil queue (capitulated, PC block never ran, 14 projects frozen 12 months, 21 civ factories idle) is fixed separately by **Fix 75** — score it there. Fix 74 routes *around* that cause by letting healthy allies build instead, and the two fixes are deliberately independent: **Fix 74's ally leg must keep firing for FRA even after Fix 75**, because FRA still fails the railway capability bar. If a campaign shows `_ally_n = 0` on ENG/USA because FRA now builds its own Tunisian rail, that is not a failure of either item — but it does mean the ally trigger and the eligibility trigger have diverged, and R47's leg 3 is where that shows up.
- **Streak:** 2
- **Status:** PASSED
- **History:**
  - 2026-08-14 · `be18f9c7` · PASSED — **All three legs, on the first test of Fix 74.** Leg 1 PASS - `wa_tlm_r74_ally_rail_ally_n` = **ENG 10** (routes_n 47, first_t 1940.8, last_t 1944.3), **ITA 6** (routes_n 16), **USA 1**; GER/SOV/JAP 0 as expected. Leg 2 **PASS, and it is the exact theatre the item was opened for** - ENG's PC queue at 1944.6 holds **4 type-13 railway projects on state 458 (Tunisia)** and 3 on state 665, both GER-owned / FRA-controlled, i.e. an ally's soil; Tunisia rail levels **9 (1943.6) -> 22 (1944.6) -> 25 (1945.6)** against a 9-10 baseline held from 1936.2, Algiers 15 -> 22. Leg 3 **PASS - Fix 27 is alive**: ENG queues rail on **UKT-controlled** states 269 and 1100 and on ETH 271/909/910; ITA on **RIT-controlled state 160** and on CRO 109 / RSE 107 / BUL 212 / BUL-held YUG 851. Subject/ally-controlled targets appear for the first time in any campaign. Regression watch clean (GER home rail did not shrink - 80 type-13 slots at 1944.6, its largest ever; no ENG/ITA/FRA target-state overlap). **Caveat for the next scoring: ENG's queue drains to a single project by 1946.7 with zero type-13 from 1945.6 onward.**
  - 2026-08-15 · `af003548` · PASSED (all three legs, 2nd consecutive) — Leg 1: `wa_tlm_r74_ally_rail_ally_n` **ENG 3** (`_routes_n` 91, 1940.8 → 1943.9), USA 1, ITA 6, GER 2. Leg 2: state 458 Tunisia (GER-owned / FRA-controlled) rail **9 → 13 → 17**; ENG's 1942.12 queue holds 10 funded type-13 on 663/451/450 (ITL-owned / ENG-controlled), Cyrenaica rail 0 → 11; ENG naval base on 459 Algiers (FRA/FRA) for ~10 months. Leg 3: ENG type-13 on 1100 N. Kenya and 549 S. Sudan (UKT/UKT) — Fix 27 alive. Normandy leg unexercised (15/1016 GER-controlled until 1946.4). Streak 2 of 3.

### R47. A capitulated-but-alive country runs priority construction on the soil it still controls (Fix 75)

- **Opened 2026-08-14**, out of the R46 diagnosis on campaign `f9321934`. R46 owns the evidence; this item verifies the second fix cut from it. Same family as R42/Fix 68 — `has_capitulated` misused as a capability test — and the third site of that same word.
- **Fix under test:** **Fix 75** (commit `<FILL AT COMMIT>`). `common/on_actions/WA_AI_misc_on_actions.txt`: the weekly PC block gate goes from `is_ai = yes` + `has_capitulated = no` to `is_ai = yes` + `exists = yes`, with the capitulation test pushed onto the three calls that still need it (training modifier, offensive posture, lend-lease relief) so statement order is unchanged. The inline railway eligibility filter becomes the scripted trigger `WA_AI_PC_country_can_build_own_logistics` (terms and thresholds verbatim), and the now-unused `@WA_AI_PC_railway_*` redeclarations are deleted from the on_action. `common/scripted_effects/WA_AI_CONSTRUCTION_PRIORITY_railway_core.txt`: the third `has_capitulated = no` removed from inside `WA_AI_PC_railway`.
- **Root cause:** `WA_AI_priority_construction_strategies` fires from `WA_AI_background.1` gated only on `is_ai = yes`, while factory assignment and progress sat behind the capitulation gate. A capitulated country therefore kept **queueing** projects it could never work — the queue was write-only and froze at whatever it held the week the capital fell. FRA: 14 projects byte-identical across 12 monthly saves, `assigned_factories^4 = 14` and `progress^4 = 651.95101` stuck mid-value, 10 railways aimed at GER-held metropolitan France, 49 controlled states and 21 idle civ factories.
- **Pass, three legs:**
  1. **Thaw leg (the fix itself).** For any tag with `capitulated=yes` and `num_of_controlled_states > 0`, the `wa_ai_pc_*` queue is **not** byte-identical between two consecutive monthly saves — `assigned_factories`, `progress`, `stall_weeks` or `queue^num` must move. On an `f9321934`-shaped run that is specifically FRA. Identical across two samples while the country holds states = FAIL.
  2. **Self-cleaning leg.** The fossils actually leave: FRA's railway projects targeting GER-controlled states (14, 15, 785, 855, 1016) are cancelled rather than merely frozen, and at least one of FRA's cancel counters — `wa_tlm_pc_sweep_n`, `wa_tlm_pc_stale_n` (both v14+) or `wa_tlm_pc_aging_reval_cancels` — is `> 0`.
